# Testing and QA

## Chart of Accounts: the page-closing note is removed - 2026-09-21

- User request: remove the text "Income and expenses are not cash flow. Balances exclude the old sample amounts and unposted documents." from the Chart of Accounts table, grid and list page.
- The paragraph was the last child of `div.am-list` in `src/AccountWorkspace.jsx`, sitting after the account table and the empty state: `p.am-note` with the 17px `IconInfoCircle` and that sentence. It is deleted rather than hidden, and the closing `div` of `am-list` is untouched, so the page now ends with the grid itself.
- Nothing else moved. `IconInfoCircle` stays imported because the file uses it elsewhere, and the shared `.am-note` rules stay because six other surfaces still render them - only this one paragraph is gone. The table, its columns, the filters, the counts, the sticky heading and the detail drawer are unchanged.
- Verified live at 1440x800: the rendered page contains neither `not cash flow` nor `old sample amounts`, `.am-coa-page .am-note` matches zero elements, and the grid still renders its 22 rows.
- Validation actually executed: the full sweep is 727 tests / 716 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passes; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passes.

## Chart of Accounts: the expenses type mark is the shopping bag - 2026-09-21

- User request: change the Expenses icon in the Chart of Accounts table, grid and list.
- `TYPE_MARKS` in `src/AccountWorkspace.jsx` now maps `Expenses` to `IconShoppingBag` where it held `IconReceipt2`, and the Tabler import was swapped in the same edit so no dead glyph stays imported. Nothing else about the mark moved: still one `span.am-type-badge` holding a 20px `span.am-type-mark` circle with the glyph reversed out at 13px, still the amber pill `#fdf0e3` / `#96540f` on the `#e08e2b` circle.
- The receipt glyph was the thing being reported. It is the payment-document shape this app already uses for payments and receipts, so an Expenses row read as a voucher rather than as a cost. The bag is the same icon family at the same 13px, so the type column stays one visual system.
- Amber stays on purpose. The rule recorded in `src/account-workspace.css` still holds for this grid, because its only status chips are the green `Active` and grey `Inactive` pills: there is no amber status chip in this table for the expenses tint to be confused with, which is the exact collision that moved assets off the status green.
- Verified live at 1440x800: the Expenses rows render `tabler-icon-shopping-bag` at 13px inside the amber mark circle, and the other four types are unchanged - assets `tabler-icon-home`, liabilities `tabler-icon-chart-pie`, equity `tabler-icon-chart-histogram`, income `tabler-icon-coins`.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/account-sticky-grid-ui.test.mjs` gained a pin on the expenses glyph and passes 11/11; the full sweep is 727 tests / 716 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passes; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passes.

## Chart of Accounts: the type tones leave the status palette - 2026-09-21

- User report: the Assets mark reads the same as the Status column's green, so it needs a colour that cannot be confused with it.
- Confirmed by measurement rather than by eye. The status pill is `background:#e8f6ee;color:#287a51` with a `#28a26b` dot, and the assets tone was `#e6f6f0` / `#1c6b52` with a `#2fa27f` circle - the same emerald family, which is why a type pill and the Active pill beside it looked like one thing. Income was the same defect one step removed: `#e9f7e3` / `#2f7d32` / `#5cb85c` is still a green.
- Both moved out of the green family, and the rule behind it is now recorded in the stylesheet: **green, amber, red and grey are reserved for status**, so no type tone may sit in a status hue family. Assets take indigo - `#eef0fd` / `#4038a8` with a `#6d63d6` circle - and income takes cyan - `#e2f5f9` / `#0b6477` with a `#1b9fc0` circle. The other three were already clear of the status set: liabilities rose `#fbe9f4` / `#8e2f6b` (`#d356a4`), equity blue `#e7f0fd` / `#1d4ed8` (`#3b82f6`), expenses amber-brown `#fdf0e3` / `#96540f` (`#e08e2b`).
- Verified live at 1440x800 with the status pills on screen: the assets badge computes `rgb(238,240,253)` / `rgb(64,56,168)` with a `rgb(109,99,214)` circle, and the Active pill beside it `rgb(232,246,238)` / `rgb(40,122,81)` - different hue, different saturation and a coloured glyph where the status pill has a dot. Income computes `rgb(226,245,249)` / `rgb(11,100,119)` with a `rgb(27,159,192)` circle. No type tone shares a hue family with a status tone any more.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/account-sticky-grid-ui.test.mjs` was repinned for the two new tones and gained an assertion that no type tone sits in a status hue, and it passes 11/11; the full sweep is 727 tests / 716 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.


## Chart of Accounts: one mark per account type in the type column - 2026-09-21

- User request: differentiate the account types simply and visually in the Chart of Accounts grid, using the attached reference (a house for Assets, a pie for Liabilities, a chart for Equity, coins for Revenue, a coin-and-arrow for Expenses, each on its own colour) as the idea rather than the layout.
- `TYPE_MARKS` is declared once in `src/AccountWorkspace.jsx` - Assets `IconHome`, Liabilities `IconChartPie`, Equity `IconChartHistogram`, Income `IconCoins`, Expenses `IconShoppingBag`, each with a tone name - and the Account Type cell renders one `span.am-type-badge` holding a 20px `span.am-type-mark` circle with the glyph beside the type label. The row map reads the mark before the row, so there is no lookup inside the JSX.
- The reference is a legend of full-width gradient bars; at table scale that would fight the grid, so the marks are pills instead: a soft tint fill, the label in a darker tone of the same hue and the glyph reversed out of the solid circle. Five tones, each in the pastel register the rest of the app uses for status fills - assets `#e6f6f0` / `#1c6b52` on `#2fa27f`, liabilities `#fbe9f4` / `#8e2f6b` on `#d356a4`, equity `#e7f0fd` / `#1d4ed8` on `#3b82f6`, income `#e9f7e3` / `#2f7d32` on `#5cb85c`, expenses `#fdf0e3` / `#96540f` on `#e08e2b` - with a neutral `#f1f4f8` / `#475467` fallback for a type the map does not know. The label keeps the 12px floor and the pill is `white-space:nowrap`, so nothing wraps or clips.
- The type help stays: the cell keeps its `title` and `aria-description` from `TYPE_HELP`, so the explanation still reaches a reader who hovers or tabs to it, and the badge adds the visual cue without replacing the word.
- Verified live at 1440x800: all five tones compute exactly as declared - Assets `rgb(230,246,240)` / `rgb(28,107,82)` mark `rgb(47,162,127)`, Liabilities `rgb(251,233,244)` / `rgb(142,47,107)` mark `rgb(211,86,164)`, Equity `rgb(231,240,253)` / `rgb(29,78,216)` mark `rgb(59,130,246)`, Income `rgb(233,247,227)` / `rgb(47,125,50)` mark `rgb(92,184,92)`, Expenses `rgb(253,240,227)` / `rgb(150,84,15)` mark `rgb(224,142,43)` - each with a 20x20 mark carrying an `svg`, a 12px label, the Account Type column 242px wide, 22 rows and no horizontal overflow.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/account-sticky-grid-ui.test.mjs` gained a test pinning the five marks and tones, the cell markup, the shared rule and the five tone rules, and it passes 11/11; the full sweep is 727 tests / 716 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.

## Chart of Accounts: the accordion is gone and the grid is one flat table - 2026-09-21

- User request: remove the accordion and the grouped sections from the Chart of Accounts page and give it a table of Account name, Code, Account Type, Account Group, Status, an Edit button and a 3-dot more action, on the table style the other modules use.
- The grid is now one flat table. `AccountGroup` is deleted along with `openGroups`, `openList` and `toggleGroup`; the body is `const flatRows=typeGroups.flatMap(group=>group.rows.map(row=>({...row,type:group.name})))`, so the chart order the sections implied is kept and the type each account belongs to travels with the row as the new **Account Type** column between the code and the group. Twenty-one CSS rules went with it (`.am-group-row`, `.am-group-toggle`, their sticky offsets, fills, hover and focus rules), and `.am-row-more` was replaced by `.am-row-actions-cell`; no `am-group` reference is left in the stylesheet.
- The row action is the standard register pair: a visible `button.am-row-edit` (36px, `1px solid #d0d5dd`, 7px radius, `#98a2b3` hover, `#84adff` focus ring) beside the existing `details.am-more` menu, inside one `.am-row-actions` cluster that right-aligns. The menu no longer duplicates the button: the `Edit Account` entry is gone and `Duplicate Account`, `View Transactions`, the activate/deactivate toggle and the guarded `Delete Account` stay.
- The account name is now the register link every other table opens a record with rather than a boxed button: `.am-tree-name>button:last-child` is `border:0;background:transparent;padding:0;min-height:0` in `#1849a9` 13px/600 with a `#245fd9` underline on hover, which is the same treatment `.soOrderNumber` and the invoice register links use. The chevron that expands sub-accounts keeps its own chip beside it, because that is hierarchy rather than the accordion.
- The type help that used to attach to the accordion headers moved with it: the `useEffect` that queried `.am-group-toggle` is deleted, and the `Account Type` cell carries the same text as `title` and `aria-description` directly, so nothing queries the DOM by a class that no longer exists.
- A real regression was introduced and repaired during the pass. The first cleanup dropped every *line* containing `am-group`, and one of those lines was a 12,302-character one-liner holding the whole `.am` base token block, the toolbar, the drawer and the status dot. The status pill suite caught it ("the Chart of Accounts dot still declares background:#28a26b"); the block was restored from `HEAD` with only its two group rules removed, and the pill, sticky-grid and header suites went green again. The lesson: never drop a line by substring match in a minified sheet - rewrite the rule inside the line.
- Verification: live at 1440x800 the grid reports **22 rows and 0 group rows**, headers `Account Name 294 · Account Code 163 · Account Type 246 · Account Group 172 · Status 86 · Actions 132`, no horizontal overflow, row height 58px, the name link computing `rgb(24,73,169)` with `0px` border and a transparent fill, the Edit button 66x36 and the kebab 32x32. A first row reads `Cash | 1000 | Assets | Cash and Bank | Active | [Edit] [⋮]`.
- Tests repinned rather than worked around: `tests/account-header.test.mjs` (the accordion test becomes the flat-table test), `tests/account-sticky-grid-ui.test.mjs` (the group-row sticky rule, fill, hover, focus list, header list and the accordion styling block all become flat-table and row-action pins), `tests/account-ui.test.mjs` (the one-open-group assertion in both views becomes "no section is open" and the collapsed-group proof becomes "every account renders") and `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` (the chip and accent comparisons lose their Chart of Accounts counterpart, because the accordion they compared against is gone).
- Validation actually executed: the full sweep is 726 tests / 715 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed. The viewport override was reset afterwards.


## Sticky column headings: the band above them no longer shows rows - 2026-09-21

- User report: while a table is scrolled the column heading stays pinned, but table rows keep scrolling underneath and appear in the gap between the page header and the pinned column heading, so the heading does not read as a solid boundary.
- Reproduced and measured. On the invoices register, scrolled with the page header away, the pinned heading sat at y=127 while the scroll port began at y=105: a **22px band** - exactly `.app>main` own `padding-top` - and hit-testing that band returned a `tbody` cell as the topmost element at ten of the sampled points, which is the broken state in the screenshot. The Chart of Accounts showed the same 22px band, and a hit-test at 107..125 returned a `td`.
- Cause: a sticky offset is measured from the scroll port **content** box, so a heading pinned at `top:0` lands one scroll-port padding below the visible top edge. The page heading row pins at the padding box top, so with the row visible the two still met, but the moment the row slid away the heading did not move up with it and the padding became a window onto the scrolling rows.
- Two fixes, both shared. First, the offset gives the padding back: `[data-sticky-head] thead th{top:calc(var(--register-head-h,0px) - var(--register-head-pad,22px))}`, and `src/register-head.js` publishes `--register-head-pad` from the computed `padding-top` of the port the page actually scrolls in - the shell main, or the Period Lock panel which owns its own. That puts the heading flush at the visible top when the page header is away and exactly under it while the heading is there. Verified on Purchase Orders, which has no page heading row: the band is 0 and the heading sits at the scroll port top edge (105 = 105).
- Second, masking, because the Chart of Accounts keeps its own heading rules at higher specificity and so never takes the corrected offset: `body:has(.registerHead.isHidden) [data-sticky-head] thead th::before,body:has(.am-coa-page.am-coa-bar-hidden) [data-sticky-head] thead th::before{content:"";position:absolute;left:0;right:0;bottom:100%;height:40px;background:inherit;pointer-events:none}`. The strip repeats the heading own fill above itself, so no row can show in the band, and it is applied **only while a page heading is actually away**, so it can never paint over a toolbar on a page that has no such row. Verified on Chart of Accounts in its hidden state: the band is still 22px wide, but zero rows paint in it.
- The stacking was already correct and is unchanged: rows are static, `thead th` is `z-index:6`, the page heading row is `z-index:9`, and the row action menus are `80`. Nothing was fixed by raising a `z-index`.
- Verified at rest on Invoices, Credit Notes, Chart of Accounts and Purchase Orders at 1440x340: `--register-head-pad` reads 22px and no sampled point between the scroll port top and the pinned heading returns a table cell on every page.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/sticky-table-head.test.mjs` was repinned for the new offset and gained assertions for the masking rule and the published padding, and passes 4/4; the full sweep is 726 tests / 715 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Honest limit on this pass: the in-app browser stopped delivering wheel events part way through, so the scrolled states were captured on some pages and not others - the defect was measured before the fix on the invoices register and the Chart of Accounts, and each of the two fixes was measured live in the state it addresses. The scroll-from-top-to-bottom sweep on every page is worth repeating by hand.


## Credit Notes: the status and the refunded amount read in full - 2026-09-21

- User report, with a screenshot: the Status column cuts the refunded amount off ("Refunded ₹2,950....") and the status must show completely rather than break.
- Measured cause: the Status column was 11% of a 1046px table, so 115px with 95px of content, while the cell holds a status pill and, under it, `Refunded ₹2,950.00` - about 120px of text. The table carries `white-space:nowrap` from `.invoiceWorkspace table`, so the small line was clipped with an ellipsis rather than wrapped, and the pill - which is `white-space:nowrap` itself - had only 95px to sit in.
- Two changes, both in the `.creditNotes` blocks of `src/invoice-workspace.css`. The widths are now 11/14/10/9/10/10/10/14/12 and still total exactly 100%: the Status column takes the three points it needs, paying one from the merged first column and two from Reason, which truncates a long phrase either way. Both blocks were rewritten to the same nine values, because the sheet carries a superseded set above the live one. And the status cell own small now wraps instead of being cut: `.creditNotes .ivInvoiceTable td:nth-child(8) small{white-space:normal;overflow-wrap:anywhere}`, so an unusual amount breaks rather than disappearing behind an ellipsis.
- Verified live at 1550x800 with the register open: the nine headers measure 127/162/116/104/116/116/116/162/139, so the Status column is 162px with about 142px of content against the 120px the refunded line needs, the Status header stays on one line, and there is no horizontal overflow. The status pill cannot wrap at any width, because `.statusPill` is `white-space:nowrap`; the column is what had to grow.
- The strings themselves are proved by a rendered check rather than by eye, because the in-app browser profile holds no credit notes: `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` now builds the page with esbuild and renders it with `renderToString` over a seeded store, asserting that a fully refunded note reads `Refunded` with its figure (`Refunded ₹2,950.00`, in full) and that a partly applied note names `Partially Applied` in full, with the number and date sharing the merged first cell. That test also pins the nine-column header list, the merged cell, the removed Date column, the width total and the alignment.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` is 12/12; the full sweep is 726 tests / 715 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Note for the record: the register is empty in the in-app browser profile (its store holds no credit notes), so the six notes in the user screenshot are their own data; the layout was therefore verified by measuring the columns and the cell content box, and the values by the rendered test.


## Credit Notes: the note and its date are one column - 2026-09-21

- User request: merge the Credit note and Date columns in the credit note register into one column headed Credit Note Id & Date.
- The register is now nine columns: `Credit Note Id & Date`, Customer, Original invoice, Reason, Amount, Applied, Remaining credit, Status and Actions. The cell stacks the note number over its date - `<button className="ivLink">{c.number}</button><small>{c.date}</small>` - which is the same shape every other register uses to state a document and its date, so the reader loses nothing by the merge and the table gains a column of width.
- The widths were renumbered to 12/14/10/11/10/10/10/11/12 and total exactly 100%. Both `.creditNotes` width blocks in `src/invoice-workspace.css` were rewritten, because the sheet carries a superseded ten-column set above the live one and leaving it would have misled the next reader.
- The merge shifted every column after the first, which surfaced an alignment defect worth naming: `src/invoice-workspace.css` right-aligns `td:nth-child(4)` and `td:nth-child(7)` for the *invoice* register, and the credit note table wears the same `ivInvoiceTable` class, so those rules reach it. Before the merge they landed on Original invoice (wrong) and Applied (right by luck), and the other two money columns were left-aligned - an inconsistency on the page. After the merge they would have landed on Reason and Remaining credit. The credit-notes block now states its own alignment: Amount, Applied and Remaining credit right, the merged column and Original invoice and Reason left. Verified live.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` gained a test pinning the nine-column header list, the merged cell, the removed Date column and the effective width set totalling 100%, and it passes 11/11; the full sweep is 725 tests / 714 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Verified live at 1440x800: nine headers reading Credit Note Id & Date (126px, left), Customer (146), Original invoice (105), Reason (115), Amount (105, right), Applied (105, right), Remaining credit (105, right), Status (115), Actions (126, right), with no horizontal overflow. The demo register holds no credit notes, so the row geometry was checked through the header and through the markup pin rather than with data.


## Day, week, month and year ranges on the five register filters - 2026-09-21

- User request: add date filtering to the filter button on Sales Orders, Invoices, Credit Notes, Payments Received and Journal Entries, so each can be read day wise, week wise, month wise and year wise.
- One shared module holds the ranges: `src/date-range-filter.js` exports `DATE_RANGE_PRESETS` (`All dates`, `Today`, `This week`, `This month`, `This year`), `resolveDateRange(key, today)` and `matchDateRange(from, to, today)`. Each range is the **whole calendar period**, not the part of it that has already happened, so a filter labelled This month includes a document dated later in the same month and This year includes December. Weeks run Monday to Sunday. `resolveDateRange` returns the same `YYYY-MM-DD` strings the app already stores dates in, and a fixed `today` can be passed in, which is what makes it testable.
- **The two dates stay the only filter state.** Choosing a range simply writes the page existing `from` and `to` fields, so every page keeps its own filtering, its own count badge and its own Clear filters row with no change to any of them - Invoices writes `invFilters.from/to`, Credit Notes `from/to`, Journal Entries `fromDate/toDate`, Sales Orders `from/to` and Payment Receipts `receiptFilters.from/to`. Each panel gained one `<label>Date range<select aria-label="Filter by date range">` immediately before its existing date fields.
- The control cannot lie about the range it is showing: its value comes from `matchDateRange(from, to)` rather than from state, so a range is named only while the two dates are exactly that period, and the select falls back to a `Custom range` entry the moment either date is edited by hand. Verified live - setting This month wrote `2026-09-01`..`2026-09-30` and the control read `month`; setting Today then read `day`.
- Payment Receipts had **no** date filtering at all, so it gained real ones rather than only a control: `from:'',to:''` joined `RECEIPT_FILTER_DEFAULTS`, the register filters on `(!receiptFilters.from||x.date>=receiptFilters.from)&&(!receiptFilters.to||x.date<=receiptFilters.to)`, and the panel gained explicit From and To date inputs beside the range, matching the other four. Its count badge needed one correction: `Object.values(receiptFilters).filter(value=>!value.startsWith('All '))` counted the two new empty strings as active, so the filter now requires a truthy value first.
- Filtering was driven end to end on two pages. Invoices: 4 rows before, This month kept all 4 (they are all September) with the badge at 2 - two fields differ from their defaults - Today left 0 rows, and Clear filters restored 4 rows and the All dates control. Payment Receipts: 1 row before, This month wrote `2026-09-01`..`2026-09-30` and kept its 2026-09-04 receipt, and Today left 0 rows. All five panels were inspected live and each carries the control with all five options in order.
- Validation actually executed: a new `tests/date-range-filter.test.mjs` resolves each range against a fixed date (including that the week is seven days starting Monday and contains the date), checks that `matchDateRange` names a period only when the dates are exactly it, checks that all five pages import the shared ranges and write the dates they already filter on, and checks the receipts register real date filtering and its corrected badge; two pins were updated, one in `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` whose stylesheet-order assertion now allows the shared import between the two sheets, and one in `tests/receipt-ui.test.mjs` for the badge expression. The full sweep is 724 tests / 713 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed. The viewport override was reset afterwards.
- Two things were left as they are, and both are visible in the live measurements: the count badge on the invoice and receipt registers reads 2 for one range choice, because a range changes two date fields and the badge counts fields that differ from their defaults; and the receipts table has no filter-aware empty state, so a range that excludes every row shows an empty table rather than the "no receipts match" sentence the invoices register carries.


## The record count opens every register heading - 2026-09-21

- User request: show the record count in the page heading on Customers, Sales Orders, Invoices, Credit Notes and Payments Received, in the parentheses the other registers already use.
- Five headings gained one span each: `Sales invoices`, `Credit Notes`, `Payment Receipts` on the `ivHeading` shell and `Customers`, `Sales orders` on the `itemsHeading` shell now read `<h2>Name <span className="registerHeadCount">({n})</span></h2>`. The counts are the register totals - `db.invoices.length`, `notes.length`, `rows.length` for the receipts register, `rows.length` for the customers register and `orders.length` for the orders register - so the heading states how big the register is and does not move while the reader filters. That matches Journal Entries, which passes its full `list` to the heading and its `filtered` rows to the table.
- One shared rule styles it, next to the item-count rule that already existed: `.registerHead .registerHeadText .registerHeadCount{font-size:24px;font-weight:700;line-height:1.25;letter-spacing:-.4px;color:#172033}`. It is the same 24px/700 `#172033` the Journal Entries, Items, Budgets and Period Lock counts already read at, so the new span simply joins that convention rather than introducing a style. Verified live: all five render at 24px/700/`rgb(23, 32, 51)` - Sales invoices (4), Credit Notes (0), Payment Receipts (1), Sales orders (2), Customers (2).
- The counts are plain numbers rather than the `05`/`02` the request illustrated, because every existing count in the app is unpadded - Journal Entries `(1)`, Items `(3)`, Budgets, Period Lock - and padding one family would have made it disagree with the others.
- Three test pins carried the old heading text and were repinned with the span: the merged-row tests in `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` and `tests/receipt-ui.test.mjs`, and the merged-row test in `tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs`. The two existing tests that assert the *old* toolbar counts are gone still pass, because they pin those exact removed expressions (`visible.length===1?'order':'orders'` and `{visible.length} customers`) and the new counts are different variables.
- Validation actually executed: a new `tests/register-heading-count.test.mjs` asserts all five headings carry the count, that none of them counts the filtered rows, and that the span is styled once at the title tokens and not at the retired 16px helper size; the full sweep is 720 tests / 709 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed. The viewport override was reset afterwards.


## Global sticky column headings and the shared page-header hide - 2026-09-21

- User request: one consistent scroll behaviour for every table and data grid in the app, implemented once as a shared pattern rather than page by page - the page context and its actions slide away on the way down so the table gains the viewport, the column headings stay pinned while the reader scrolls the data, and everything returns on the way up, with a deliberate threshold, a smooth transition, no jumping, no re-rendering and no per-page logic.
- What already existed: `src/register-head.css` and `src/register-head.js` were installed once from `src/main.jsx`, and the merged heading row (`registerHead`) already pinned to the shell scroll port, hid while scrolling down and returned on the first upward scroll, publishing its measured height as `--register-head-h`. Eleven registers were on it. What did not exist was the second half of the request: **no register outside the Chart of Accounts pinned its column headings**, so the header row scrolled away with the data.
- The page header hide is unchanged in shape and tightened in threshold. `HIDE_AFTER=48` is now a named constant, and the row hides on `y>HIDE_AFTER && delta>0 && y>h.offsetHeight` - past the threshold *and* past its own height, because hiding a row that is still partly on screen at its natural position is what reads as a jump. The slide is the existing `transition:transform .22s ease`, inside the 200-300ms the brief asks for.
- The column headings are marked and offset by the same module. It finds the page grid - the first table in the scroll port with a `thead` that is not inside `form,dialog,[role=dialog],details,.ivOverlay,.ivDetailSheet,.ivDetailBody,.dpPaper,.jp-page,.jp-sheet,.ivCreateForm,.itemCreatePage,.soCreatePage,.budgetWizard,.am-create-drawer` - sets `data-sticky-head` on it, and republishes `--register-head-h` as the row height while the row is there and as `0px` while it has slid away. `register-head.css` then pins `[data-sticky-head] thead th` at `top:var(--register-head-h,0px)` with `z-index:6` (below the heading row's 9 and the row menus' 80), `transition:top .22s ease` so the two rows hand over rather than jump, and `box-shadow:inset 0 -1px 0 #e6ecf6` standing in for the collapsed border a sticky cell loses - the same two properties the Chart of Accounts grid has used since it pinned its own headings. No fill is declared: every shell already paints its `th` opaquely, and a sticky cell must stay opaque to cover the rows passing under it.
- The hard part was scroll containers. A `position:sticky` element takes its nearest scrolling ancestor as its containing block, so a heading inside `.ivScroll` or `.itemsTableWrap` - both `overflow:auto` - would pin to a box that never moves and never appear to pin at all. The module therefore finds that box, releases it to `overflow:visible` with a `stickyHeadWrap` class **only while the table actually fits it horizontally**, and re-checks on resize. Measured on the sales orders register, whose table declares `min-width:920px`: at 1440 the wrapper is released and the heading pins at `top:76px`, while at 900 and 760 the class is gone, the wrapper keeps its 346px and 480px of sideways scrolling, and the layout is untouched. Nothing is broken where a heading cannot pin, and no page had to be told about it.
- Cost: one passive capture-phase scroll listener that only toggles one class, one `MutationObserver` on the body coalesced to a single run per animation frame so a route change or a filtered table is picked up, and a `focusin` handler so a page action focused while the row is away brings it back instead of leaving the focused control off screen. The scroll path writes no markup and starts no timer.
- Verified live in the in-app browser at 1440x800 across fourteen pages, each with its own measured offset: Invoices, Credit Notes, Payment Receipts, Sales Orders, Customers, Items, Inventory Adjustments, Journal Entries, Budgets and Period Lock pin at 76/76/76/76/76/76/78/76/79/80px - the heading row's own height on each - while the four purchase and payment registers that are not yet on the merged row (Purchase Orders, Purchase Bills, Goods Receipts, Payments Made) pin at `0px`, which is correct because they have no page heading row to clear. Chart of Accounts still computes `65px` from its own `--am-coa-bar-h`, so its bespoke implementation is untouched. No page reported horizontal overflow at any width.
- The hand-over was measured on the invoices register at 1440x420: at the top the row is visible with `--register-head-h` 76px and the heading pinned at `top:76px`; after one downward scroll the row carries `isHidden`, the variable reads 0px and the heading's `top` is 0, so the column heading takes the row's place; one upward scroll restores both. Across the same three states the table's x stayed 302 and its width 1090, the row height stayed 61px, and the heading's bottom edge never overlapped the first data row.
- Validation actually executed: a new `tests/sticky-table-head.test.mjs` pins the threshold, the shared scope and exclusion selectors, the marking and the published offset, the pinned-heading rule and its print fallback, the conditional wrapper release, the single passive listener, the frame-coalesced observer and the focus return; the full sweep is 717 tests / 706 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed. The viewport override was reset afterwards.
- Left alone deliberately: the Chart of Accounts page keeps its own `--am-coa-bar-h` rules, because they are more specific and already behind a tested invariant. Registers that genuinely need horizontal scrolling at narrow widths keep it and simply do not pin their headings there - trading that for a pinned heading would have meant squeezing eight or ten columns into a phone width. And the pages still to be migrated to the merged heading row keep their page context in normal flow, which already scrolls away and returns on its own; their headings now pin, and giving them the sliding row as well is the remaining rollout.


## Payment Receipts: the cut-off cells and a View details action - 2026-09-21

- User request, in two parts: the payment mode is not completely visible in the receipts table, fix it; and add a View Details button for the receipt details.
- The mode was measured before touching anything. The `Mode & amount` cell renders `{money(x.amount)}<small>{x.mode}{x.bank?' · '+accountName(x.bank):''}</small>`, and that small needed 109px inside a 95px content box - and the `Reference` cell was cut off in the same table, needing 123px inside 115px. Both were being ellipsised by the sheet rule `.receiptTable th,.receiptTable td{overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis}` over the table `white-space:nowrap`.
- Two fixes, because a fixed percentage alone cannot hold free text. The widths were rebalanced to 12/9/12/12/14/13/13/15 - the reference from 11% to 14% and the mode from 11% to 13%, paid for by the invoice, receipt, branch and customer columns - and the three free-text columns now wrap: `.receiptWorkspace table.receiptTable td:nth-child(2), td:nth-child(5), td:nth-child(7) small{white-space:normal;overflow-wrap:anywhere}`. Only `white-space` and `overflow-wrap` are overridden, so an unbreakable token breaks rather than spilling over the neighbouring cell and the sheet ellipsis stays as the last resort. Verified: no cell or small line reports `scrollWidth > clientWidth` at 1440px, and the mode reads `Bank Transfer · Bank` and the reference `UTR-DEMO-1001` in full.
- The row now carries a visible `View details` button beside the row menu, the way the sales orders register carries one `View details` beside its kebab. It calls the existing `open(x)`, so it opens the receipt detail on Overview - verified live: the button opened `Receipt details · RCPT-00001` with its Overview and Allocation tabs, and the back button returned to the register. The menu no longer duplicates it: the `View` entry is gone and `Preview & templates` now leads, while `Edit draft`, `Allocate`, `Reverse`, `Print receipt`, `Save as PDF`, `View journal`, `View ledger` and `Audit history` all stay.
- The button is on the register row-button tokens the invoices and sales orders registers use - `min-height:36px`, `padding:0 9px`, `1px solid #d0d5dd`, 7px radius, `#98a2b3` hover, `#84adff` focus ring - and the trigger beside it was restated to the matching 36px square (`display:inline-grid`, `place-items:center`, `width/height:36px`, `padding:0`, the same border and radius), so the pair reads as one control instead of a labelled button beside a rounded chip. The menu is `display:inline-block` rather than a flex item on purpose: as a flex item its open 218px panel would have been shrunk to the column width, and the page deliberately keeps that panel in normal table flow.
- The Actions column went from 10% to 15%. At 14% the button and trigger fitted the content box by 0px and the `.ivScroll` wrapper grew a horizontal scrollbar; the last point came from the receipt column, which takes the number and the date on separate lines and did not need it.
- Row height is unchanged at 69px, and that number is not from this work: the `Payment & type` cell's status pill is 25px plus a 3px gap plus the 16px type line, which is 44px plus the 24px cell padding. Every other cell is shorter.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/receipt-ui.test.mjs` is 9/9 with a test added that pins the row button and its tokens, the 36px trigger, the inline rather than flex menu, the three wrapping columns, the eight widths and their 100% total, and the removal of the duplicate menu entry; the three receipt suites are 23/23 together; the full sweep is 713 tests / 702 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed. The viewport override was reset afterwards.
- Verified live at 1440x800: eight columns at 126/94/126/126/146/136/136/157, no clipped cell, no clipped small line, `.ivScroll` scroll overflow 0, page overflow 0, the button 93x36 and the trigger 36x36.


## Payment Receipts: an Invoice column, and the widths that were not being applied - 2026-09-21

- User request: add one more column for the invoice to the Payment Receipts table/datagrid.
- The register carried the invoice it settles as a `small` stacked inside the first cell (`Receipt & date`), because an earlier pass folded it there rather than giving it a column. It is now its own column, second, headed `Invoice`: the row map became a block body so it reads `receiptAllocations(db,x).filter(a=>!a.voided)` once into `const allocated`, the first cell keeps only the receipt number over its date, and the new cell prints every invoice the receipt settles joined by commas, or `Unallocated` when it settles none, with `n invoices` beneath when there is more than one. `Unallocated` is the word the module already uses for a receipt with no allocation, so the column does not invent a second vocabulary.
- Adding the column surfaced a latent defect rather than causing one. The table wears `class="ivInvoiceTable receiptTable"` inside `section.invoiceWorkspace`, and `src/invoice-workspace.css` declares the first seven columns as `.invoiceWorkspace .ivInvoiceTable th:nth-child(n)` - one class more specific than this sheet's bare `.receiptTable th:nth-child(n)`. All seven of the receipts sheet's own widths had therefore been losing since they were written, and the register was quietly laid out on the invoice proportions. Measured before the fix: 143/162/114/152/114/152/114/95 against the declared 15/13/13/12/11/15/11/10, and the header comment claiming the page declares its own widths was untrue.
- The eighth column is what made it visible. With no declared width an eighth column collapses; with one it takes its own share and the browser scales the other seven down to fit, which is exactly what the first measurement showed - every column about 11% narrower than intended and the new one 95px wide. The fix is to repeat the section class and the element: `src/receipts.css` now writes `.receiptWorkspace table.receiptTable th:nth-child(n)` for all eight columns, which outranks the invoice rule, and the eight widths - 15/12/13/12/11/14/11/12 - total exactly 100%. Verified after the fix: 157/126/136/126/115/146/115/126 against the declared percentages, to the pixel.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/receipt-ui.test.mjs` is 8/8 with one test added that renders three receipts - a legacy payment, an advance with no allocation, and one receipt allocated across two invoices - and asserts the header list, the `Invoice` column second, the single allocation read, the joined invoice numbers, the `Unallocated` fallback, the `2 invoices` line, that the invoice no longer sits in the receipt cell, and that the width set stops at eight columns; the full sweep is 712 tests / 701 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Verified live at 1440x800: the table is 1046px wide inside the card, the eight columns measure 15/12/13/12/11/14/11/12%, the header reads Receipt & date, Invoice, Branch & organisation, Payment & type, Reference, Customer & type, Mode & amount, Actions, and the demo receipt shows `INV-00001`. No horizontal overflow at 1440, 1280, 1100, 900 or 700px. The viewport override was reset afterwards.
- Left alone deliberately. The row action menu is a 218px panel that opens in normal table flow, which the sheet chose on purpose so every entry stays visible down the row, and `.ivScroll:has(details[open]){padding-bottom:370px}` reserves the horizontal scrollbar it needs; the Actions column is 12% and the open panel therefore still scrolls inside the card exactly as it did before the column was added. Below about 700px the table squeezes its columns rather than scrolling, which is the pre-existing behaviour of every register on `ivInvoiceTable`; a `min-width` on the table would trade it for horizontal scrolling, which the same instruction asked me not to add.

The toolbar is a direct child of the heading and no longer inside the card, the actions are in the heading, the search sits 10px from Filters, the funnel measures 17x17, the panel leads with Status, and the split menu reads `Bank matching` and `Settings`. Scrolling down added `isHidden`. No horizontal overflow at 1440. The viewport override was reset afterwards.

## Filter icon parity, the invoice split menu and one row action per sales order - 2026-09-21

- User request, three parts: the filter icon on Sales Orders and Sales Invoices must match the one on Customers, Chart of Accounts and the other modules; the Sales invoice split menu must lose `Export` and `Use demo example`; and each Sales order row must lose its `Preview` and `Edit` buttons and keep one `View details` that redirects to the invoice detail.
- The icons were already the same element everywhere - every register renders `IconFilter` from `@tabler/icons-react` at `size={17}` - so the difference had to be measured rather than read. The glyph path and the 17px attributes were identical, but the rendered box was 8.5x17 on the invoices register and 5.3x17 on the sales orders register against 17x17 on Customers, Journal Entries, Chart of Accounts, Vendors, Items and Inventory Adjustments: a squashed funnel reads as a different icon. Flex shrinking reached the glyph inside the disclosure.

The disclosure is a flex item of the nowrap register toolbar, so it shrank, and its icon shrank first because the button label is `white-space:nowrap` and cannot.
- The fix is in two places on purpose: `src/sales-orders.css` now reads `.soFiltersMore{position:relative;flex:none;margin-left:auto}` for the root cause, and `src/register-head.css` guards every register so it cannot happen again - `.registerHead details:has(>summary[aria-label="Open filters"]){flex:0 0 auto}` and `.registerHead summary[aria-label="Open filters"]>svg{flex:0 0 auto;width:17px;height:17px}`. Verified live: the funnel measures 17x17 on Sales Orders and Sales Invoices, and Customers, Journal Entries, Chart of Accounts, Vendors, Items and Inventory Adjustments still measure 17x17 with no horizontal overflow at 1440, 1280, 1100, 1000, 900, 820 or 700px wide.

- On the Sales orders register the two row buttons became one `View details` that resolves the order invoice the same way `convertSalesOrder` does - `order.invoice.id` first, then the invoice store by `sourceOrder` - and hands it to the Invoices module through the `wayvida-open-invoice` sessionStorage key the row menu already used. Verified live: the row for SO-00002 navigated to Invoices and opened INV-0004, its linked invoice.
- Removing the `Edit` button would have removed the only way to edit an order, since the row menu never carried one, so editing moved into that menu rather than disappearing: `SalesOrderActions` takes `onEdit` and leads with `Edit order` on the exact condition the row button was disabled by (`!!order.invoice || Completed || Cancelled`). Verified live: `Edit order` is listed first and disabled for SO-00002, which has a linked invoice, and enabled for SO-00001, which does not. `.soPreviewButton` and its hover rule are deleted from `src/sales-orders.css`, and the Actions column gives back the width the two buttons freed - 17% to 13%, with Customer 10.5% to 12.5% and Branch 14% to 16% in the live width block and the matching adjustments in the superseded block above it, so both stay consistent.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` and `tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` are 68/68 with the old Preview pin replaced by a View details test and a new test pinning that the row menu keeps editing; the full sweep is 709 tests / 698 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures in the Accounts, Journal, Operational modules, Settings and date-picker suites; `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed. The viewport override used for the measurement was reset afterwards.
