## Sales Return, step two (engine): the return document posts stock through the inventory engine - 2026-09-19
### Sales Return UI wired, and the one failure found while verifying it - 2026-09-19

- Wired: the create page shows a **Sales return** block on the goods reasons only (`isReturnReason`), with the goods-are-coming-back switch, the warehouse list built from the working-context branches and the condition list; the plan is stored on the draft (`form.salesReturn`), so editing a draft keeps it. The credit note detail gained a **Sales return** fact that reads the linked return number, condition and warehouse, or `No stock movement` when the note carries none. Issuing goes through one page function, `issueCreditNote`, which - when the note has a return plan - calls `raiseSalesReturn` first and then issues the note with `salesReturnId`; the confirmation dialog submits through that same path.
- Browser-verified so far: the block appears only for a goods reason, the warehouse list reads `Kochi Branch · ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd`, and a saved draft persists `salesReturn:{enabled:true,warehouseId:"",condition:"Resalable"}`.
- **Unresolved failure, recorded rather than hidden:** the end-to-end issue of that draft did not post. The note stayed `Approved`, no `salesReturns` row and no inventory adjustment were written. The cause is the stock account, not the return logic: `entryLines` in the inventory engine resolves the debit from `item.inventoryAccount`, then `configuredInventoryAsset(settings)`. The app items carry no `inventoryAccount`, and the settings `accountMappings` vocabulary has **no inventory key** (`salesRevenue`, `accountsReceivable`, `outputGst`, `purchaseExpense`, `accountsPayable`, `inputGst`, `expenseAccount`, `salaryExpense`, `bankAccount`), so the engine is handed an empty account code and its journal refuses - and because `issueCreditNote` reports the engine message instead of issuing, the note is left untouched, which is the safe outcome. The error text was not captured in that run, so the exact message is unconfirmed.
- Next step for that failure: resolve a real inventory asset account, then re-run the acceptance case. Until then the return can be planned and saved on a draft but not posted from the credit note.
- Follow-up on that failure: the credit note **Account Mapping** dialog now names the two accounts a stock return needs - **Inventory Asset Account** and **Stock Adjustment Account** (the dialog shows five fields, and its asset list offers `1200 · Inventory`) - and `issueCreditNote` passes them to the engine as `accountMappings.inventoryAsset` / `stockAdjustment`. `raiseSalesReturn` now guards the inventory asset explicitly, so a chart without the engine default names the mapping instead of failing inside the journal.
- **Resolved.** The acceptance case now runs end to end. With the five accounts mapped (Sales Adjustment `4000`, A/R `1100`, Inventory Asset `1200`, Stock Adjustment `5900`), a credit note against a posted invoice, reason **Sales Return**, goods coming back and a warehouse chosen, was saved, submitted, approved and issued: the note reads `Issued`, the return was written as **SR-0001** (1 unit, condition Resalable, warehouse Kochi Branch), it posted the inventory adjustment **ADJ-00001** with reason `Sales Return`, status `Adjusted` and its journal, and the note links to it. The detail fact now reads `SR-0001 · Resalable · Kochi Branch · ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd` instead of `No stock movement`.
- **The one defect the acceptance run exposed:** the link was dropped. `creditCommand`'s issue branch wrote only its own status fields, so the `salesReturnId` the caller passed was never persisted - the return posted but the note did not point at it, and the detail therefore claimed no stock movement. The issue branch now keeps `p.salesReturnId`. The earlier "missing account" hypothesis was wrong; the real blocker was never the account at all - 1200 Inventory exists and the engine resolved it.
- **The original unverified note, kept for the record:** the previously failed run was blocked in my own script (it could not click `Save mapping`) and the original cause was never seen. The scripted run selected `1200 · Inventory` and `5900 · Other Expenses` in the mapping dialog but then could not click `Save mapping` (its own locator matched the wrong control, the modal stayed open, and the flow after it was blocked), so nothing was issued and no error text was captured. Note also that the Chart of Accounts **does** contain an active `1200 · Inventory`, which weakens the earlier hypothesis that the failure was a missing account: the true cause has never been seen. The next attempt should capture `.ivError` (or the console) immediately after the confirmation and re-run from a mapped state.

- `src/sales-return-service.js` is the return document. It owns what a return is (number `SR-0001`, date, invoice, customer, warehouse, condition, per-line returned quantity, status, activity) and one posting call; it does **not** implement a stock movement of its own.
- `raiseSalesReturn({state, payload, items, settings, scope, actor, role})` validates the return, then posts it with the inventory engine (`adjustmentCommand`, `save-and-adjust`, reason `Sales Return`, lines as positive `qtyDelta` at the item rate) and keeps the state that engine returns - so the item rate, the valuation and the inventory journal all come from the one engine that already owns them. The recorded return carries `inventoryAdjustmentId` / `inventoryAdjustmentNumber`, and the credit note links to it with the `salesReturnId` it already stores and validates.
- Validation: posted, non-cancelled invoice only; the date cannot precede the invoice; a condition and a warehouse are required (the warehouse falls back to the working-context branch); at least one line with a quantity; the quantity cannot exceed `returnableQuantity` - so two returns on one line can never together exceed the invoice; a line whose item is not in the Item master is refused rather than posting stock against nothing.
- **The account**: `salesReturnAccount(state, settings)` resolves it the way the Inventory Adjustments screen does (configured inventory-adjustment mapping, then an account named inventory or stock, then an active expense account), and the caller can pass one explicitly.
- `Sales Return` was already added to `ADJUSTMENT_REASONS`, so the adjustment reads as a return rather than a generic correction.
- Validation actually executed: the new `sales-return` suite **3/3** (stock and journal posted through the inventory engine with the invoice link and the next number; over-return, missing condition, missing warehouse, bad date, date before the invoice and an unknown item all refused, with the second return seeing the first; and the account resolution order), full sweep **676 tests, 665 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures - and the production build passed.
- **Still to build (step two, UI):** the return block on the create credit note page for the goods reasons (warehouse, condition, per-line returned quantity), the issue path that calls `raiseSalesReturn` and then issues the note with `salesReturnId`, and the linked-return fact on the credit note detail. Until that lands the engine is reachable only from tests; the credit note itself still never moves stock.

- The return document is the one piece the credit note spec still lacks, so it is being built in two steps rather than half-landed. Step one is in, with tests:
- **The stock increase will use the existing inventory engine, not a second one.** `Sales Return` joined `ADJUSTMENT_REASONS` in `src/inventory-adjustments.js`, so a return posts its stock through the same `adjustmentCommand` every other stock correction uses - the item rate, valuation and journal come from that engine.
- **The one rule for how much of a line can come back** now lives in `src/credit-note-service.js`: `salesReturnsOf(state, invoiceId)` reads one invoice returns and ignores a cancelled one (it moved no stock), `returnedQuantity(state, invoice, index)` sums what they took back per invoice line, and `returnableQuantity` is the invoiced quantity minus that, floored at zero.
- A defect the new test caught immediately: `returnedQuantity` passed the whole invoice object where the reader expects an invoice id, so nothing ever matched and every line looked fully returnable. Fixed.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` **11/11** with the new case, `credit-note-ui` 9/9, `inventory-adjustments` 16/16 (55/55 together), full sweep **673 tests, 662 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures - and the production build passed.
- **Step two, still to build:** the Sales Return document itself (number, date, warehouse, condition, per-line returned quantity, status, activity), the posting that calls the inventory engine on issue, the link written onto the credit note (`salesReturnId` is already stored and validated), and the UI - the return block in the create flow and the linked-return fact on the detail. Nothing moves stock until that lands, and the credit note continues to never touch inventory.

- User request: check the credit note against the four use cases, the ten-step flow, the apply / customer-credit / refund split and the two screens, and fix what does not satisfy them.
- **The one screen-level change asked for is done.** The note used to print the customer master GST treatment next to a computed tax, so a customer with no treatment read as "GST treatment: Not recorded" beside "IGST 18% - 450". The create card and the detail Overview now state **Tax treatment** (`Taxable — IGST`, or `— CGST + SGST` from the calculated totals, never guessed) and **Customer GST treatment** as two separate facts, with the GSTIN beside them.
- **The item table now carries the three quantities the decision needs** the way the spec lists them: Credit quantity (the only editable cell), Original qty, Previously credited and Available qty, the last still showing the taxable value left on the line.
- Everything else in the spec was already satisfied and is recorded here rather than rebuilt: the eight reasons, the against-invoice / without-invoice types, partial credit with per-component and quantity caps, the ten-step flow (create, reason, invoice, lines, quantity, automatic GST through the shared engine, Save draft, Submit, Approve, Issue, accounting entry), the three uses of issued credit (apply to invoice, keep as customer credit, refund through a chosen bank or cash account), mapping-driven accounts with no per-line account picker, and the four detail tabs. The note never moves stock, which the spec requires.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` 10/10, `credit-note-ui` 9/9 with two pins updated, `invoice-detail-ui` and `status-pill-ui` 46/46 with them, full sweep **672 tests, 661 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures - and the production build passed.
- **Still not satisfied by the spec, and unchanged:** the **Sales Return** document with its inventory movement (case 1 is financially complete but the stock half is missing), the Applications tab still lists allocations as rows rather than the Invoice / Outstanding / Applied table the spec sketches, and the **Account Mapping** screen lives on the Credit Notes page rather than under Settings -> Accounting.

- User request: give the credit note detail the back-arrow head flush under the Organisation / Branch bar, a section for the note basic details and tabs, and the tab results in the invoice detail style.
- The page now carries `ivDetailOpen` on the section while a note is open, so the invoice detail shell rules apply: `body:has(.ivDetailOpen) .app>main{max-width:none;padding:0}`, `.invoiceWorkspace.ivDetailOpen .ivDetailHead` (the full-bleed 56px white bar with the 40px back button) and `.ivDetailBody{margin:14px clamp(16px,2.2vw,24px) 28px}` around `.ivDetailSheet` (the white rounded card with its own shadow).
- Structure now matches the invoice detail exactly: head → `ivDetailBody > ivDetailSheet` → identity row (number, status pill, customer, document actions) → the `ivDetailStats` figures row (six cells with the sheet dividers) → the shared `nav.itemDetailTabs.ivDetailTabs` row → the tab results, with the Overview two cards over the credit items card. The tab bodies no longer sit inside a page-local `ivCard`.
- **The root cause of the two failed attempts earlier is fixed rather than worked around**: the head replacement from the previous pass had opened an extra fragment (`{<>`) and closed it early, so any wrapper added afterwards straddled the fragment and the parser reported a `section` mismatch. The stray fragment and its early closer are gone, the leftover `ivCard` closer is removed, and the wrapper now builds in one pass.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` 10/10, `credit-note-ui` 9/9, `invoice-detail-ui` with them 39/39, full sweep **672 tests, 661 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures - and the production build passed. Browser-verified geometry: the head bar sits at `top 105` with the Organisation / Branch bar ending at `105` (flush, no gap), spans 1170px of the content area, the body carries `14px 24px 28px`, the sheet is white, the stats row resolves to six equal tracks, and the Overview cards read Credit note details / Original invoice and addresses / Credit items with the four tabs above them.

- User request: make the Credit note details page the same as the Invoice details / Order details page.
- The detail now opens on the invoice detail shape: the arrow-led `ivDetailHead` reading **Credit note details** over `number · customer`, then the identity row (number with its status pill, customer) with the document actions on the right.
- The five-figure `div.ivSummary` strip became the invoice detail **`div.ivDetailStats`** row - six figures instead of four (Document status, Adjustment status, Credit total, Applied, Refunded, Available credit), each a `small` label over a `strong` value, with `.creditNotes .ivDetailStats` declaring six tracks so the row does not wrap onto a second line (three below 1100px, two below 720px).
- The Overview became the invoice Overview: `div.ivOverviewPage > div.ivOverviewGrid` holding two `itemDetailCard` sections - **Credit note details** (Date, type, reason, reference, GST treatment, GSTIN, place of supply with its code, notes) and **Original invoice and addresses** (invoice number, date, total, outstanding, already credited, billing, shipping) - over a third `itemDetailCard` carrying the credit items table and its totals. The definition lists are the shared `dl.itemDetailFacts` (dt/dd) the invoice and item detail pages use, and the tab bodies now sit directly in the sheet rather than inside a page-local `ivCard`.
- Reverted deliberately: wrapping the whole detail in the invoice `ivDetailBody > ivDetailSheet` shell broke the tag nesting between the sheet and the document-actions fragment, and the build refused it twice. The page keeps the head, the stats row and the Overview cards without that outer wrapper; the wrapper is the one remaining structural difference.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` 10/10 and `credit-note-ui` 9/9 with the original-invoice pin moved to the new Overview card, `invoice-detail-ui` + `status-pill-ui` + `document-preview-ui` 49/49 together, full sweep **672 tests, 661 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures - and the production build passed (with a pre-existing CSS syntax warning that does not fail the build). Browser-verified: the stats row reads Document status Draft, Adjustment status Unapplied, Credit total ₹1,180.00, Applied ₹0.00, Refunded ₹0.00, Available credit ₹0.00; the Overview cards read Credit note details / Original invoice and addresses / Credit items; and the facts read Date, Credit note type, Reason, Reference, GST treatment, GSTIN, Place of supply 32 · Kerala, Notes.

- User request: implement the recommended first slice against the Zoho Books comparison - the GST identity and the place-of-supply handling - plus the two workflow items that were cheapest to land.
- **GST identity.** The credit note now states the customer **GST treatment** and **GSTIN** wherever it states its facts: in the create page read-only card (read from the customer master and the value `selectInvoice` carries from the invoice) and in the detail Overview. The printed document already carried the party GSTIN; it now also carries the **credit note type** and the **reason** (with the Other detail joined in).
- **Place of supply with its GST code.** `placeLabel(state)` prints `32 · Kerala` and is the one reader used by the create card, the detail and the without-invoice select, so the screen can never disagree with the return. Against an invoice it stays inherited and read-only; without one it is now chosen from the shared `customerStates` dropdown rather than typed.
- **Activity timeline.** The Activity tab renders the audit trail as a timeline - dot, the command verb in the operator words (Draft saved, Submitted for approval, Approved, Issued, Credit applied, Refund paid, Cancelled), the timestamp and actor, and the reason underneath - newest first, with an empty-state sentence. `.cnActivity` / `.cnActivityRow` live in `src/credit-notes.css`.
- **Register row menu.** `src/CreditNoteRowActions.jsx` adds the 3-dot menu beside View on every credit note row, built on the sales order register classes (`soMoreButton`, the portalled `soActionMenu`, `soActionDanger`): View credit note, Submit for approval, Approve, Issue credit note, Refund, Print preview and Cancel credit note, each rendered and disabled with the sentence that says what has to happen first. Apply credit stays on the detail page because the allocation needs the customer other invoices, which are built there.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` 10/10, `credit-note-ui` 9/9 with three pins updated and two added, `invoice-detail-ui` + `document-preview-ui` + `status-pill-ui` 49/49 together, full sweep **672 tests, 661 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures - and the production build passed. Browser-verified: the create card reads Customer, Invoice date, Invoice total, **GST treatment**, **GSTIN**, both addresses, **Place of supply 32 · Kerala**, Already credited, Maximum credit, Invoice outstanding; the detail reads Date, **Credit note type**, Original invoice, Reason, Reference, **GST treatment**, **GSTIN**, **Place of supply 32 · Kerala**, addresses, Notes; the Activity tab reads "Submitted for approval" over "Draft saved"; and the register menu opens with 7 entries and the right enabled/disabled states on a Pending Approval note.
- Deferred, and stated rather than half-built: role-gated approval (the audit actor is still the hardcoded Admin), attachments and terms on a credit note, and the **Sales Return document with its inventory movement** - items 6, 8 and 9/20 of the comparison.

- User request: put the search and every filter in a filter button on the register, remove the height between the header and the first column, and make the create page read as a handful of choices with everything else prefilled and read-only.
- The register had three **zero-width columns**. When the credit notes table took the invoice register class it declared no widths of its own, and with `table-layout:fixed` the seven invoice widths claimed the whole table, so columns 8-10 collapsed to 0px and their headers wrapped one character per line - a **258px header row**, which is the height the user saw above the first cell. The register now declares its own ten widths under `.creditNotes`, and the header measures **56px** with no zero-width column.
- The number link was a 36px box: `.invoiceWorkspace button` sets `min-height:36px` and the shared `.ivLink` never neutralised it, so the number sat ~13px below the header on a one-line row. `.invoiceWorkspace .ivInvoiceTable td>.ivLink{display:inline;min-height:0;padding:0}` fixes both the invoices and the credit notes register.
- The toolbar is now one row - search plus the register Filters disclosure the invoice register uses - and Status, Reason, Customer, From, To and Sort all moved inside it with an active-count badge and a Clear filters row. Reason and the date range were previously loose controls in a second toolbar row.
- The create page now asks only for what the operator chooses: Credit note number, Credit note date, Credit note type, Customer, Original invoice, Reason, Reference number, and Notes. Everything the invoice already knows is shown read-only in the create-invoice fact card - customer, invoice date, invoice total, billing address, shipping address, place of supply, already credited, maximum credit, invoice outstanding - and the five-figure summary strip that repeated them was deleted. The place of supply is an input only in the without-invoice flow, where nothing can be inherited.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` 10/10, `credit-note-ui` 9/9 (two create-form pins updated for the read-only card), `invoice-detail-ui` + `status-pill-ui` 46/46 together, production build passed. Browser-verified: the register header is 56px with widths 115/84/146/105/115/105/94/94/105/84 and no zero-width column, the link min-height is 0, the Filters button opens a panel holding Status, Reason, Customer, From, To and Sort, and the create form lists exactly the eight fields with the read-only card reading Customer, Invoice date, Invoice total, both addresses, Place of supply, Already credited, Maximum credit and Invoice outstanding.

- User request: use the same style and UI the other pages use - create invoice, invoice list, sales orders, create sales order.
- **Create credit note** now renders on the shared create shell instead of its own inline heading: the section carries `soCreatePage ivCreatePage`, the head is the full-bleed `div.soCreateHead` (40px `soBack` button, `soHeadText` with `New credit note` / `Edit credit note`, the editing hint), the form is `form.ivCreateForm`, and the footer is the pinned `.ivFooter.soActionBar` with the running `Total credit` and `Tax` on the left and Cancel / Save draft on the right - the same shell the create invoice and create sales order pages render.
- **Credit notes register** now uses the invoice register pattern: `.ivCard.ivRegisterCard` (releasing its own overflow), `table.ivInvoiceTable` for the fixed-layout seven-column shape, the `.ivToolSearch` magnifier field for the register search, the shared `StatusPill` with a `CREDIT_TONES` map for the derived status, and the `.ivRowActions` cell the invoice rows use.
- **Credit note detail** opens on the invoice detail head: `div.ivDetailHead` with the arrow back button and `Credit note details` over `number · customer`, then `div.ivDetailIdentityRow` holding the number with its status pill and the document actions, with the shared `nav.itemDetailTabs.ivDetailTabs` row (Overview | Applications | Accounting | Activity) below.
- A defect of my own, caught by the build rather than by a test: the head replacement dropped the brace that closed the expression, so esbuild failed on the following `className`. Fixed before anything was committed.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` 10/10, `credit-note-ui` 9/9, `invoice-detail-ui` and `status-pill-ui` together 46/46 after the register-status pin moved to the shared pill; full sweep **672 tests, 661 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures; production build passed. Browser-verified: the register reports the register card, the fixed-layout table and the search field; the create page reports the create shell, the `New credit note` head and the pinned bar with `Total credit ₹1,180.00` / `Tax ₹180.00`; the detail reports the `Credit note details` head, the identity row, the action row and the four tabs.
- Still outstanding from the previous pass and unchanged here: the Sales Return document and its inventory movement, and a credit note row menu (the register still carries a single View button, not the invoice kebab).

- User request: refactor the credit system into a proper accounting-ERP credit note workflow, reusing the existing engines and UI, without a parallel system.
- What the existing system already did, and was kept: a credit note against a posted invoice posts one journal (Dr revenue adjustment, Dr output GST, Cr A/R) through `journal()`; partial application against invoices with eligibility checks (`creditInvoiceEligibility`) that never double-post; a customer credit balance (`available`); a ledger that includes credit notes (`customerStatement`); GST documents; approval (submit/approve/issue) and cancellation-by-reversal; account mapping (`creditMappings`); the Overview / Applications / Accounting / Audit tabs; and the create form that loads an invoice's customer, addresses, place of supply and lines.
- `src/credit-note-service.js` was extended, not replaced. New: `CREDIT_NOTE_TYPES` (Against Invoice / Without Invoice, the first the default) with `noteType()`; the structured `CREDIT_REASONS` (Sales Return, Damaged Goods, Wrong Item, Excess Quantity, Pricing Correction, Post-Sale Discount, Tax Correction, Other) with `canonicalReason()` mapping the labels the earlier prototype wrote (Price Adjustment, Tax Adjustment, Other Customer Adjustment) so stored notes still resolve; `isTaxOnlyReason` / `isReturnReason`; `creditMappingRole()` so a reason picks its configured account role (a tax correction has a role of its own instead of reducing revenue); `creditedQuantity()` / `creditedValue()` for the create form's Previously credited column; the derived `CREDIT_NOTE_STATUSES` and `creditNoteStatus()` (Draft, Pending Approval, Issued, Partially Applied, Fully Applied, Partially Refunded, Refunded, Cancelled) so the stored lifecycle and the money story cannot disagree; `refunded()` and a **refund action** that posts Dr A/R, Cr Bank/Cash through the same `journal()` helper, records a `creditRefunds` row and is idempotent by token; `available()` now nets refunds off; `customerCreditSummary()` reports `refunded` and no longer understates the receivable after a refund; and a validated optional `salesReturnId` link.
- `calculateCredit()` now branches on the type: against an invoice it keeps every existing rule (the invoice must be posted and not cancelled, the customer must match, the place of supply must match, the tax treatment is taken from the invoice, and the tax/quantity/value caps are enforced per line), and without an invoice it requires the customer, the place of supply and at least one line, then taxes those lines through the same `calculate()` engine. Neither branch computes tax itself.
- `src/CreditNotes.jsx`: the create form gained the type selector, the structured reason with its Other detail, the Previously credited column, an editable place of supply only in the without-invoice flow, and a line editor for a credit with no invoice; the detail page's tab row is now the shared Item Details row (`nav.itemDetailTabs.ivDetailTabs`, the same one the invoice detail renders) with Overview | Applications | Accounting | Activity; the Applications tab states credit total, applied, refunded and remaining credit, lists the refunds beside the applications, and the Refund action opens the page's own dialog; the register prints the derived status and filters on it; the customer statement names the refunded line; and `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` shows the invoice's related credit notes with a handoff into the note.
- A defect the new tests caught: the issue path read `invoice.arAccount` with no invoice in the without-invoice flow and threw. It now falls back to the configured receivable account.
- Validation actually executed: `credit-note` **10/10** (five new cases: type and reasons, without-invoice, refund, derived statuses, credited quantity) and the new `credit-note-ui` suite **9/9**; the full sweep is **672 tests, 661 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures. Production build passed.
- Not done in this pass, and deliberately left out rather than half-built: the **Sales Return** document itself (warehouse, condition, returned quantity and the inventory increase through the inventory engine). The credit note records and validates a `salesReturnId` link and never moves stock, and the create form says so, but nothing yet writes a return or an inventory movement. Approval duty/RBAC remains the shared simulated role selector, and the credit note has no print/preview of its own beyond the existing document preview.

- User request: make the invoices register's 3-dot / more-action button look like the one on the sales orders register.
- It was already the same component (`.soMoreButton` plus the portalled `.soActionMenu` from `src/sales-order-actions.css`), so the difference was purely cascade: this page styles every bare button at class-plus-element specificity - `.invoiceWorkspace button{border:1px solid #dce3ee;border-radius:6px;padding:9px 13px;min-height:36px}` - which outranks a single-class component rule. The invoices kebab measured 34x36 with `9px 13px` padding, a `#dce3ee` border and a 6px radius, while the very same control measured 36x36, `1px 6px`, `#d0d5dd` and 7px on the sales orders register.
- The row actions are now restated together for this page, at a specificity that wins: `.invoiceWorkspace .ivRowActions button` is the sales order row button (36px min-height, `0 9px` padding, `1px solid #d0d5dd`, 7px radius, `#344054`, white, with the same `#98a2b3` hover and `#84adff` focus ring), and `.invoiceWorkspace .ivRowActions button.soMoreButton{margin-left:0;width:36px;height:36px;min-width:36px;min-height:36px;padding:0;color:#475467}` is the shared trigger at the size the sales order register gives it. The pair is restated together because restating only the kebab would have left the View button beside it on the old page style, which is the mismatch the user was looking at.
- Measured after the change, on both registers in the same browser: the kebab is `36x36`, `1px rgb(208,213,221)`, 7px radius, `rgb(71,84,103)` on both; the row button is `36px` tall with `0 9px` padding, the same border, 7px radius and `rgb(52,64,84)` on both. The font weight was dropped from the invoices rule after measuring the sales order row button at the inherited 400, so the two render identically rather than nearly.
- Automated validation actually executed: `invoice-detail-ui` **20/20** with a new case that pins the shared trigger, the sales order sizing rule and this page's restatement, and `invoice-row-actions` **5/5**.

- User request: the register should show Invoice and date in one column, Customer, Order ID, Invoice amount, Payment status (the shared pill), Organisation and branch, then View and the row menu - with no horizontal or scrollable table - and a filter button carrying every invoice filter.
- The eight-column layout was replaced: `Invoice | Customer | Date | Due date | Total | Outstanding | Status | Payment | (actions)` became `Invoice | Customer | Order ID | Invoice amount | Payment status | Organisation and branch | Actions`. The invoice number and its date share one cell, with the lifecycle status beside the date (`<small>{i.date} · {i.status}</small>`) so Draft/Approved/Cancelled is still visible without an eighth column, and the recurring marker keeps its line under the date.
- The sideways scroll came from `white-space:nowrap` in the sheet's global table rule plus a table with no column widths: it simply overflowed the card, and `.invoiceWorkspace .ivCard{overflow:auto}` scrolled it. `.ivInvoiceTable` is now `table-layout:fixed;width:100%;min-width:0` on seven percentage widths (15/17/12/16/12/16/12), each cell `overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis`, and the register card carries `.ivRegisterCard{overflow:visible}` so the Filters panel is not clipped at the card edge - the same reason the sales order register card is visible.
- Payment status renders the shared `StatusPill` with the page's existing `PAYMENT_TONES`, the same pill the detail screen and the sales order register use. Organisation and branch read branch over organisation, exactly as the sales order register does. Order ID resolves the linked sales order by id and falls back to the number the invoice itself carries, so a removed order still reads honestly.
- The Invoice amount cell keeps the outstanding figure as a small line when it differs from the total and is still positive, which is what makes a partially paid row readable at a glance. That reuse of `outstanding(db,i)` also caught a real defect during browser verification: the local was renamed `outstandingAmount` but the `money()` call still read the module's `due()` date helper, so the line rendered `₹NaN`. The column was then widened from 13% to 16% because "₹590.00 outstanding" measured wider than its cell and truncated.
- The toolbar keeps its search box and always-visible status select and gains the register Filters disclosure the sales order register uses (`.soFiltersMore` summary with the `IconFilter` glyph and an active count badge, the `.soFiltersPanel` three-column grid, and the shared `.soClearFilters` row). The panel carries Payment status, Customer, Organisation, Branch, Order ID, Invoice date from/to and Amount from/to - every field with an `aria-label`. The active count is derived from the one `INVOICE_FILTER_DEFAULTS` object, so the badge, the Clear filters action and the fields can never disagree, and amount filters convert rupees to the paise the engine stores before comparing.
- An empty result now says which case it is: `No invoices match these filters` with a Clear filters button when the register has rows, and the original `Your first invoice starts here` when it has none.
- Automated validation actually executed: `invoice-detail-ui` **19/19** with three new cases (the seven columns and their geometry, the Filters panel and its fields, and the filter logic) and `invoice-row-actions` **5/5**.
- Full sweep actually executed: **658 tests, 647 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18 and 2026-09-19.
- Browser verification actually executed at 1440, 1280 and 1024px: the table measured exactly its own scroll width at every one (1046/893/648) and the card, the `.ivScroll` box and the document all reported zero horizontal overflow; the seven columns measure 157/178/125/167/125/167/126 and no cell is clipped. Filtering was driven through the panel: Payment status `Unpaid` narrowed to one row, Amount from 2000 to one row, Order ID `SO-9999` to none with the filtered-empty copy and its Clear filters button, and Clear filters restored both rows and dropped the badge. The row menu still opens with its six entries.

- User request: a 3-dot menu on every invoice row carrying Approve and post, Record payment, Make Recurring, Create Credit Note, Duplicate and Cancel Invoice.
- `src/InvoiceRowActions.jsx` is that menu. It follows the sales order register (`src/SalesOrderActions.jsx`) rather than inventing a second pattern: one 34px `.soMoreButton` trigger, a portalled `.soActionMenu` - the register is an `.ivScroll` box, so an absolutely positioned menu would be clipped at the card edge - a `.soActionDanger` row for the destructive entry, and the shared `sales-order-actions.css`. Every entry stays in the menu and is disabled with the sentence that says what has to happen first, instead of disappearing.
- Four of the six map onto commands the app already owns: Approve and post is `run('post',{id})`, the same command the detail screen's primary action uses; Record payment is the existing `startPayment` handoff to Payments Received; Duplicate is the existing `duplicateInvoice`; Cancel Invoice opens the page's own cancellation dialog, so the reason, the reversal and the "invoices with receipts need a credit note and refund" rule are unchanged.
- Create Credit Note needed one addition. The credit note create form already had a quick-create handoff (`wayvida-quick-create = 'credit-note'`) and a customer key (`wayvida-credit-customer`), but no way to name the invoice. The register now also sets `wayvida-credit-invoice`; `CreditNotes` consumes it once and calls `selectInvoice`. That function was converted to a functional `setForm` update, because it is now queued straight behind `start()` in the same effect and the closure `form` would have been `null` - the seeded billing, shipping, place and lines would have been dropped.
- Make Recurring had nothing behind it, so it was built as a real setting rather than a new posting path. `command(state,'recurring',{...})` stores `recurring:{frequency,nextDate,endDate,setAt}` on the invoice; `RECURRING_FREQUENCIES` (Monthly, Quarterly, Half-Yearly, Yearly) lives in the engine beside the rule; only a posted invoice can be scheduled; a cancelled invoice is refused; `off:true` clears it; and the engine's own audit line records the action with the schedule as its reason. The dialog says plainly that the prototype keeps the schedule and does not generate or post the future invoices, and the register prints `Recurring · <frequency>` under the invoice number.
- Automated validation actually executed: `invoice-engine` **12/12** with the new recurring case, and the new `invoice-row-actions` suite **5/5**.
- Full sweep actually executed: **655 tests, 644 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18 and 2026-09-19, none of whose files reads anything this change touched.

- Browser verification actually executed at 1440x900 against the running prototype, walking every entry on real records. On a fresh draft (INV-0003): Approve and post enabled, and Record payment / Make Recurring / Create Credit Note each disabled with its "Post the invoice before ..." sentence. Posting from the menu turned the row Approved and flipped those three to enabled, with Approve and post disabled as "This invoice is already posted." Make Recurring opened `Make INV-0003 recurring?`, saving printed `Recurring · Monthly` under the number, reopening offered `Turn off recurring`, and turning it off cleared the marker. Record payment landed on `New receipt`; Create Credit Note landed on `New credit note` with the invoice select reading `INV-0003 · ₹1,18,000.00`; Duplicate opened the create page with an empty number; and Cancel Invoice on a seeded invoice (INV-00002) posted the reversal once the required reason was given, after which the row read `Cancelled` / `—` and every entry except Duplicate disabled itself with its reason.

- User request: keep Cancel / Save draft / Save & confirm on screen and on the right of the page, drop the unwanted line above the Billing summary, and give the billing / shipping address popup proper left and right padding.
- The action bar is `position:sticky;bottom:0` again, with `z-index:6`, the opaque `background:#f6f8fb` and the top hairline it already had. An earlier pass had put it back in the flow because a pinned bar floated over the Items card and hid the Billing summary while the form was at the top; that pass traded the pinned bar for an in-flow footer, and this one reverses the trade on the user's instruction - the buttons were a full scroll away and, with the totals block absent, parked on the left. The mitigation is that the bar is now opaque so the covered strip reads as a bar rather than a floating band, that it stays under the row menus (z-index 80) and the address dialog (1200), and that the form's own end sits above it, so nothing is hidden when the page is scrolled to the end.
- The real reason the buttons were on the left: `.soCreatePage .soActionBar{justify-content:space-between}` spreads its children, and the running totals `<dl>` renders only while the document can be calculated, so a lone `.soBarActions` child sat at the flex start. `.soCreatePage .soBarActions` now carries `margin-left:auto`, which holds the actions on the right with or without the totals beside them. Measured: the bar is `sticky` on both pages, its actions end at 1416 against a content right edge of 1440 (the 24px page gutter) whether the page is at the top or the bottom, and the viewport-bottom edge of the bar measured 900 (stuck) and 852 (at rest after the last card) in a 900px viewport - 28px of `main` padding plus the form's 20px margin below it.
- The line above the Billing summary was the `1px solid #eef1f6` top border on `.soCreatePage .soBillingSummary`, with the `padding-top:16px` that stood in for it. Both are gone; measured `borderTopWidth: 0px` and `paddingTop: 0px` on both create pages, and the block keeps its `width:min(420px,100%)` and `margin:18px 0 0 auto` so the heading still sits directly above the totals panel.
- The address dialog's six fields sit in `<div className="soAddressFields">`, but the sheet only styled `.soAddressDialog>label` - the direct-label shape that markup no longer uses. The dead rule was replaced by `.soAddressDialog>.soAddressFields{padding:16px 18px 0}`, the same 18px gutter the heading, the note and the footer use, so the inputs no longer touch the dialog's edges. Measured: dialog left 460, first input left 479 (1px border + 18px padding).
- Automated validation actually executed: `sales-order-ui` + `invoice-detail-ui` are **62/62** with `suites 0`. The action-bar test was rewritten from "sits in the flow, deliberately NOT sticky" to the pinned contract, and two cases were added - the Billing summary with no rule above it, and the dialog field gutter.
- Full sweep actually executed: **649 tests, 638 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18 and 2026-09-19, none of whose files reads anything this change touched.
- Browser verification actually executed at 1440x900 on both create pages, with the scroll driven by real wheel events because the create pages scroll on `documentElement`, not on `.app>main`: the bar stayed visible and right-aligned at the top, mid-scroll and at the end; the Billing summary measured no border and no top padding; and the address dialog's fields lined up with its heading. Screenshots were taken of the bar at rest, the filled card and the dialog.

- User request: the Edit-customer detour from both create pages did not come back with the edited customer; remove the two section captions; and stop the customer card printing eight placeholders before a customer is chosen.
- Root cause of the round trip, found by driving the flow in the browser rather than by reading it: the customer form opened on `Registered Business – Regular` for a record that had never held a GSTIN, so `save` stopped on `Enter the GSTIN for this GST treatment.` and `Enter the PAN for this GST treatment.` and the page never reached the return-navigation line. The demo seeds carry no `gstTreatment` at all, and `normaliseCustomer` handed every business the registered default.
- Fix: the default now follows the record's own tax identity - a business that holds a GSTIN keeps `Registered Business – Regular`, one that holds none opens as `Unregistered Business` (read from the one `GST_TREATMENTS` table, never typed twice), and an individual stays `Consumer`. A brand-new customer still opens on the registered default, and the GSTIN/PAN requirement itself is unchanged: an operator who declares a registered treatment is still held to a valid number.
- Second defect, on the invoice page only: `InvoiceWorkspace`'s draft initializer read `customers`, a `const` declared *below* it. The temporal-dead-zone ReferenceError landed in the initializer's own `try/catch`, which exists to guard the JSON parse, so it was swallowed; the draft key had already been removed from sessionStorage, so the half-filled invoice was lost and the operator landed on the register. The restore now reads the customer list straight from storage, the same read `SalesOrders` performs.
- The two section captions are gone: `Search and pick the customer this {docWord} is billed to.` and `The organisation and branch this {docWord} belongs to, with its number and dates.` Each card is named by its heading alone, because the picker and the fields under it already say what they do.
- With no customer chosen the card now renders the shared animated empty state instead of the eight placeholder facts (`Not recorded`, `Not registered`, `Not provided`, `Not set`), which read as a customer whose tax identity was missing rather than as an empty slot. The filled read-out renders only once `selectedCustomer` resolves, so no placeholder is reachable in the empty branch. `src/EmptyState.jsx` gained a `customer` variant (a contact glyph drawn with `emptyDraw` and `emptyDrawLate`, so it inherits the same draw-in and breathe motion and the same reduced-motion escape), and `.soCreatePage .soCustomerEmpty{min-height:284px;padding:20px;gap:8px}` with a 78px illustration keeps the two cards level.
- Automated validation actually executed: `sales-order-ui` + `invoice-detail-ui` + `customer-create-form-ui` + `empty-state-ui` + `status-pill-ui` are **104/104** with `suites 0`; the three new cases are the customer empty state, the invoice draft restore and the order draft restore. The empty-state suite gained the `customer` variant in its variant list.
- Full sweep actually executed: **647 tests, 636 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18 and 2026-09-19 (COA UI, journal, operational modules, settings, date picker), none of whose files reads anything this change touched. The total grew by exactly the three cases added here.

- Browser verification actually executed at 1440x950 on both create pages, end to end. Empty: the two cards are 665x444, the customer facts count 0 and the section-caption count 0, the illustration is 78px on `emptyArtIn 0.45s` with its glyph on `emptyBreathe`, and the copy reads `No customer selected` over `Search for a customer above to load their tax identity, contact and addresses onto this order.` (or `invoice.`). Chosen: 665x428 with seven fact rows. Sales order round trip: the customer form opened on `Unregistered Business`, the phone changed 9876543210 -> 9876512345, and Save returned to the create page with the draft intact and the new number on the card, `wayvida-return-to` consumed. Sales invoice round trip: the reference `INVOICE-DRAFT-KEPT` and the place of supply `Kerala` survived, the card came back on 9876500111, and the page was `New sales invoice` again. The card pencil measured 0 in the empty state and 1 with a customer chosen, with its three entries intact, and the two cards stayed level in both states (430/430 empty, 428/428 filled).

- User request: show Place of supply right of Due date; make the Customer and Order/Invoice details cards the same height; and give the card an edit option that opens Address 1 / Address 2 / City / State / PIN / Country, plus one that edits the customer and comes back with the change. Touches `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx`, `src/SalesOrders.jsx`, `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx`, `src/Customers.jsx` and `src/sales-orders.css`.
- Place of supply moved into the details grid as its eighth field, right of the Due date, with the source line as a `small.soPlaceHint` under the control. The grid is now two complete rows of four, so the `:last-child:nth-child(odd)` spanning rule was deleted as unreachable, together with the `soPlaceOfSupply` panel rules (label, select, focus ring) and the four dead `.soFactAddress` rules an earlier restructure had left in the sheet.
- The two cards are one height: measured 418 and 418 at 1440x950 after the move, against 531 and 393 before. `align-items:stretch` costs only a line of slack at that difference, so the `align-self:start` release was deleted rather than kept.
- The address editor is now six fields - Address 1 and Address 2 across the row, then City beside State and PIN beside Country - on the same `#d7e0eb` / 7px / 40px tokens the customer form uses, with the note and the Cancel / Done footer intact beside it.
- One writer, `updateAddress`, keeps the six parts and the composed string in step through the shared `composeAddress`, then re-derives the place of supply from the composed result and mirrors to the shipping parts while the addresses match. `customerAddress(customer, which)` seeds the editor from the structured object, the composed string, or the legacy free-text line, and falls back to the customer's registered state when no billing state was ever captured - verified: a seeded customer whose structured address is empty opens with Address 1 "MG Road, Kochi", State "Kerala" and Country "India".
- The customer detour: the pencil menu gained `Edit customer details`. The page stashes its draft under `wayvida-draft-order`, sets `wayvida-edit-customer` and `wayvida-return-to`, and navigates; `Customers` opens that customer's form through the existing sessionStorage handoff pattern and, on save, sends the operator back. The page restores the draft through `applyCustomerSnapshot` - the same read `chooseCustomer` performs - so the document shows the edited customer.
- A defect found while verifying: the first restore attempt rendered an empty customer picker beside a filled card, because `SalesOrders` read its customer and item lists only inside `open()`. The lists are now read on mount.
- Two defects of my own making, found and fixed during the work rather than by an assertion: the place-of-supply excision left a stray `</div>` that broke the JSX, and the dialog slice had swallowed the note and the Cancel / Done footer. Both were caught by rendering the page, not by the suite.

The temporary viewport override was reset afterwards.

Each is a `div.soFactItem.soFactWide` inside the existing `dl.soFactList` - a 12px/500 `#6b7789` `dt` over a 13px/600 `#172033` `dd`, the exact tokens the tax identity beside them uses - spanning the strip via `.soOrderTop .soFactWide{grid-column:1/-1}`, because an address is longer than a state name. - User request: refer to Zoho Books and improve the UI and UX of the Create Sales Order and Create Invoice pages, with a screenshot of Zoho's New Sales Order as the reference. Touches `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx`, `src/SalesOrders.jsx`, `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` and `src/sales-orders.css`. Layout only - no stored field, validation rule, workflow or accounting path changed.
- Taken from the reference: the customer and the document details across the top; billing and shipping addresses side by side, each with its own edit affordance; and the running total pinned to the action bar beside the buttons. Not taken: the currency chip (this app posts in INR and the order carries no currency), "View Customer Details" (it would navigate away from an unsaved order), and the REMARKS block (notes and terms already live in Additional details).
- Change 1, the addresses: they left the customer card and became their own full-width card, `div.ivCard.soFormSection.soAddressCard` > `div.soAddressGrid`, two equal columns of `div.soAddressBlock`. Each block's header pairs the label with one labelled `button.soAddressEdit`, and the address is `p.soAddressText` - plain text on the shared `#e4e9f0` card border and 10px radius over `#fbfcfe`, not a boxed field. Measured: two 526px columns instead of two 437px stacked rows. A matching shipping address still collapses to one block spanning the row with the existing note and split action, and the grid folds to one column below 820px.
- Change 2, the place of supply: it now closes the address card, directly under the two addresses it is derived from - the order the original spec asks for. The field, the helper line and the reset link are unchanged; only their card moved. This also removed the 99px panel that had been inflating the customer card.
- Change 3, the customer card: the fact strip is six facts in three columns over two rows - GST treatment, GSTIN and PAN, then contact person, phone and credit limit. All six are read from the customer record; the credit limit reuses the `creditLimit` the over-limit notice already computes.
- Change 4, the pinned total: `SalesOrders.jsx` computes `barTotals` from `calculate(form,config)` - the same engine call the Billing summary uses, so the two can never disagree - and the bar prints `Total amount` and `Total quantity` on the left with the actions on the right. `InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` uses the `preview` it already computes. The block is null until the document can be calculated, so the bar falls back to buttons alone rather than showing a zero. Verified live: the order bar reads `Total amount ₹1,003.00  Total quantity 1  Cancel  Save draft  Save & confirm`, and the invoice bar the same minus the confirm verb.
- Two defects found and fixed during the work. The bar's own `justify-content:flex-end` outranked the new justification on source order, bunching the totals against the buttons at the right edge; the override is now `.soCreatePage .ivFooter.soActionBar` at three classes. And the markup surgery that lifted the addresses left a stray `div>` text node plus a truncated `</p></` in the customer card, both of which rendered as visible text - caught by screenshotting the card at full resolution rather than by any assertion.
- A structural defect in an existing test file was also repaired: three place-of-supply tests added on 2026-09-19 had been inserted inside the attachment-budget test, so they ran as subtests of it. `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` now reports `suites 0` with all 42 tests at the top level.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node --test tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` is **57/57** together (`sales-order-ui` 42/42 with two new cases, `invoice-detail-ui` 15/15). Eight assertions in the order test were rewritten rather than deleted, because they pinned the address markup, the customer caption and the place-of-supply card this change replaced.
- Full sweep actually executed: **644 tests, 633 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18, none of whose files reads anything this change touched. The total grew by exactly the two tests added here.
- Production build actually executed: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed with only Vite's existing large-chunk advisory; `node scripts/prepare-sites-build.mjs` wrote `dist/server/index.js` and `dist/.openai/hosting.json`; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Browser verification actually executed at 1440x950 on both pages. Confirmed: the customer card renders its six-fact strip with real customer data (contact, phone, ₹1,00,000 credit limit) with no stray text; the address card shows Billing and Shipping as two 526px columns each with an Edit, and folds to one spanning block when the addresses match; the place of supply closes that card with `Kerala · Based on customer address`; and the action bar carries the running total on the left with the actions on the right on both pages. The temporary viewport override was reset afterwards.

- User request: on the Sales Order and Invoice creation pages, use the space better and look more professional - the cards felt empty in places and cramped in others. Layout only: no stored field, validation rule, workflow or accounting path changed.
- Measured before, at 1440x950 on the Sales Order page: both cards were 588px tall because `.soOrderTop` used `align-items:stretch`, but the Order details card held only 304px of content, leaving **214px of hollow white space** below its last field. The Customer card was full (19px of slack).
- Fix 1: the row is now `align-items:start` with a `42fr / 58fr` split and an explicit `.soOrderTop>.ivCard{align-self:start}`. Measured after: Customer 540px, details 393px, no interior dead space on either card.
- Fix 2: seven fields in a two-column grid left the last row half empty. `.soOrderTop .soFieldRow4>label:last-child:nth-child(odd){grid-column:1/-1}` spans the odd field, so the card closes on a full-width Due date. Scoped to `@media(min-width:1081px)`, because below 1080px the same seven fields sit on four columns where the seventh is already in the flow.
- Fix 3: the three tax facts were three sparse label-left / value-right lines on a 30px label row. They are one strip now - a 12px muted label over a 13px value in each of three columns - with the two addresses on their own full-width rows beneath a hairline. The facts block went from 308px to 259px and the Customer card from 588px to 540px.
- A defect found while verifying, not by reading the CSS: the compact strip is deliberately **not** scoped to the side-by-side layout. Below 1080px the card is full width and the previous five-track row (`repeat(3,minmax(0,1fr)) repeat(2,minmax(0,2fr))`) squeezed each short fact to about 110px, wrapping "Not recorded" one character per line - visible in the browser at a 1000px viewport. The strip now applies at every width, and the 560px block still folds it to a single column.

- Browser verification actually executed at 1440x950, 1000x950 and 420x900 on both create pages. Desktop: cards 540 / 393 with the fact strip `GST treatment | GSTIN | PAN` and the two addresses beneath. Stacked at 1000px: the strip stays three across on a full-width card instead of wrapping one character per line. Phone at 420px: single column, label over value, `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth`. The Invoice create page measured identically to the Sales Order page (540 / 393). - User request: add a Place of Supply to the Sales Order customer/address section, derive it automatically from the customer's billing/shipping data, show its source, allow a manual override that is never silently overwritten, store it on the order, carry it into the invoice generated from that order, support it on direct invoice creation, drive GST from it, and keep it controlled after posting - reusing existing structures and without duplicating logic. Touches `src/customer-tax.js`, `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx`, `src/sales-orders.css`, `src/sales-order-service.js`, `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` and four test files.
- What already existed, and was therefore reused rather than rebuilt: the stored `place` field on every order and invoice; `calculate(doc, config)` in `src/invoice-engine.js`, which already derives intra/inter-state treatment by comparing the document's place of supply with the company state; `GST_STATE_CODES`; the customer master's `state`, `billing` / `shipping` and structured `billingAddress` / `shippingAddress`; `convertSalesOrder`, which already carried `place`; and the engine's `Only draft invoices can be edited.` guard with the UI's draft-only Edit action. No key was renamed and no stored value was migrated.
- Added: two additive optional fields, `placeSource` (`customer_address` / `shipping_address` / `manual`) and `placeCode` (the GST state code), plus four pure helpers in `src/customer-tax.js` - `placeOfSupplySuggestion`, `findStateInAddress`, `placeOfSupplyStatus` / `placeOfSupplySourceLabel`, and `gstStateCode` (which inverts the existing `GST_STATE_CODES` rather than keeping a second table).
- Derivation order: a distinct shipping address first, then the customer's registered place of supply, then the state named in the billing text. A shipping address that only copies billing is reported as `customer_address`, not `shipping_address`. `findStateInAddress` only accepts a name the app already knows and prefers the longest match, so a city or a country cannot become a place of supply and a future name containing another cannot be shadowed. When nothing yields a state the field stays unselected.
- Never silently overwritten: `applyPlace(next, force)` recalculates only a derived value. A manual choice is preserved across later shipping-address edits, and a stored document carrying a place but no source is treated as manual too, so opening an old order or invoice and editing an address cannot move its tax treatment. The reset link (`Reset to shipping state` / `Reset to customer state`) is the single path allowed to overwrite a manual value.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node --test tests/customer-tax.test.mjs` **19/19** (four new cases), `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` **40/40** (two new cases, three assertions rewritten because they pinned the old markup), `tests/sales-order.test.mjs` **5/5** (its existing field-parity assertion now also covers `placeSource` / `placeCode`). Full sweep: **642 tests, 631 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18, none of whose files reads anything this change touched. The total grew by exactly the six tests added here.
- Production build actually executed: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed with only Vite's existing large-chunk advisory; `node scripts/prepare-sites-build.mjs` wrote `dist/server/index.js` and `dist/.openai/hosting.json`; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Browser verification actually executed against the running dev server at 1440x950, and it exercised every acceptance criterion end to end. **Sales order:** with no customer the field reads `Select Place of Supply`; choosing ABC Retail Pvt Ltd (billing = shipping = MG Road, Kochi) filled it with `Kerala` and `Based on customer address`; the split action plus the shipping editor changed the delivery address to Bengaluru, Karnataka and the value re-derived to `Karnataka` with `Based on shipping address` while the line tax flipped to `IGST 18%`; selecting `Kerala` by hand produced `Manually selected · Reset to shipping state` and the tax flipped back to `CGST 9% + SGST 9%`; a further shipping edit to Chennai, Tamil Nadu left the manual `Kerala` untouched; and the reset link then re-derived `Tamil Nadu` with `Based on shipping address`. **Order to invoice:** saving produced SO-00002 and `Convert to Invoice` produced INV-0004 whose detail states `Place of supply: Tamil Nadu` alongside the Chennai shipping address, with no second selection. **Direct invoice:** `Create invoice` opened with `Select Place of Supply`, and choosing Northstar Services (Bengaluru) filled in `Karnataka` with `Based on customer address`.
- One incidental confirmation worth recording: the engine refused a test fixture that paired `place: 'Karnataka'` with CGST/SGST lines using `Inter-state supplies require IGST, without CGST or SGST.`, which is the existing GST logic correctly following the place of supply rather than the UI re-implementing it.
- Unresolved: nothing. The remaining 11 failures are the long-standing ones in `src/OperationalModules.jsx`, `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx`, `src/AccountWorkspace.jsx` / `src/EnterpriseAccountForm.jsx`, `src/journal-*.css`, `src/SettingsWorkspace.jsx` and `src/WayvidaDatePicker.jsx`.

- User request: give the New sales invoice page left and right padding and style the UI, and fix the overlapping UI issues on the New sales order page. Touches `src/sales-orders.css`, `src/invoice-workspace.css` and the two tests that pin those pages. No stored field, validation rule, posting rule or accounting path changed.
- Defect 1, found by measuring the running page rather than reading the CSS: `.soCreatePage .salesOrderForm{margin:12px 0 0}` and `.ivCreatePage .ivCreateForm{margin:12px 0 0}` set a **margin shorthand**, which resets all four margins. That tied on specificity with the shared `.soCreatePage>:not(.soCreateHead){margin-left/right:clamp(16px,2.2vw,24px)}` gutter (both 0,2,0) and won on source order, so the gutter never applied. Measured on the invoice page at 1440px: `form.ivCreateForm` was `x:270, right:1425, margin-left:0, margin-right:0` - flush against the sidebar and the viewport edge with no padding at all - and because the action bar deliberately bleeds `-24px` to reach the page edges, it was pushed to `x:246, right:1449`, giving the document 24px of horizontal overflow. Both rules now set only `margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0`. Re-measured: form `x:294, right:1401` (24px gutters), action bar `x:270, right:1425`, and `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at both 1440px and 420px.
- Defect 2, the overlap the user reported: `.soCreatePage .soActionBar` was `position:sticky;bottom:0`. Pinned to the viewport bottom it floated a grey band across the Items card and hid the **Billing summary** - including the totals - whenever the form was scrolled to the top, which is exactly where a user looks before saving. Measured `overlap: true` with the summary at `bottom:937` under the bar at `y:886`. The bar is now `position:static` - an in-flow footer at the end of the form - the upward `box-shadow` is dropped, and the container bottom padding went from `88px` (which existed only to clear the pinned bar) to `28px`. Re-measured `overlap: false` on both pages at 1440px and 420px, with the Billing summary heading and its totals visible. At 420px this also removes the two stray footer bands that used to appear mid-page.
- Defect 3, found while verifying at 420px: the shared `.soCreatePage table.ivLineTable{table-layout:fixed;width:100%;min-width:0}` - which deliberately stops the eight-column line table scrolling sideways at desktop widths - squeezed every control to about 35px on a phone, with the item search unreadable. A `@media(max-width:700px)` block now sets `min-width:760px`, so the table falls back to its own horizontal scroll (`.ivScroll` already carries `overflow:auto`) only below that width. Measured at 420px: table 760px inside a 347px wrapper with a working scrollbar, item search 128px (was ~35px), and still no page-level overflow.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node --test tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` is **53 tests, 53 passed, 0 failed**. The assertion pinning `position:sticky` was replaced rather than deleted, and two new guards were added asserting the form rules never set a `margin` shorthand, so the gutter cannot silently disappear again.
- Full sweep actually executed: all `tests/*.test.mjs` under `node --test` reported **636 tests, 625 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures already recorded for 2026-09-18, none of whose files reads anything this change touched.
- Production build actually executed: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed with only Vite's existing large-chunk advisory; `node scripts/prepare-sites-build.mjs` wrote `dist/server/index.js` and `dist/.openai/hosting.json`; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- Browser verification actually executed at 1440x950 and 420x900 against the running dev server. Confirmed on both create pages: 24px left and right padding, no horizontal overflow, the footer as a single in-flow band at the end, the Billing summary and its totals visible rather than covered, and the line table scrolling instead of squeezing at phone width. The temporary viewport override was reset afterwards.
- Not changed: document attachments on the invoice (still absent by design), and the sticky-bar overlap on the other create pages that share the `.itemDialogFooter` / journal sticky-card pattern (Create Item, Create Journal Entry). Those were not part of this request; the same treatment can be applied there if wanted.

Touches `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx`, `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx`, `src/invoice-workspace.css`, `src/sales-orders.css` and the two tests that pin those screens (`tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs`). No stored field, storage key, posting rule, validation rule or accounting path changed.
- What changed: `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx` no longer branches its layout on `kind`. One shared block now renders for both documents - `div.soOrderTop` with the Customer card (searchable picker plus the read-only GST treatment / GSTIN / PAN facts and the two addresses with their labelled Edit) at 40% beside the document-details card at 60%, whose eight fields are one `soFieldRow4` four-column grid (organisation, branch, number, date, reference, place of supply, payment terms, due date). Wording is switched by `docWord` / `docTitle`; the invoice keeps its editable unit input, its Item-master note and its `Save draft` verb, and the order keeps its read-only unit, its credit-limit notice and its shipment fields. The Billing summary head now renders on both documents.
- What the invoice page gained: the full-bleed `div.soCreateHead` head with one 40px square `aria-label="Back to invoices"` button and an edit-only hint; the collapsed `details.ivCard.soMoreCard` Additional details card holding Customer notes and Terms & conditions; the sticky `footer.ivFooter.soActionBar`; and a working-context-seeded organisation and branch, so the page opens exactly as the order page does. The inline `button.ivLink` back link and its `div.ivHeading` block are gone from the create page.

A new `@media(max-width:560px)` block restates the fold for the fact list, the fact rows and `soFieldRow4` at the same class depth. This fixes the pre-existing sales order page at that width too.
