- Dev-server check actually executed: the running Vite server transformed `/src/Customers.jsx` with HTTP 200, so the file parses as JSX; the same check caught a duplicated `div.itemDialogBody` block left by a stale-offset patch, which was removed before the final test run. The source now contains exactly one `itemDialogBody`, four `section.itemSection` blocks and one `details.itemAdvanced`.
- Covered by the new tests: the four-section order plus the More details disclosure; Business / Individual name label and treatment default; GSTIN / UIN visibility and the Get Taxpayer Details wiring; legal and trade name never required; the Tax Exempt / exemption-reason condition; the required searchable place of supply; the shipping-as-billing switch hiding the shipping block; the contact-person repeater with exactly one primary; the document limits and their exact helper text; the deliberate absence of any Customer Portal field; the validation rules; and the stored-field compatibility the invoice and sales-order screens depend on.
- Covered as pure functions: `decodeGstin` accepts a correctly checked GSTIN and reads its state, state code and PAN, normalises case and surrounding space, and reports a distinct reason for an empty number, a wrong length, a wrong shape, a wrong check digit and an unrecognised state code; `gstinChecksum` accepts exactly one check character per 14-character prefix; `composeAddress` / `parseAddress` round-trip and preserve a legacy single-line address; `paymentTermDays` / `daysToPaymentTerm` round-trip every standard term and leave a non-standard period as Custom; `normaliseContacts` guarantees exactly one primary; `contactFromName` converts a legacy contact person; `fileSize` reads bytes, kilobytes and megabytes.
- Follow-up density and simplification pass on the same day, at the user request: Payment & accounting moved inside More details; the visible body is now three `section.itemSection` blocks (Customer details, GST & Tax Details, Billing address) plus the disclosure; Billing and shipping use plain Address 1 / Address 2 inputs, with `line2` added to the stored address and the Address textarea removed; the create grid is three columns (two below 1100px, one below 700px) with a `14px 16px` gap; `.itemTypeField` gained a `0 0 16px` bottom margin so the Customer type choice no longer touches the name row; and the Display name, Tags, Custom fields and Notes inputs were removed along with the duplicated Contact persons heading and its empty-state sentence. The stored `displayName`, `tags`, `notes` and `customFields` values are still carried through `save` untouched, so nothing is lost. Because a required field can now sit inside the collapsed disclosure, the disclosure is controlled through `open={moreOpen}` and a failed save that errors on a hidden field opens More details instead of blocking invisibly. Re-verified after the pass: customer-register-ui 8/8, customer-create-form-ui 18/18, customer-tax 12/12, the full sweep 526 tests with 515 passing and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed with 6,960 modules.
- Third pass on the same day, at the user request, made the GST and tax fields dynamic and moved the taxpayer lookup server-side. Executed and verified: `customer-create-form-ui` **29/29** (11 new tests), `customer-tax` **14/14** (2 new), a new `tests/gst-lookup.test.mjs` **5/5**, and `sites-worker` **6/6** (2 new covering the lookup route). Full sweep: all 83 `tests/*.test.mjs` files reported **546 tests, 535 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, none of which touches Customers, the worker or the lookup client. Production build passed and `scripts/prepare-sites-build.mjs` packaged the worker; `scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- What the new tests actually pin: the per-treatment visibility of GSTIN / UIN, PAN requirement, place of supply, legal and trade name, and the `countryRequired` Overseas path; that the four-value taxability list exists and only Tax Exempt asks for an exemption reason; that the browser module contains no `GST_API_KEY`, `GST_API_URL`, `client_secret`, `apiKey` or `Bearer ` and no external host, and posts only to `/api/gst/lookup`; that the worker reads its key from `env`, sends it upstream in `x-api-key`, never echoes it in the response, never falls through to the asset handler, and answers `400 invalid_gstin`, `503 not_configured`, `502 unavailable` and `405 method_not_allowed`; that a successful lookup fills the editable taxpayer fields and writes the `gstLookup` timestamp/status stamp; that a failed lookup shows the exact sentence "Unable to fetch GST details. Please verify the GSTIN or enter the details manually." while still filling place of supply and PAN locally and never setting a blocking `err.gstin`; that the Website URL input, its pattern and its validation rule are gone while a stored value still round-trips; and that the receivable account and opening balance helper texts carry the business-friendly wording, including that an opening balance does not create a journal entry until posted or confirmed.
- Method note: the worker is executed in Node with a fake `ASSETS` binding and a stubbed `globalThis.fetch`, which is how the upstream request (URL, `x-api-key` header) is asserted without any network access. The route is matched by exact pathname before the asset handler, so the existing "missing API is not the app shell" contract still holds.
- Fourth pass on the same day, at the user request: the contact person's Primary contact checkbox became the `Set as Primary Contact` toggle button. `src/Customers.jsx` no longer renders `<input type="checkbox" checked={contact.primary}>` inside a contact card; it renders `button.customerPrimaryToggle` with `aria-pressed={contact.primary}`, the label `Set as Primary Contact` at rest and `Primary Contact` plus `IconCheck` when active, and the same `setPrimaryContact(current.contacts, contact.id)` handler, so exactly one contact stays primary. `src/customers.css` adds the toggle on the create-page control tokens (36px tall, `#d9e1ec` hairline, 7px radius, 12px/600, `#eef4ff` / `#245fd4` hover, `#245fd9` focus ring, filled `#eef4ff` tint with a `#7aa7f8` border and a 2px ring when active) and drops the now-dead `.customerContactTools .itemCheck` rule. Verified: `tests/customer-create-form-ui.test.mjs` **31/31** with two new tests - one asserting the checkbox is gone, the toggle carries `aria-pressed` and both labels, and it still routes through `setPrimaryContact`; one asserting the stylesheet keeps the control tokens, the focus ring, the active tint and the `space-between` row geometry, and that the dead checkbox rule stays deleted. The customer, lookup and worker suites together are **64/64**, and the production build passed.
- Fifth pass on the same day, at the user request: the status displays were unified on the shared pill. New `src/StatusPill.jsx` over `src/status-pill.css` renders the Chart of Accounts shape (`padding:4px 9px`, `border-radius:14px`, a 7px `#28a26b` dot on `#e8f6ee` / `#287a51` at 12px/550) with `ok` / `neutral` / `warn` / `info` / `violet` / `danger` tones, an unknown tone falling back to green and an empty status rendering nothing. It replaces four bespoke badges: the Customers register and detail (`.customerStatus`, deleted), the Period Lock register cell through `CLOSING_TONES` (`.pc-badge.pc-status.*`, deleted - `.pc-badge` itself stays for unlock-request badges), Inventory Adjustments through `STATUS_TONES`, which also feeds the create and detail head badges so one page never shows two shapes (`.iaBadge` and `.iaHeadSide .iaBadge`, deleted), and a new **Status** column in the Items register between Default tax and Actions (`.itemDisabledBadge` and its rule, deleted). No stored value, status vocabulary or workflow changed. Verified: a new `tests/status-pill-ui.test.mjs` is **7/7**, asserting the component contract (dot plus label, the six tones, the safe fallbacks), that `status-pill.css` still carries the exact tokens `src/account-workspace.css` declares for `.am-status` so the two cannot drift, that each of the four registers uses the pill, and that all four retired badge classes stay deleted while `.am-status` and `.je-status` remain untouched. The full sweep is unchanged at **548 tests, 537 passing** with the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed.
- Sixth pass on the same day, at the user request: the Inventory Adjustments register was condensed from ten columns to six. `REGISTER_COLUMNS` is now `['Reference','Type / Mode of adjustment','Organisation','Items','Status','Actions']`, with four cells carrying two facts each - the voucher number over the date, the adjustment type over `Entry mode · <entryModeLabel>`, the organisation over its branch, and the item count over the signed amount - so the entry mode now reads on the left of the row instead of only in the create form. Reason, adjustment account and Created by left the register because the detail screen already shows them under Adjustment information, and the detail gained an **Entry mode** fact so the field the register surfaces is not hidden on the authoritative screen. The signed amount keeps its `iaUp` / `iaDown` colouring inside the Items cell through new `.iaAmountLine` rules, the dead `column==='Amount'` branch was removed from the header className, and no stored field, filter, search term or export changed. Verified: `tests/inventory-adjustments.test.mjs` is **28/28** with three new tests pinning the merged cells, the left-hand entry mode, the removed standalone columns, that the dropped facts stay reachable on the detail, and the legibility rules. The full sweep is **558 tests with 547 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed. One self-inflicted defect was caught and repaired during the work: a PowerShell slice with a negative range index duplicated lines at the top of `src/InventoryAdjustments.jsx`; `git diff --numstat` confirmed the repaired file diffs to exactly 17 insertions and 12 deletions, and the dev server transforms it with HTTP 200.
- Seventh pass on the same day, at the user request: the Inventory Adjustments row menu now always offers **Edit, Duplicate and Delete** alongside View, Export and the status action, and the detail screen gained a **Preview** button. `ADJUSTMENT_ACTIONS` lists the same set for every status, `ADJUSTMENT_DUTY` maps `Delete` onto the existing `cancel` duty, and one new `actionEntries` builder now feeds both the register menu and the detail menu so they cannot disagree. Because the engine refuses two of the actions once a record has moved on, they render disabled with the reason instead of disappearing - the mechanism the menu already used for a role that lacks a duty - and Delete carries the destructive treatment. The engine gained a matching `delete` command that refuses a posted or adjusted record ("An adjusted adjustment already posted its journal. Create a reversal instead of deleting it."), removes a permitted record from `state.inventoryAdjustments`, and writes an `inventory-adjustment-delete` audit event with `toStatus: 'Deleted'`. The page confirms before deleting. Preview is opt-in state that replaces the detail screen, exactly as Journal Entries opens JournalPreview; `src/AdjustmentPreview.jsx` prints the stored document through the internal-document preview shell (`jp-*` over `.dpPaper`) and reads every figure from `storedLines(row)` without recomputing anything. Verified: `tests/inventory-adjustments.test.mjs` is **35/35** with seven new tests - four exercising the real engine (a draft deletes and the deletion is audited; a pending record deletes before it posts; a posted record is refused with the reversal message while its journal survives; an Accountant may delete because the duty is `cancel`) and three reading the sources (the menu offers all three actions on every status with the disable reasons, the detail head wires the Preview button and clears the preview on both open and back, and the preview renders an accessible dialog that never calls `computeLine`). The full sweep is **565 tests with 554 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed.
- Eighth pass on the same day, at the user request: the Sales Orders register was rebuilt. Removed: the blue `span.soEyebrow` reading "Sales", the `{visible.length} ... 'order' : 'orders'` record count, and the Expected shipment column (it became a filter). The grid is now Order (SO number over order date), Customer, Branch & Organisation, Amount, Status, Payment status, Created by, Actions - eight columns with refreshed widths - and both status cells render the shared pill, so the bespoke `.soStatus*` badges and rules were deleted. Payment status is derived from the linked invoice through the engine's `paymentStatus(state, invoice)`, and an uninvoiced order reads "Not invoiced" rather than "Unpaid". The toolbar gained one `details.soFiltersMore` Filters disclosure with Payment status, Customer, Organisation, Branch, Created by, Order date from/to and Expected shipment from/to, a Clear filters row and an `activeFilters` count. Because an order stored no organisation, branch or creator, save now stamps them from `getCurrentOrganizationContext()` and the acting role, written as `form.x||stamped` so a re-save never overwrites a stored value. Verified: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **9/9**, rewritten to pin the eight columns, the merged Order and Branch cells, the removal of the eyebrow, the count and the Expected shipment column, the shared pill tones for both status columns, the invoice-derived payment status, every filter clause, and the stamping - the old assertions on `soStatus soStatus` and `.soStatusConfirmed` were replaced because those badges are gone. The full sweep is **572 tests with 561 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed.
- Not done, and deliberately: the request also named **Fulfilment** and **Payment** filters. A sales order stores neither a fulfilment state nor a payment mode, and `src/SalesOrderActions.jsx` / `sales-order-service.js` define no such vocabulary, so building those two filters would have produced controls that can never match. They are reported rather than invented.

The shell gives up its padding and max-width for this state (`.app main:has(.soCreatePage)`, `.app main>.itemsPage.salesOrdersPage>.soCreatePage`) and `.soCreatePage>:not(.soCreateHead)` hands the `clamp(16px,2.2vw,24px)` gutter back to every other child, narrowing to `12px` at the 760px breakpoint the Organisation / Branch bar uses. Selecting a customer now renders `div.soCustomerRef` from `SalesDocumentFields` when `kind==='order'`: a billing card and a shipping card, each with a 30px pencil carrying an `aria-label` that names the address, plus a card stating the customer's GST treatment and GSTIN. The pencil opens `div.soAddressLayer` / `section.soAddressDialog` (the account-drawer fixed-layer recipe, `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, backdrop and close button) which edits `form[addressEdit]` - this order's copy only - and says so in a note. The invoice branch of the same component is byte-identical to before. Verified: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **12/12** with three new tests covering the head markup, geometry and shell rules; the customer panel and its `kind==='order'` scoping; and the editor's dialog contract, backdrop, close, textarea binding and "order only" note. The full sweep is **575 tests with 564 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed.
- Tenth pass on the same day, at the user request, fixing the Sales Orders filter panel that a screenshot showed as crowded. **This pass was verified by rendering the page**, which earlier passes could not do: headless Chrome (153.0.8010.47) was launched with `--remote-debugging-port=9333` against the running dev server and driven over the DevTools protocol from `tmp/so-filters-check.mjs` and `tmp/so-create-check2.mjs`, using Node 24's global `WebSocket` plus `Runtime.evaluate`, `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` and `Page.captureScreenshot`. Three defects were found and fixed, and two of them were invisible in the source:
  1. Every filter field was boxed with a centred caption. The cause was not the panel: `src/items.css` styles `.itemsTools label` as the register search pill (`border:1px solid #d0d5dd`, `border-radius:6px`, `padding:0 10px`, `width:340px`, `align-items:center`), and the panel lives inside that same toolbar. `.salesOrdersPage .soFiltersPanel label` now resets it - measured after the fix: `border 0px`, `radius 0px`, `padding 0px`, `width 218`, `alignItems stretch`, `textAlign start`.
  2. The panel was clipped at the register card because the shared `.itemsCard` is `overflow:hidden`. `.soListCard` is now `overflow:visible`, the same fix the Inventory Adjustments and Budgets cards carry. Measured: the panel runs to y=565 while the card ends at y=395, and it renders.
  3. The address editor's backdrop computed to `rgb(255,255,255)` - solid white, hiding the page - because `.invoiceWorkspace button` (specificity 0-1-1) beats a single-class component rule and the editor renders inside `.invoiceWorkspace.soCreatePage`. The same rule was also giving the pencil and close buttons a 36px min-height and 9px 13px padding instead of their 30px and 32px squares. The editor's controls are now prefixed with `.soCreatePage` and the backdrop pins `background-color:rgba(15,23,42,.28)!important`, exactly as `src/account-create-drawer.css` already had to.
- Also found by rendering: two `<header>` elements used as address-card and dialog headings were painted as the fixed 58px application bar by `src/styles.css` `header{position:fixed;…}`, leaving a white strip across the top of the viewport and blank cards. Both are now `div`s with their own classes; a page-level check confirms exactly one `<header>` remains on the page - the app's own bar.
- Measured and confirmed after the fixes: the create head is flush under the working-context bar (`orgContextBottom 105`, `headTop 105`, `gap 0`), the back button is 40x40 at x=294 - the same 270px sidebar plus 24px padding the Organisation / Branch bar uses - and selecting a customer renders `div.soCustomerRef` at 1109px in three 361px columns with both address cards carrying a pencil and the GST card reading `Not recorded` / `Not registered` for the seeded customer, which has no tax identity. The editor opens with `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, the title `Edit billing address`, the note naming the customer and a textarea.
- Automated validation in the same pass: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **14/14**, with two new guards - one asserting the panel is never a bare `<header>` and that the card and dialog heads are `div`s styled through their own classes, and one asserting the two specificity fixes stay in place (the pencil and close reset `min-height:0`, the backdrop keeps `background-color:…!important`, and the file the panel must beat really does carry `.invoiceWorkspace button{…background:#fff}`). The full sweep is **577 tests with 566 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build passed.
- Eleventh pass on the same day, at the user request: the create-state subtitle was removed and the customer panel was re-laid out. `<small className="soHeadHint">` now renders only while editing (`{form.id&&…Editing {form.number}}`) and the sentence "Order only — generate an invoice when ready. No accounting entries are posted." is gone entirely; measured in headless Chrome, the head dropped from 77px to 58px and is still flush under the working-context bar (gap 0), which also matches the Customers and Items heads, where the hint is edit-only. `div.soCustomerRef` changed from three narrow columns to two wide address columns over one full-width tax strip, and the strip gained **PAN** alongside GST treatment and GSTIN. Measured at 1440px: two address cards of 549x82 and a 1109x60 strip, a 154px panel in total, versus the previous single 104px row of three cramped columns that left a one-line address in a mostly empty box; the addresses fold to one column below 860px. - Twelfth pass on the same day, at the user request: the order header was put on one row and the address cards were compacted. `.soCreatePage .ivFields` moved from three columns to four (folding to two below 1180px, one below 640px), so Sales order number, Sales order date, Customer and Reference number share a single row, and the new `.soPlaceField` spans two columns so the second row of place of supply / payment terms / due date also fills exactly rather than ending in a gap. The address cards no longer print one address part per line: a new `inlineAddress` helper in `SalesDocumentFields` splits the stored multi-line value, trims it and joins it with `, `, and the `white-space:pre-line` rule is gone, so a five-part address reads as one or two lines at the card's width. Padding dropped to `10px 12px` and the pencil to a 26px square. Rendered and measured at 1440px in headless Chrome (`tmp/so-create-check4.mjs`, screenshot `tmp/so-create4.png`): four header cells at y=186, 265px wide each, and each address card 549x71 with the tax strip 1109x60. - Thirteenth pass on the same day, at the user request: the create page's shell padding was brought onto the Create Item standard. Both pages were measured in headless Chrome side by side (`tmp/so-padding-compare.mjs`) and three defects showed up, none of them visible in the source. First, the head bar stopped 15px short of the right edge (1425 instead of 1440) because the shell keeps a `scrollbar-gutter` for the registers while a create page scrolls in the document; `body:has(.soCreatePage) .app>main` now releases it (`height:auto;max-height:none;overflow:visible;scrollbar-gutter:auto`) beside the body and `.app` rules the other create pages already carry. Second, the form ran 24px past the content edge (right 1449 against main's 1440) because `.soCreatePage .salesOrderForm` was `width:100%` while the shared gutter rule adds `margin-left` and `margin-right` - a 100%-wide block plus margins overflows - so it is now `width:auto` and fills the remaining width the way `.itemDialogBody` does. Third, it sat flush against the head bar where the other create pages leave a gap, so it now carries `margin-top:12px`. Measured after the change, the two pages agree exactly: Create Item head 270..1440 at y=105..163 with its body 294..1416 at y=175 (`margin:12px 24px 0`, 1122px wide), and Create Sales Order head 270..1440 at y=105..163 with its form 294..1416 at y=175 (`margin:12px 24px 0`, 1122px wide) - previously 1425, 1449 and y=163 respectively. Verified: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **17/17** with one new test pinning all four shell rules and one assertion updated from the old `.app main:has(…)` selector to the `body:has(…)` form; the full sweep is **580 tests with 569 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build and Sites packaging passed. Screenshot: `tmp/so-create4.png`.
- Fourteenth pass on the same day, two defects the operator reported on the Create Sales Order page. First, **the page would not scroll**. `html,body,#root{height:100%;overflow:hidden}` in `src/ui-quality-polish.css` makes the shell a fixed-height app whose scroll port is `main`, so a create page must release `html`, `body` and `#root`; the port had copied the Create Item page's `body:has(...)` and `body:has(...) #root` selectors but dropped the leading `html:has(...)`. Measured: the document reported a 1145px scrollHeight against a 700px viewport, `window.scrollBy` moved it, but a **trusted** CDP wheel at the head, a field, an address card, the table and the item scroll wrapper each moved it **0px**. Adding `html:has(.soCreatePage)` to that selector list, exactly as `src/items.css` carries it, fixed it - the same wheel now moves the page 0 to 300px and `html` computes `height:1144.53px;overflow-y:auto`, matching the Create Item page. Two methods notes recorded for the next session: a synthetic `dispatchEvent(new WheelEvent(...))` never scrolls and proves nothing, so the wheel must be sent as a trusted `Input.dispatchMouseEvent{type:'mouseWheel'}`; and comparing the computed `height`/`overflow-y` of `html`, `body`, `.app` and `main` between a working page and the broken one located the cause faster than reading the stylesheets. Second, **the order items Tax cell stacked two controls**. `.ivLineTax{display:flex;flex-direction:column}` put the rate selector above the price-tax treatment, so every order line was 116px tall in a table where every other cell is 40px. `.soCreatePage .ivLineTax` is now a 242px grid with both controls side by side at 40px, the rate summary truncating rather than widening the table; the row dropped to **69px** and the table to 1154px, which is its own `min-width:1150px` floor rather than anything the tax cell added. The invoice page keeps the stacked `.ivLineTax` from `src/sales-document-tax.css`, untouched. Verified: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **19/19** with two new tests, one pinning the `html:has(...)` selector and its absence from the port, one pinning the 242px grid, the truncating summary, the 40px control and that the shared stacked rule is still what the invoice page uses; the full sweep is **582 tests with 571 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build and Sites packaging passed. Screenshots: `tmp/so-items-tax2.png`.
- Fifteenth pass on the same day, two more points on the Create Sales Order page. The head title rendered at **23px** instead of 16px: `src/invoice-workspace.css` styles the bare elements inside the workspace (`h2{font-size:23px;margin:0 0 7px}`, and `h3`, `small`, `textarea`) and that sheet is imported **after** `src/sales-orders.css`, so its one-class-one-element rules beat a single-class component rule. Prefixing the head title, head hint, dialog title and dialog textarea with `.soCreatePage` fixed it - measured in headless Chrome, the Create Item head title and the Sales Order head title now both compute `16px`, weight 650 (the same shape of defect as the `.invoiceWorkspace button` backdrop fix a few passes earlier, and now recorded as a general rule in `UI-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`). Also, **matching billing and shipping addresses printed twice**: the panel now renders one `Billing & shipping address` card with a `Shipping uses the same address.` note while the two match, and only the two separate cards with their pencils when they differ. Because the single card's editor writes both stored fields, a matching pair could no longer be made different, so a `Use a different shipping address` action splits them, remembered per customer so choosing another customer returns to the single card. Verified by rendering all three states in headless Chrome (`tmp/so-address-check3.mjs`): same gives 1 card labelled `Billing & shipping address` with the note and the split action, split gives 2 cards with pencils, and editing shipping gives 2 cards reading `MG Road, Kochi` and `99 Beach Road, Fort Kochi`. The tax rate column was also sized to its own label after the intra-state summary `CGST 9% + SGST 9%` measured 151px in a 134px column: the cell is now 284px with `grid-template-columns:auto minmax(96px,1fr)` and measures 213px needed against 213px available, so nothing truncates. Verified: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **21/21** with two new tests; the full sweep is **584 tests with 573 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build and Sites packaging passed. Screenshots: `tmp/so-address-same.png`, `tmp/so-address-different.png`, `tmp/so-items-tax3.png`.
- Sixteenth pass on the same day, three points on the Create Sales Order page. The order items table scrolled sideways: it is `min-width:980px` but its **content** measured 1196px inside a 1061px wrapper, and shaving padding could never be permanent because the em dash standing in for an empty amount is far narrower than a real figure. `.soCreatePage .ivLineTable` is now `table-layout:fixed;width:100%;min-width:0` with percentage columns (Item 19.5, Quantity 7.5, Unit 6.5, Rate 8, Discount 13.5, Tax 30.5, Amount 10.5, Delete 4), which cannot exceed the wrapper whatever the content; the two shared widgets that were wider than their columns - `.ivItemSearch{width:230px}` and `.ivTaxSelect{width:215px}` in `src/invoice-workspace.css` - are sized to their columns here (185px and `auto`). Measured after: **table overflow 0px**, all eight cells unclipped, and the Tax cell at 311px resolving to a 167px rate summary reading `CGST 9% + SGST 9%` beside a 137px treatment select reading `Tax exclusive`. The first two attempts at this were instructive and are recorded: a fixed tax-cell width of 284px gave the rate summary only 99px and clipped it, because `grid-template-columns:auto minmax(96px,1fr)` lets the `1fr` column take the space - `max-content` on the first column and `width:100%` on the cell fixed it. Second, the section was tightened: card padding 22px to 16px, cells 14px 12px to 10px 8px, the gap between cards to 14px and under the Order items heading to 12px - the card measured 312px tall before and 267px after. Third, the address card's edit affordance became a labelled `Edit` button (66x30) instead of a bare 26px pencil icon. Verified: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **23/23** with two new tests; the full sweep is **586 tests with 575 passing** and the same 11 pre-existing unrelated failures, and the production build and Sites packaging passed. Screenshot: `tmp/so-final.png`.
- Seventeenth pass on the same day, at the user request, on the Create / Edit Sales Order order-items line and the order's scope. The **Unit** column is now read-only text taken from the selected item (`span.soUnitValue`) instead of a second editable input; that is deliberately the `kind==='order'` branch only, so the Invoice create page - which shares `SalesDocumentFields` and whose engine refuses a line with no unit - kept its editable unit input, and its scope check never fires because it passes no `organisations`; the **Discount** number yields so the `%` / `₹` selector is visible inside its own cell; the remove-line action is a 32px red destructive target; and the order now carries a required **Organisation \*** and **Branch \*** picker validated against the Item master through new `itemScopeError` / `orderScopeError` helpers. Automated validation actually executed: `node --test tests/sales-order-scope.test.mjs tests/sales-order.test.mjs tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **38 tests, 38 passed, 0 failed**, with **8** new pure-function cases in `tests/sales-order-scope.test.mjs` and **5** new UI cases (`the order line prints the unit…`, `the discount number yields…`, `the remove line action is a red, padded target`, `the create page picks the organisation and branch…`, `an order cannot be saved against a scope the item is not set up for`). Two pre-existing assertions were updated rather than deleted: the column percentages in `the order items table never scrolls sideways` and the `organisation-context.js` import line in `the working context and the acting role are stamped on save`.
- Eighteenth pass on the same day, at the user request, on the Create / Edit Sales Order page layout. The page is now **one section per job** - Customer, Order details, Order items, Billing summary, then the collapsed Additional details - the customer's GST treatment / GSTIN / PAN / addresses are rendered as a **read-only fact list** instead of input-like boxes, Place of supply and the two address Edit buttons are the row's only controls, the action bar is pinned, and attachments upload through a drag-and-drop tile with a file list. Automated validation actually executed: `node --test tests/sales-order-scope.test.mjs tests/sales-order.test.mjs tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` is **42 tests, 42 passed, 0 failed** after `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` was updated (it is **32/32** on its own, four tests of it new: the section order, the billing summary block, the pinned action bar and the drop-zone upload). Eight assertions in that file were rewritten rather than deleted, because the markup they pinned (`soAddressCard` / `soGstCard` / `soAddressCardHead` / the two-column `soCustomerRef`) no longer exists.
- Nineteenth pass on the same day, at the user request, on the same page: **Place of supply left the read-only customer panel** and the crowded row was reformatted. It is an editable field, so it now sits in the Order details section, which holds six fields on one three-column grid and therefore renders as two rows of three (Sales order number, Sales order date, Reference number / Place of supply, Payment terms, Due date). The customer panel is left purely read-only - GST treatment, GSTIN, PAN and the two addresses on three narrow tracks plus two wider ones - and every label now occupies the same 30px line (`dt{display:flex;align-items:center;min-height:30px}`), because an address label sits in a row that also carries its Edit button and was dropping below the plain values beside it, which is what made the row look broken in the screenshot the user supplied. The five-column field row (`.soFieldRow5`) and the `.soFactField` select styling are deleted rather than left as dead rules.
- Twentieth pass on the same day, at the user request, on the Create / Edit Sales Order page: **Organisation and Branch moved into the Order details section**, and the **customer is now chosen through a searchable picker with a search icon** instead of a native select. Order details holds eight fields on one four-column grid (`.soFieldRow4`), which renders as two complete rows of four - Organisation, Branch, Sales order number, Sales order date / Reference number, Place of supply, Payment terms, Due date - and the Customer section is left as the picker plus the read-only facts. Rather than hand-rolling a second dropdown, the picker the customer form already used for place of supply was extracted into a shared component: `src/SearchSelect.jsx` over `src/search-select.css`, with `src/Customers.jsx` importing it and `src/customers.css` losing the rules rather than keeping a second copy of them.
- Twenty-first pass on the same day, at the user request: the Create / Edit Sales Order page now opens on a **40/60 two-column row** - the Customer card on the left, the Order details card on the right - instead of two full-width bands. Inside the 40% column the customer's read-only facts changed shape from five across to **one fact per line** (label left, value right, a hairline between rows), with the two address facts kept stacked because their value is long and their row carries the Edit action; the right column pairs the eight order fields **two-by-two** instead of four across. Below 1080px the two cards stack into one column and both the facts and the order fields return to their wide-layout shapes.
- Twenty-second pass on the same day, at the user request: the Customer and Order details cards on the Create / Edit Sales Order page are now the **same height**. The row was already a 40/60 grid, but `align-items:start` let each card keep its own height (measured 446px against 432px), so it became `align-items:stretch`. That alone was not enough, and measuring found why: a grid item stretches its **margin box**, and the second card carried the stacked-card `margin-top:14px`, which was therefore subtracted from its border box - exactly the 14px gap that remained. `.soOrderTop>.ivCard+.ivCard.ivCard{margin-top:0}` drops the margin inside the row; the extra class is deliberate, because `.soCreatePage .salesOrderForm .ivCard+.ivCard` ties on class count and would otherwise win by order.
- Twenty-third pass on the same day, three points on the Create / Edit Sales Order page at the user request. The `Billing summary` heading now sits **directly above the totals card**: the block is `width:min(420px,100%);margin-left:auto`, the same box as the panel, where it previously spanned the card and left the heading at the far left with the figures orphaned on the right - measured after the change, the heading and the totals panel both run 964..1384. The `Item tax and price treatment are filled from the Item master. You can change them on this document.` note was **removed from the order page** and now renders for `kind!=='order'` only, so the invoice page keeps it. And the customer panel now shows the **billing address only**, with the labelled Edit as its single control: a matching shipping address is stated as `Shipping address is the same.` beside the split action rather than printed twice, and a separate Shipping address row appears only when the two differ.
- Twenty-fourth pass on the same day, closing the sales-order gaps the previous audit listed. **Payment terms** are now one vocabulary: the order's select is built from `PAYMENT_TERM_DAYS` plus a Custom row, and a new `customerTermDays()` replaces `c?.days||'30'` - a Due-on-Receipt customer stores `days:'0'` and `0||30` had been turning them into Net 30 on the order and the invoice. **Expected shipment prints** (`doc.expectedDate||doc.shipment`), and `delivery` and `salesperson` joined it, so three write-only fields are now visible. **Attachments** declare and enforce 10 files / 1 MB each / 2 MB total with copy built from the same constant, and a full store reports an actionable sentence. The order's **organisation and branch** now travel to the invoice, the **customer's receivable ledger** is carried on the order and honoured by `command('post')`, a **legacy TDS/TCS** order is refused at conversion instead of silently dropping it, the **credit limit** is stated as a warning, the register gained **Duplicate / Export / Delete** (delete guarded behind a confirm and disabled with its reason when an invoice exists), and every write leaves an **audit trail** shown as History in Additional details.
- Twenty-fifth pass on the same day, at the user request: the **invoice detail screen** was rebuilt. It opened with a breadcrumb strip, a blue `SALES INVOICE` eyebrow, a four-tile summary row and a five-tab strip across four separate blocks; it now opens with the arrow-led full-bleed head (`Back` + `Invoice details` + the document number) the other detail pages use, and everything that identifies the document sits in **one card** - identity row (number, lifecycle pill, customer, action row), the four figures, the tab row and the tab body. **Items and Payments stopped being tabs** and became sections of Overview, leaving **Overview / Accounting / Activity**. The action row keeps the lifecycle buttons and the primary Receive payment; Preview & print, Send or share invoice, Accounting details, View journal, View ledger, **Duplicate** and the destructive **Cancel invoice** moved into the More actions menu. `InvoiceOverview` was rebuilt as labelled panels - Organisation & branch, Customer, Items, Invoice information, then Amount summary / Payment summary / Accounting summary / Related documents - and the blue eyebrow, the breadcrumb and their CSS are deleted.
- Twenty-sixth pass on the same day, at the user request: the invoice detail page's tab row and padding. The three tabs were `display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(3,minmax(0,1fr))`, so they sat ~90px apart across the card and read as three separate controls; the row is now the module's own left-aligned underline row (`display:flex;align-items:center;gap:0;height:42px;padding:0 12px`, buttons `flex:0 0 auto` on a 12px gutter) - the same shape the Item Details row uses. Padding was reduced throughout the detail card: identity `18px 22px 16px` -> `14px 18px 12px`, figures `14px 22px` -> `11px 18px`, overview page `18px`/`16px` gap -> `14px`/`12px`, panels and cards `18px 20px` / `20px 22px` -> `14px 16px`, fact list `14px 22px` -> `12px 18px`, the tab body `22px` -> `18px`, and the per-card `.ivOverviewCard .ivSectionTitle` override was replaced by one rule for every section title in the card (`.ivDetailSheet .ivSectionTitle{padding-bottom:11px;margin-bottom:14px}`).
- Twenty-seventh pass on the same day, at the user request: the **Overview** tab was rebuilt to the layout the user supplied. It now reads as the invoice itself in five blocks - **Customer** (name, `GSTIN: ...`, one address line), **Invoice summary** (`ivAmountLines`: Subtotal, Discount negative, GST as a single line, Round off, then a rule and Total / Paid / Balance), **Items** (Item / Qty / Rate / **Tax rate** / Amount), **Payments** (count badge plus one row per payment or allocation: date, method or the receipt it came from, amount) and **More details**, a collapsed `details.ivOverviewMore` holding Order number and status, Invoice date, Due date, Place of supply, Salesperson, Organisation, Branch, Notes and Terms & conditions. The standalone Organisation & branch and Invoice information panels and the Amount summary / Payment summary / Accounting summary / Related documents cards were removed as unwanted or repeated, and the now-unused `totals` renderer and its prop went with them. The linked sales order is read once so More details can state the order number with **its** status and the order's salesperson, rather than the invoice inventing either.
- Twenty-eighth pass on the same day, at the user request: the Overview tab's summary moved **inside the Items panel, right-aligned under the table** (`section.ivInvoiceSummary`, `width:min(420px,100%)`, `margin-left:auto`, a hairline above it), so the totals close the item list the way the document itself reads; the Customer panel now states the full master record - Customer, Customer id, Type, Contact person, Email, Phone, GSTIN, PAN, GST treatment and Place of supply on the three-column grid, plus **Billing address** and **Shipping address** as full-width rows, with a matching pair shown as `Same as the billing address` rather than printed twice; and every status on the page now renders the shared `StatusPill` through new `INVOICE_TONES` / `PAYMENT_TONES` maps, so the invoice reads like the registers. The bespoke `ivStatus` badge and the coloured `ivStatusText` are deleted, and an address falls back through `composeAddressText()` to the structured `billingAddress` / `shippingAddress` halves when the invoice holds no composed string.
- Twenty-ninth pass on the same day, at the user request. The **Payments** section left the Overview and is now **its own tab between Overview and Accounting** (tabs: Overview / Payments / Accounting / Activity), rendering the rows from a new pure `invoicePaymentRows(invoice, db)` that the Overview's `Paid` figure also uses, so the two cannot disagree. The figures row now leads with **Order number and status** - the linked sales order's number and its own status - instead of repeating the invoice status that the header pill already states, and that fact was removed from More details; the other three figures are unchanged. The Customer panel's descriptive subtitle ("Billed party, tax identity and both addresses") is gone. And the two status pills in the figures row were being **stretched to the full width of their tile** because a pill is a column flex item: `.ivDetailStats>span` now carries `align-items:flex-start`, so each pill keeps its own size.
- Thirtieth pass on the same day, at the user request: the invoice Overview now follows the **Item Details** page's style and structure. `div.ivOverviewGrid` puts the **Customer** and **More details** sections side by side at half width each (measured 531.5px / 531.5px), and each is rendered with the Item Details classes - `itemDetailCard` (1px `#e6ebf2`, radius 10px), `itemDetailSectionTitle` (a `#f9fbfd` strip, `12px 16px`, icon + h2, hairline underneath) and a two-column `itemDetailFacts` grid (`dt` `rgb(107,119,137)` 12px, `dd` `rgb(23,32,51)` 13px - the Item Details tokens), with the billing and shipping addresses spanning the grid because they wrap. More details is a **section card beside the customer** now rather than a disclosure, the Items card follows at full width with the right-aligned Invoice summary inside it, and the tab row is the Item Details row itself, shared by class (`nav.itemDetailTabs.ivDetailTabs`, 46px, `0 4px`, hairline top, `aria-current` on the active tab). The columns stack to one below 900px, where the fact grid also folds to one column.
- Thirty-first pass on the same day, at the user request: the Invoice Details tab row now matches the **Item Details** row exactly. The markup already carried both classes (`nav.itemDetailTabs.ivDetailTabs`); what was missing were the values Item Details' own rule sets and the invoice copy did not - `justify-content:center`, `border-radius:0` (the host sheet's `.invoiceWorkspace button` was giving the tabs a 6px radius), `white-space:nowrap` and the shared transition - and the two states that only existed on the Item Details sheet: the `#f5f8fd` hover tint and the count pill. `.ivDetailSheet .itemDetailTabs` now restates the row (46px, `0 4px`, `#e6ebf2` top hairline) and `.ivDetailSheet .itemDetailTabs button` restates the tab (`0 12px`, square, 13px/600, centred, transparent, 2px `#3478f6` underline when active) plus `:hover`, `.active`, `:focus-visible`, the `span` count pill and the 520px equal-cell fallback. The scoped selector is the point: `.invoiceWorkspace button` is class-plus-element and the two sheets tie on specificity, so whichever loads last would decide how the tabs look.
- Measured side by side in headless Chrome - the Item Details row (`/src/items.css`) against the invoice row - every compared value agrees: row `display:flex`, `height:46px`, `padding:0px 4px`, `gap:0px`, `border-top:1px rgb(230,235,242)`, `border-radius:0px`; tab `padding:0px 12px`, `height:45px`, `border-radius:0px`, `justify-content:center`, `background:transparent`, `color:rgb(36,95,217)`, `font:13px/600`, `border-bottom:2px rgb(52,120,246)`. Two differences are deliberate and documented: the row is `overflow:auto` where Item Details uses `hidden` (four tabs must never be clipped) and the tab carries `min-height:0` to neutralise the host sheet, which Item Details never has to do.

- Two display defects were found and fixed by rendering rather than by reading. The Items **Rate** printed as `₹10.00` for a ₹1,000 line, because `money()` takes paise and the stored `line.rate` is the entered rupee string - it is now `money(Math.round(Number(l.rate||0)*100))`. And the Customer block printed two address lines (`Kerala` beside `MG Road, Kochi`) because the structured `billingAddress.city` is empty on the seeded record while the composed billing string is not; it now prints one line - the structured city and state when the master has them, otherwise the composed address.

## Customer create page wheel scrolling fixed - 2026-09-17

- User report: scrolling on the customer creation page was not working properly. Touches `src/items.css` (one declaration plus its reason), `tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` (new contract) and the transfer documents. No markup, storage, validation or accounting change.
- Reproduced against the running dev server in headless Chrome over the DevTools protocol, because the page had to be measured rather than reasoned about: the register list, the Customer Details page and the create page were each walked with a synthetic 300px wheel at several pointer positions.
- Root cause: the create page body card `div.itemDialogBody` was a **wheel trap**. `src/items.css` gives `.itemCreatePage .itemDialogBody` `overflow:hidden` (it clips the section hairlines to the card radius), which makes the card a scroll container, and `src/ui-system.css` declares `.itemDialogBody{overscroll-behavior:contain}`, which stops the wheel chaining out of it. The card has nothing to scroll (`scrollHeight` equal to `clientHeight`), so the gesture was consumed and discarded. Every wheel position whose hit test landed inside the card moved the page **0px** - over a label, a select, an input or a section - while the same wheel over `div.itemDialogHead`, the fixed `footer.itemDialogFooter` or the page gutter moved it the full 300px.
- Why it surfaced now: the trap exists on the Items create page too, but that form is 738px tall against a 1041px Customers form, so at a normal window height there was almost nothing to scroll and the dead zone was invisible. Converting the Customers form from a modal into a page made the form taller than the viewport, which exposed it.
- Fix: `src/items.css` now declares `overscroll-behavior:auto` on `.itemCreatePage .itemDialogBody`, scoped so the dialog surfaces that legitimately contain their overscroll keep `contain`, with the reason recorded beside the rule. The document is the only scroll surface on a create page, so the wheel must chain to it.
- Verified after the fix, same harness, 1440x600 viewport: a single 300px wheel over the form now moves the page 300px at every tested position (y=80, 160, 240, 320, 400, 480, 560), the page reaches its maximum scroll (566px) and the last form field is clear of the fixed action bar (last input bottom 475 against footer top 526).
- Regression checks in the same sweep: the Items create page still scrolls to its maximum (263px), and the Customer Details page still scrolls inside `main` (`main.scrollTop` 287), so the two screens' different scroll surfaces are unchanged.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` is **10/10** with the new `the create page body never traps the wheel` contract, which pins `overscroll-behavior:auto` on the page body, forbids `contain` there and requires the reason to stay recorded; `node tests/customer-register-ui.test.mjs` is 8/8.
- Full sweep actually executed: `node --test "tests/**/*.test.mjs"` reported **496 tests, 485 passed, 11 failed** - one test more than the run recorded in the section above, which is this fix's new wheel-chaining contract - and the 11 failures are the same pre-existing ones in the account, journal, settings, period-lock, operational-module and date-picker suites.
- Production build actually executed: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed with 6,959 modules and the existing bundle-size advisory, emitting `dist/client/assets/index-Buknbl28.css` and `dist/client/assets/index-DAJvddTy.js`; the CSS hash changed from the create-page checkpoint because this fix adds a real declaration, and the build recorded in the section above is superseded by this run.
- Method note for the next session: the harness that found this is a headless Chrome started with `--remote-debugging-port` plus a small WebSocket DevTools-protocol client in `tmp/`, driving `Page.navigate`, `Runtime.evaluate` and `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` wheel events. `Input.synthesizeScrollGesture` and plain `window.scrollTo` are not substitutes - the former is ignored in headless and the latter bypasses wheel hit testing entirely, which is exactly what hid this bug. The Chrome profile must live outside the repository, because a profile inside the tree makes the Vite file watcher die with `EBUSY ... Cookies` and takes the dev server down mid-run.
- Not verified: any painted review of the page in a real browser window, and trackpad behaviour on macOS/Windows hardware.
## Create Customer moved from a popup to a full page - 2026-09-17

- User request: the Create Customer form was a popup, so it must become a page. Touches `src/Customers.jsx`, the stale header comment in `src/items.css`, `tests/customer-register-ui.test.mjs`, the now-false prose in `tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs`, and the transfer documents. No storage key, field, validation rule, posting path or calculation changed.
- Change: the form is no longer `div.itemOverlay` > `form.itemDialog`. It is `form.itemCreatePage`, rendered as a direct child of the register `section.itemsPage.customersPage`, on the Create Item shell. `div.itemDialogHead` carries one 40px square `button.itemBack` beside `div.itemHeadText` (the `h2` reads `Create Customer` or `Edit Customer`, plus a `span.itemHeadHint` naming the customer while editing), `div.itemDialogBody` is the single card holding three `section.itemSection` blocks labelled by plain `div.itemSectionTitle` elements, and `footer.itemDialogFooter` is the shared action bar. The `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` attributes, the focus trap and the Escape-to-close handler went with the overlay; the back button and Cancel both return to the register.
- No new CSS was required: `src/items.css` already hides the register for any `.itemsPage` containing `.itemCreatePage` (`.itemsPage:has(.itemCreatePage)>.itemsHeading,.itemsPage:has(.itemCreatePage)>.itemsCard{display:none}`), gives the flow the full-bleed document scroll surface and hands every non-head child the `clamp(16px,2.2vw,24px)` gutter. The `.itemDialogBody` / `.itemDialogFooter` classes keep their dialog meaning everywhere else because each rule is scoped to `.itemCreatePage`, which is why the shared stylesheet needed only its stale comment corrected.
- Deliberate consequence: `.itemOverlay` and `.itemDialog` now have no JSX user. The rules were left in place rather than deleted, because `src/ui-system.css` groups `.itemDialog`, `.itemDialogBody` and `.itemDialogFooter` with the other dialog surfaces and because `tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` pins those shared declarations. Only that test's now-false wording ("the Customers page still uses" / "the Customers dialog still shares") was corrected; its assertions are untouched. Reported, not fixed: the unused overlay and dialog-box rules are dead CSS in `src/items.css`.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node tests/customer-register-ui.test.mjs` is **8/8**, including the rewritten `create and edit customer is a full page, not a modal popup` contract; `node tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` is **9/9**.
- Full sweep actually executed: `node --test "tests/**/*.test.mjs"` reported **495 tests, 484 passed, 11 failed** - the same 11 pre-existing failures in the account, journal, settings, period-lock, operational-module and date-picker suites, none of which reads a file this change touched.
- Production build actually executed: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed with 6,959 modules and the existing bundle-size advisory, emitting `dist/client/assets/index-B-S5DWlo.css` and `dist/client/assets/index-CUSwfIne.js`.
- Served-module check actually executed: the dev server on port 4001 returns 200 for `/src/Customers.jsx`, the transformed module contains `itemCreatePage`, `itemHeadText` and `customerDialogTitle`, and it is not a `Preview transform failed` stub.
- Documentation updated in the same change: `docs/MODULES.md`, `docs/project-manifest.json` (`constraints` and `implementationStatus.customerRegister`), `docs/TESTING-AND-QA.md`, `docs/TRANSFER-TO-ANOTHER-AI.md`, `docs/AI-HANDOFF-PROMPT.md`, `docs/UI-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` and `docs/WAYVIDA-BOOKS-HANDBOOK.md`.
- Not executed in this session, and therefore not claimed: any painted or visual confirmation of the new page and any click-through of Save, Cancel or the back button. In-app browser automation is denied in this environment, so the shell behaviour is reasoned from the existing `:has()` rules and confirmed only through the transformed module.
## Customers register and Customer Details - 2026-09-17

- User request: on the Sales > Customers page show the customer name and id in one column, remove Contact Person, Opening Balance and Credit Limit from the table, and replace the row actions with `View Customer` plus a three-dot More actions menu holding Edit customer, Active/Inactive, Duplicate and Delete; the customer details page must carry all the basic data and the customer's transactions with a clean UI. Touches `src/Customers.jsx`, `src/customers.css`, `src/page-knowledge.js` and the new `tests/customer-register-ui.test.mjs`. No storage key, validation rule, posting path, calculation or navigation contract changed, and the create/edit modal still uses the shared `.itemDialog*` classes that `tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` pins.
- Register: the header row is `Customer Details`, `Type`, `Email / Phone`, `Net receivable`, `Available credit`, `Status`, `Actions`. The identity cell reuses the shared `.itemIdentity` / `.itemIdentityCopy` stack, so the name sits over the customer id (the `code` the form validates as unique). Contact person, Opening balance and Credit limit are no longer columns but are still stored, still edited by the form and now read on the detail page, together with Credit days, the receivable account resolved through the Chart of Accounts, Net receivable and Available credit.
- Row actions: one `button.customerView` (`View Customer`) plus one `details.customerKebab` per row with Edit Customer, Activate/Deactivate Customer, Duplicate Customer and the destructive Delete Customer. The kebab keeps the canonical register geometry (32px square trigger on `#d9e1ec`, 7px radius, open `#3478f6` / `#245fd9`; menu `right:0;top:calc(100% + 6px)`, `min-width:196px`, `padding:6px`, 9px radius, `min-height:36px` rows) and one page-level `pointerdown` / `Escape` effect closes any open `.customerKebab`, shared by the row menus and the detail header menu.
- Behaviour: `nextCustomerCode(rows)` is a pure exported helper that returns the first free `CUSnnn` code; Duplicate is a faithful copy with a new `crypto.randomUUID()` id, the ` Copy` name suffix and that code. `toggleStatus` writes the opposite status and appends to `auditTrail`. `referenceCount(id)` counts the customer on `invoices`, `payments`, `receipts`, `creditNotes` and `wayvida-sales-orders`, and `deleteCustomer` refuses to delete while any reference exists, notifying that the customer should be marked inactive instead; a permitted deletion still asks for confirmation.
- Detail page: `section.itemsPage.customersPage.customerDetailPage` is full bleed through `.app main:has(>.itemsPage.customerDetailPage){max-width:none!important;padding:0!important}`, `div.customerDetailHead` is the 56px back bar under Working Context, and `section.customerDetailPanel` owns the card edge for the identity row and the `Basic Data` / `Transactions` / `History` tab row with count pills. `Basic Data` renders three fact cards, `Transactions` renders the shared `customerStatement(accounting,id)` sub-ledger (Date, Reference, Source, Debit, Credit, Balance) with the `Open customer statement` link and the unchanged `wayvida-credit-customer` handoff, and `History` renders the newest-first `auditTrail` or an empty state. The panel deliberately has no `overflow:hidden` (it would clip the `z-index:90` menu) and `.customerDetailTabs button` keeps `width:auto` because `src/styles.css` declares `nav button{width:100%}`.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node tests/customer-register-ui.test.mjs` is **8/8 passing** (register columns and removed columns, View Customer plus the canonical menu, one page-level dismissal effect, status/duplicate/delete-guard behaviour, the full-width detail page and its tabs, the basic data and transactions and history content, the untouched shared dialog classes, and the 12px type floor).
- Full sweep actually executed: `node --test "tests/**/*.test.mjs"` reported **495 tests, 484 passed, 11 failed**. The 11 failures are pre-existing and unrelated: they live in `tests/account-enterprise.test.mjs`, `tests/account-master.test.mjs`, `tests/create-page-navigation-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/journal-business-view.test.mjs`, `tests/journal-detail-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/journal-heading-count-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/operational-modules-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/settings-ui.test.mjs` and `tests/wayvida-date-picker-ui.test.mjs`, none of which read any file this change touched (`git status --short` lists only `src/Customers.jsx`, `src/customers.css` and the new test file).
- Production build actually executed: `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build` passed with 6,959 modules transformed and the existing >500 kB chunk advisory, emitting `dist/client/assets/index-B-S5DWlo.css` and `dist/client/assets/index-e0otl-op.js`.

## Item Details header panel merge - 2026-09-17

- User request: merge the item card and the tab strip into one div or section so the Item Details page reads cleaner and more polished. Touches `src/Items.jsx`, `src/items.css` and the tab-row contract test; no storage, posting, routing or validation contract changed.
- Structure: `src/Items.jsx` now renders `section.itemDetailPanel` wrapping `div.itemDetailIdentity` and `nav.itemDetailTabs`, so the tabs are part of the item header instead of a separate floating pill. The identity markup, the `itemDetailActions` order, the three tab labels, the `aria-current` wiring and the `itemMore itemDetailMore` menu are unchanged.
- Styling: `.itemDetailPage .itemDetailPanel{background:#fff;border:1px solid #e6ebf2;border-radius:10px}` now owns the single card edge, and `.itemDetailIdentity` keeps its grid and `padding:14px 16px` while losing its border, radius and background. `.itemDetailTabs` becomes a transparent full-width footer with `padding:0 4px` and `border-top:1px solid #e6ebf2`; the tabs stay content-sized (`flex:0 0 auto;width:auto`) at `0 12px` padding, the vertical separators are removed, and `.active` is now blue text over the 2px `#3478f6` bottom border with no white fill.
- Deliberate constraint: the panel must not take `overflow:hidden`, because that would clip the More actions menu (`z-index:90`) that opens from the identity row.
- Alignment: the 4px bar padding plus each tab's own 12px padding lines the first label up with the 16px card gutter; below 700px the bar padding drops to `0` so it follows that breakpoint's 12px identity gutter.
- Narrow screens keep their intent: the 520px block still returns the tabs to equal `flex:1 1 0` cells at `0 8px`/12px, because content-sized tabs can outgrow a narrow card and be clipped by the bar's `overflow:hidden`.
- Contract: the tab-row test in `tests/items-master-workspace-ui.test.mjs` is now `the item identity card and tab row are one header panel and the tabs never scroll`. It asserts the panel wrapper in the markup, the panel rule, the absence of `overflow:hidden` on the panel, the retained `width:auto` guarantee and the cascade ordering of the 520px fallback.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node tests/items-master-workspace-ui.test.mjs` is **5/5 passing**; `tests/item-master.test.mjs` 5/5, `tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` 9/9 and `tests/item-tax-ui.test.mjs` 5/5 - Items suites 24/24. The `node --test` runner remains unusable here because its per-file child spawn is blocked (`spawn EPERM`).

## Item Details tab-strip gap reduction - 2026-09-17

- User request: reduce the gaps or padding between the tabs on the Item details page (`Basic Data`, `Transactions`, `History`). Touches `src/items.css` and its contract test only; no markup, storage, posting, routing or validation contract changed.
- Root cause found: the strip already carried `gap:0` and `padding:0`, so there was no literal gap between the tab cells. The visible spacing came from `.itemDetailTabs button{flex:1 1 0}`, which gave every tab an equal third of the full content width and centred its label, so on a wide screen the three labels sat hundreds of pixels apart while the strip stayed full width.
- Change: the nav gained `align-self:flex-start` so the bar shrinks to its tabs inside the column body, and the tabs became content-sized (`flex:0 0 auto`) at `0 10px` padding with a 6px label-to-count gap, replacing `flex:1 1 0`, `0 12px` and 7px. The bar keeps `gap:0`, `padding:0`, `overflow:hidden`, `height:46px`, `flex:0 0 auto`, the 1px `#e6ebf2` border, the 10px radius, the `#f7f9fc` background, the 1px `border-right` separators, the transparent 2px bottom border that turns `#3478f6` on `.active`, the `white-space:nowrap` labels and the count pills.
- Narrow-viewport guard: content-sized tabs can outgrow a small viewport and then be clipped by `overflow:hidden`, so the existing 520px block now also carries `.itemDetailTabs{align-self:stretch}` beside the `flex:1 1 0`, `0 8px` padding and 12px label it already had.
- This reversed a previously recorded invariant. `docs/AI-HANDOFF-PROMPT.md` item 36 and `docs/UI-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` both said never to give a tab a content-sized width; both were rewritten to describe the new geometry and the 520px fallback, so the documents and the stylesheet now agree.
- Contract updated, not deleted: the tab-row test in `tests/items-master-workspace-ui.test.mjs` was renamed at that point to `the item detail tab row is one gap-free segmented strip that hugs its labels and never scrolls`, pinning `align-self:flex-start`, the content-sized tab rule and the 520px fallback. It has since been superseded by the header panel merge recorded above.
- Regression found after the first release of this change and fixed: the user reported that the Transactions and History tabs were no longer visible on Item Details. Cause was the interaction between the new content-sized tabs and a pre-existing element rule - `src/styles.css` declares a bare `nav button{width:100%}`, and because `flex:0 0 auto` resolves `flex-basis` to `auto`, that `width` became the flex base size of every tab, so each tab claimed the full strip width and `overflow:hidden` clipped the last two off the right edge. The previous `flex:1 1 0` had masked the same rule behind `flex-basis:0`. `src/items.css` now sets `width:auto` on `.itemDetailTabs button`, and the test pins it.
- Second defect fixed in the same pass: the narrow-viewport fallback was dead code. The tab-strip declarations sat inside the shared `@media(max-width:520px)` block, which is declared **above** the base `.itemDetailTabs` rules; at equal specificity the later base rule won, so the small-screen `align-self:stretch` / `flex:1 1 0` layout never applied (the same was already true of its padding and font-size before this change). The fallback now lives in its own `@media(max-width:520px)` block declared after the base rules, and the test asserts that ordering.
- Automated validation actually executed: `node tests/items-master-workspace-ui.test.mjs` (run directly, in-process) is **5/5 passing**. The `node --test` runner could not be used because its per-file child spawn is blocked in this session (`spawn EPERM`).
- Served-output check actually executed: the running static preview returns 200 for `http://localhost:4001/src/items.css` and the body contains the new `.itemDetailTabs`, `.itemDetailTabs button` and 520px rules.
- Not executed in this session, and therefore not claimed: any painted or visual confirmation of the new spacing, and the production build. In-app browser automation was denied (the automatic approval reviewer is misconfigured for this provider, so the review cannot complete), so no screenshot or measurement of the rendered strip exists, and `dist/client/assets/index-C9MJASD_.css` is now stale relative to `src/items.css`.
