## Create Item page section headings removed and the head bar put on the journal bar checkpoint - 2026-09-16

- The user instruction: on the Create Item page the head bar must sit under the Organisation and Branch bar at the app header width and match the Create Journal Entry bar, the five section headings (`Basic information`, `Sales information`, `Purchase information`, `Inventory information`, `Tax information`) must go, and the dark blue line above them must go.
- Root cause of the dark blue line: each section heading was a bare `<header>` element, and `src/styles.css` styles every `header` as the application bar - `.app header{border-top:3px solid #1f5fae;height:59px;padding:8px 18px;background:#fff;box-shadow:0 1px 0 #e7ebf1}`. `src/items.css` is injected before `src/styles.css` (the shell imports `./App.jsx` ahead of `./styles.css`, so `Items.jsx` pulls `items.css` in first), so the earlier `.itemSection>header` reset lost on equal specificity and each heading was painted as a white 59px block carrying the 3px dark blue top border.
- Fix: the form no longer renders any heading element. `src/Items.jsx` is `function Section({children}){return <section className='itemSection'>{children}</section>}` and none of the five `<Section>` call sites passes a `title`, so the rendered create page contains zero `<header>` and zero `<h3>` elements while the five `.itemSection` containers and their hairline dividers stay. The `.itemSection>header`, `.itemSection>header h3` and `.itemSection>header p` rules and their explanatory comment were deleted from `src/items.css`.
- Fix: the head bar now uses the Create Journal Entry compact bar geometry. `.itemCreatePage .itemDialogHead` is `display:grid;grid-template-columns:42px minmax(0,1fr);align-items:center;gap:14px;min-height:56px` with `padding:8px 24px` and `border:1px solid var(--ui-border);border-left:0;border-right:0;border-radius:0;box-shadow:none`, which is the journal bar `.je-create > header.je-header-compact` (56px, `padding:8px 12px`, one 1px `#dfe6ef` top and bottom edge, no radius, no side borders, no shadow) at this page's own gutter. The 16px/650 title and the 40px square back button were already the journal `.je-compact-title` and header button, and the page still scrolls as one document, so the bar keeps the width of the app header and of the `.orgContextBar` - both start at the 270px sidebar edge.
- Fix: the head bar is flush under the working-context bar. `main` was still carrying the shell box - `.app main{padding:22px clamp(16px,2.2vw,32px) 40px;max-width:1680px}` in `src/ui-system.css` - so its 22px top padding opened a gap between the Organisation / Branch bar and the head bar and its 32px side padding inset the bar inside the page. `src/items.css` now takes both off that `main`: `.app main:has(>.itemsPage:has(.itemCreatePage)),body:has(.itemsPage .itemCreatePage) .app main{max-width:none!important;padding:0!important}`. The top edge of the bar is therefore the bottom edge of the fixed `.orgContextBar` (52px shell padding + `main` `margin-top:46px`, no gap) and its width is the org bar's own width, 270px left to the right edge. The rule is written twice, once keyed off the direct child and once off a plain descendant, and both carry `!important`, because the earlier version without `!important` was still not reaching the bar in the running app while the sibling `.itemCreatePage .itemDialogHead` rule did - the cascade or the direct-child relative selector was not settling the way the stylesheet read. The head bar's own gutter is now exactly `.orgContextBar`'s `padding:8px 24px`, and both bars narrow to 12px at the same 760px breakpoint (`.orgContextBar` is 12px from 760px down in `src/ui-quality-polish.css`), so the 40px back button lines up with the org bar's building icon at every width.
- The operator screenshot was measured rather than eyeballed: the PNG is 1902x246 at a 1.25 device pixel ratio (the 270px sidebar is 337 image px), the org bar ends at image y=126, the head bar's top border sits at y=155 - a 28 image px = 22 CSS px gap, exactly the `main` padding-top - and the bar runs x=378..1860, leaving 32 CSS px of `#f6f8fb` page background on each side, exactly the `main` side padding. That is what identified the shell box, not the bar's own rules, as the cause.
- Validation actually executed: `node tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` 7/7 (its heading test now asserts that no section renders a `<header>`, that no section is given a title, that the five heading strings are gone, and that `src/styles.css` still holds both legacy rules); `node tests/item-tax-ui.test.mjs` 4/4; `node tests/item-master.test.mjs` 4/4; `node tests/empty-state-ui.test.mjs` 6/6; `node tests/ui-quality.test.mjs` 5/5; `node tests/navigation-structure.test.mjs` 2/2; a `react-dom/server` render of the real page has 0 `<header>` and 0 `<h3>` elements and 5 `.itemSection` containers; the preview server serves the new stylesheet verbatim (16855 chars with the `!important` flush rules, the 24px head padding, the `body:has()` duplicates and the 760px block); `tmp/countheader.mjs`, `tmp/x-item-create.mjs` and `tmp/browser-sim.mjs` all pass; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed; the full sweep is 78 files with 447 assertions passing, 63 files green and the same 15 pre-existing failures (12 assertions).
- Not verified: no rendered browser or human visual pass and no production build, because this sandbox cannot spawn a child process (`spawn EPERM`), so `vite` and `esbuild` cannot run. The geometry above is asserted from the stylesheets and the shell cascade (`src/ui-system.css` `.app main`, `src/ui-quality-polish.css` `.orgContextBar` and `.app>main{margin-top:46px}`), from pixel measurements of the operator's 1.25x screenshot, the module the preview server compiles, and the server-rendered markup, not from a painted page.

## Create Item page head bar, section headings and static action bar checkpoint - 2026-09-16

- The user instruction: `Create Item` must sit in a head bar placed directly under the working-context Organisation and Branch bar at the same width as the application header, the heading must be static, the sentence under it must go, the section description lines (`Basic information ...`, `Purchase information ...`, `Inventory information ...`, `Tax information ...`) must go, and the Cancel / Create Item action row must have its own space below the form instead of floating over it. The reference for the fix is the Create Journal Entry page.
- Root cause of the width gap: `.app>main` is the shell scroll port and carries `scrollbar-gutter:stable` in `src/ui-quality-polish.css`, so a full-bleed head bar rendered inside it reserved a scrollbar gutter and came out roughly one scrollbar narrower than the fixed application header and `.orgContextBar`. `src/journal-form.css` already avoids this for Create Journal Entry by letting the page scroll as one document.
- `src/items.css` applies the same contract to the item form: `html:has(.itemsPage .itemCreatePage)` and `body:has(.itemsPage .itemCreatePage)` stop being the scroll port, `.app:has(>main>.itemsPage:has(.itemCreatePage))` drops its fixed height, and `.app main:has(>.itemsPage:has(.itemCreatePage))` becomes `overflow:visible` with no `max-height`, so the head bar and the page scroll together and the bar keeps the header width. Both gutters are `clamp(16px,2.2vw,24px)` and the head bar is `padding:8px clamp(16px,2.2vw,24px)`, which lines them up with the `padding:8px 24px` Organisation and Branch bar.
- `.itemCreatePage .itemDialogFooter` is no longer sticky or translucent: it is `position:static` with `margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:28px`, a white 1px-bordered card on `var(--ui-shadow)`, so the action row sits after the form content rather than over it.
- `src/Items.jsx` renders the shared `Section` as `<section className="itemSection"><header><h3>{title}</h3></header>{children}</section>` and passes no `description` to any of the five sections, so no section subtitle renders. The earlier `.itemSection>header` reset (`position:static;inset:auto`) stays, because `src/styles.css` declares an unscoped `header{position:fixed;...}` that had made the section headings render as the application bar.
- Validation actually executed: `node tests/item-create-page-ui.test.mjs` 7/7, including the new contract that the page scrolls as one document, the action bar is static and no section carries a description; `node tests/item-tax-ui.test.mjs` 4/4; `node tests/item-master.test.mjs` 4/4; `node tests/empty-state-ui.test.mjs` 6/6; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed; a full sweep of `tests/*.test.mjs`, one file per `node` process, is 78 files with 63 passing and the same 15 pre-existing failures.
- Not verified: no rendered browser or human visual pass and no production build. This sandbox refuses `spawn` (`spawn EPERM`), so neither `vite` nor `esbuild` can run. The geometry above is asserted from the stylesheet and the rendered markup, not from a painted page.

## Shared table empty states checkpoint - 2026-09-16

- The user instruction: every table or list with no records must show one consistent, professional empty state - a small neutral illustration, a short title, a concise description and, only where the user can create the missing record, one primary action - centred horizontally and vertically inside the table's own content area, with no emoji and no large colourful graphics.
- `src/EmptyState.jsx` is the single implementation: `EmptyState({variant,title,description,action,actionLabel,onAction,className})` plus the named `EmptyIllustration`. It renders `div.emptyState` holding one `svg.emptyArt` (`viewBox="0 0 96 96"`, `aria-hidden="true"`) with a shared plate and stroke glyph, then `h3.emptyTitle`, `p.emptyText` and an optional `div.emptyAction > button.emptyCta`. The variants `budget`, `lock`, `request`, `journal`, `adjustment` and `default` share one plate, one 2.4 stroke weight and one grey, an unknown variant falls back to `default`, and the module imports only React and its own stylesheet.
- `src/empty-state.css` centres the group (`display:flex;flex-direction:column;align-items:center;justify-content:center`), bounds it at `min-height:250px` with `32px 20px` padding so it never forces a scroll, holds the illustration at 96px (82px below 640px, inside the requested 80-120px band), keeps the title at 15px/650 and the description at 13px on the existing palette, and reuses the design-system primary button for the optional action. `td.emptyStateCell.emptyStateCell.emptyStateCell` clears the table cell padding, `.emptyStateRow,.emptyStateRow:hover` clears the row hover fill and `.emptyStateHost` spans the card-list form. No visible text falls below the 12px floor.
- Wired call sites: `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` (register row, `colSpan="4"`), `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` (lock register row and the mobile lock card list, `colSpan="7"`), `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` (accountant register row, `colSpan={9}`) and `src/InventoryAdjustments.jsx` (register row, `colSpan={REGISTER_COLUMNS.length}`); the unlock request register renders the same component with no action. The copy is the requested wording - `No budgets found`, `No locks created yet`, `No unlock requests yet`, `No journals found`, `No adjustments found` - each with its own sentence and, except for unlock requests, its own create action.
- The replaced inline markup is gone: the register rows that printed a bare sentence (the budget `budgetEmpty` cell, the Period Lock mode-aware `emptyText` line and its drawer sentence) and the legacy `je-empty` block. Removing that block also deleted an unreachable duplicate `return` statement in `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` - a whole second `je-page` register sitting after the live `return`, confirmed by walking the parsed function body with `@babel/parser` - so no reachable behaviour changed.
- Validation actually executed: `node tests/empty-state-ui.test.mjs` 6/6 (component contract, centring and layout CSS, the five copy contracts, the spanning-row placement, the empty-state-only-as-fallback guard, and the illustration animation contract); `node tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` 7/7; `node tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` 27/27; `node tests/inventory-adjustments.test.mjs` 20/20; `node tests/journal-entries-ui.test.mjs` 1/1; `node tests/navigation-structure.test.mjs` 2/2; `node tests/ui-quality.test.mjs` 5/5; every changed JSX module parses under `@babel/parser`; `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed.
- A full sweep of `tests/*.test.mjs`, run one file per `node` process because `node --test` cannot spawn its workers here: 62 files pass and 15 fail, against 60 passing and 16 failing before this change (`tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` is green again and `tests/empty-state-ui.test.mjs` is new). Every remaining failure is pre-existing and none is caused by this change. `tests/account-enterprise.test.mjs`, `tests/account-master.test.mjs`, `tests/account-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/create-page-navigation-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/document-preview-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/general-ledger-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/receipt-ui.test.mjs`, `tests/sites-worker.test.mjs` and `tests/wayvida-date-picker-ui.test.mjs` read only files this change never touched; `tests/operational-modules-ui.test.mjs` (App.jsx `Budget vs Actual`) and `tests/settings-ui.test.mjs` (Navigation.jsx) belong to the earlier Budget-destination and settings work. Of the four that read changed sources, three - `tests/journal-business-view.test.mjs`, `tests/journal-detail-ui.test.mjs` and `tests/journal-heading-count-ui.test.mjs` - assert strings absent from both the current file and the pristine `HEAD` blob, and `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs` matches a newline-separated regex against a CRLF checkout (false raw, true once normalised). This repository checks out with `core.autocrlf=true`, so any test that embeds a bare LF in a source match is broken on this machine; `tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` was fixed that way, normalising the source to LF before matching.
- Rendered verification without a browser: the served `src/EmptyState.jsx` was evaluated through `react-dom/server`, where 13 markup contracts held (the `emptyState` root, the `emptyArt` plate and glyph with `viewBox="0 0 96 96"` and `aria-hidden="true"`, the `emptyTitle`, the `emptyText` and one `emptyCta` button labelled `Create Budget`), every one of the six variants rendered its own plate and glyph, and an unknown variant fell back to `default`; with no action wired, neither `emptyCta` nor the `emptyAction` wrapper rendered. `tmp/x-empty-live.mjs` then loaded the real `src/InventoryAdjustments.jsx` over HTTP from the preview server (through a small loader that maps the served React dependency onto the Node copy, so one React instance renders) and rendered its register over an empty store: the markup contains `<tr class="emptyStateRow"><td colSpan="10" class="emptyStateCell">` inside `<tbody>`, carrying `No adjustments found`, the requested description and the `Create Adjustment` button, which is the end-to-end proof that the empty state lands inside the table body rather than beside it. Those two `tmp/` harnesses are scratch verification, not committed tests. 
- Follow-up, same day, on the user request to animate the illustrations: the empty-state artwork now draws itself in and then rests, entirely inside `src/empty-state.css`, with no markup, copy, layout or accounting change. Six `@keyframes` drive it - `emptyArtIn` (fade plus a 5px rise), `emptyPlateIn` (scale 0.9 to 1), `emptyBarIn` (the three budget bars growing from `transform-origin:50% 100%`), `emptyDrawIn` (`stroke-dashoffset` 120 to 0), `emptyBreathe` (a 1.5px drift) and `emptyPulse` (an opacity dip) - applied as a roughly one-second staggered entrance (`.emptyArt` 0.45s, `.emptyArtPlate` 0.5s, the `.emptyBar` hooks at 0.15/0.27/0.39s, `.emptyDraw` at 0.75s with its `:nth-of-type(2/3/4)` delays and `.emptyDrawLate` at 0.5s for the shapes that follow another) over a slow 7s `emptyBreathe` on `.emptyArtGlyph` and a 3.6s `emptyPulse` on `.emptyDot`. The stroke glyphs carry `stroke-dasharray:120` with `stroke-dashoffset:0` as their base value, so the artwork is complete in any browser that runs no animation, and `@media (prefers-reduced-motion:reduce){.emptyState .emptyArt,.emptyState .emptyArt *{animation:none!important}}` at the end of the file removes all of it. `src/EmptyState.jsx` only gained the hook classes `emptyBar`, `emptyDraw`, `emptyDrawLate` and `emptyDot` on the shapes it already drew.
- Validation actually executed for the animation: `node tests/empty-state-ui.test.mjs` 6/6, whose new case `the illustration animates itself in and then rests` asserts all six keyframes, the applied rules, the shape hooks, the reduced-motion escape, a maximum animation duration at or below 8s and a pulse opacity floor of 0.4, and cross-checks that every keyframe used is declared and every brace balanced; the served `src/empty-state.css` and `src/EmptyState.jsx` were fetched from `http://localhost:4001/` and carry the keyframes and the hooks; `tmp/x-render.cjs` rendered the served component through `react-dom/server` with `rect.emptyBar` x3 and `path.emptyDraw` for the budget variant; `tmp/x-empty-live.mjs` still renders the real `src/InventoryAdjustments.jsx` register over an empty store as `<tr class="emptyStateRow"><td colSpan="10" class="emptyStateCell">` inside `<tbody>`; `tmp/browser-sim.mjs` reports `BROWSER-SIM OK`; `tmp/ssr-smoke.mjs` renders 43,336 characters of markup with `.app` present; and `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed. The full sweep stayed at 62 files passing and 15 failing - the same pre-existing set - so the motion added no regression.
- Not executed here and not claimed: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is denied in this environment) and no production build (`esbuild` cannot spawn and the approval reviewer refuses escalation), so the static preview server on `http://localhost:4001/` remains the operator's window into this change.

## HTTP VM crypto.randomUUID checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user report: after the hosting fix the page showed `Wayvida Books could not start. crypto.randomUUID is not a function`.
- Cause: `crypto.randomUUID()` exists only in a secure context (HTTPS or localhost). A Docker VM opened at `http://<ip>:4002` has `crypto.getRandomValues` but not `randomUUID`. Demo bootstrap writes `localStorage`, the audit wrapper calls `crypto.randomUUID()`, and the start error boundary shows that message.
- `src/crypto-uuid.js` installs a UUID v4 fallback on `crypto` when the method is missing. `index.html` runs the same install in a classic script before the app module. `src/main.jsx` and `src/audit-log.js` import the helper first. Existing `crypto.randomUUID()` call sites stay unchanged.
- Validation actually executed: `node --test tests/crypto-uuid.test.mjs tests/audit-log.test.mjs` 6/6; `npm run docs:check` passed; `npm run build` succeeded (existing large-chunk advisory remains); `npm run test:sites` 4/4. A Node bootstrap of `installAuditLog` plus `bootstrapDemoData` against memory storage completed `{ loaded: true }` after `installRandomUUID()`. Docker Engine is still absent here, so a live HTTP VM pass is not claimed.

## Deployed UI visibility checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user report: after deployment the UI is not visible.
- Cause: Vite 6 `allowedHosts: ["terminal.local"]` blocks every VM IP and hostname with a host-not-allowed page, and nginx `try_files` could return `index.html` for a missing hashed `/assets/*.js` file, which browsers refuse to execute as a module so `#root` stays empty.
- `vite.config.mjs` now sets `allowedHosts: true` on both `server` and `preview`. `docker/nginx.conf` 404s `/assets/` misses, pins JS MIME types, and sends `Cache-Control: no-cache` for `index.html`. Instrument Sans loads from `index.html` instead of a blocking CSS `@import`. `src/main.jsx` reports a start failure instead of a silent blank root.
- Validation actually executed: `node --test tests/vite-port.test.mjs tests/docker-vm.test.mjs tests/ui-quality.test.mjs` 11/11; `npm run docs:check` passed; `npm run build` succeeded (existing large-chunk advisory remains); `npm run test:sites` 4/4. Production preview at `http://127.0.0.1:4003/` returned HTTP 200 HTML titled `Wayvida Books` with `#root` and `/assets/index-C7y23PRo.js` (`Content-Type: text/javascript`); the matching CSS returned `text/css`. Docker Engine is still absent here, so a live container on 4002 is not claimed. The running local Vite on 4001 still served HTTP 200.

New exports `plannedTotal(row,count)` (a row total over only the planned periods), `budgetDateRange(financialYear,period,startDate,endDate)`, `budgetDurationLabel(...)` (`12 months · Monthly`, `4 quarters · Quarterly`, `2 half-years · Half-Yearly`, `1 year · Yearly`, `6 months · Custom`, and `Set the period dates` while a custom range is incomplete) and `budgetDateError(...)` (`Custom budget dates must fall within FY 2026-27.`, plus the start-after-end and missing-date messages). `periodLabels(period,financialYear)` returns `[financialYear]` for Yearly and `['Custom Period']` for Custom.

- The user instruction: refine the existing Budget module in place without redesigning the application or changing the visual language - keep the three-step Create Budget workflow, add Budget Details metadata, make Overview the default detail tab, separate the financial statements into their own tabs, replace the mismatch presentation with a Budget balance check, correct the variance maths and colouring, and add Accounts, Activity History and Revisions tabs.
- `src/budget-store.js`: `BUDGET_PERIODS` is now `['Monthly','Quarterly','Half-Yearly','Yearly']`; `createBudget` stores `startDate` and `endDate`; `logBudgetActivity(state,id,action)` appends an activity entry; `parseBudgetCSV(text,accounts)` parses an imported CSV into `{accounts,allocations}`; `computeVarianceRows` returns `{account,budget,actual,variance,percent,achievement,direction}` with `variance` as `actual - planned`; new `varianceDirection(type,variance)` returns `favourable` / `unfavourable` / `neutral` and inverts for an expense account, so a lower spend reads favourable.
- `src/budget-statements.js`: `budgetTotalRows` now emits eight rows labelled `Total Revenue`, `Total Expenses`, `Net Profit / Loss`, `Profit Margin`, `Total Assets`, `Total Liabilities`, `Total Equity` and `Total Liabilities & Equity`, and the old `Budget Mismatch Adjustment` row is deleted; new `budgetBalanceCheck(accounts,allocations,period)` returns `{assets,liabilities,equity,profit,liabilitiesAndEquity,difference,balanced}`; `budgetStatementRows` renamed the statement closes to `Net Profit / Loss`, `Total Liabilities & Equity` and `Net Cash Flow` and appends a `kind:'check'` `Balance Difference` row carrying `className:'budgetStatementMismatch'` and a note that it is reported and never posted.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx`: step one gained Start date, End date and Description fields and a `span.budgetScopeSummary` chip; step two gained `select.budgetTypeFilter`, `Clear all` and `span.budgetSelectedCount`, and `AccountSelectGrid` now folds each accounting type behind a `button.budgetGroupCell`; step three groups the grid under `BUDGET_ALLOCATION_BANDS`, keeps a sticky `td.budgetStickyCol` account column, folds its type rows, and adds a Growth % input, `Import Budget` and `Clear All` ahead of `section.budgetTotals` and the new `section.budgetBalanceCheck`; the detail head keeps only the back button, the name, the metadata line, the status badge and Edit / Duplicate / Export / Archive, because the KPI cards moved into the new `BudgetOverview`; `BUDGET_DETAIL_TABS` is now the flat eight-item `[key,label]` list with `BUDGET_DETAIL_DEFAULT='Overview'`; `VsActual` was rewritten with a Variance % column, a separate Achievement % column and a `neutral` total row; new `BudgetAccountsTable` and `BudgetDetailsPanel`; `AccountTree` and `ForecastPanel` were deleted.
- `src/budget-workspace.css`: a large appended block covers `.budgetOverview`, `.budgetPanel`, `.budgetHealth`, `.budgetAlertsPanel`, `.budgetDetails`, `.budgetAccountsView`, `.budgetVariance.favourable` / `.unfavourable` / `.neutral` (`#19734c` / `#b42318` / `#475467`), `.budgetStatementCheck`, the sticky allocation header and account column, `.budgetAllocBand`, `.budgetAllocGroup`, `.budgetGrowthField`, `.budgetImportButton`, `.budgetClearAll`, `.budgetBalanceCheck`, `.budgetScopeSummary` and `.budgetTypeFilter`; the detail tab row grid moved from four to eight columns so the eight tabs form one row, and the two dead `.budgetTotalsGrid tr.budgetTotalsMismatch` rules were removed.
- Tests, all green: `tests/budget-store.test.mjs` 8/8, `tests/budget-statements.test.mjs` 10/10, `tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` 6/6, `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` 8/8, `tests/budget-navigation-ui.test.mjs` 3/3.
- Rendered-markup harnesses: `tmp/bw-detail-ssr.mjs` is new - it renders a copy of the component with `BudgetDetail` exported and asserts the metadata line, the absence of a stray brace, the eight flat tabs, the four Overview figures, the details facts, the health bars and alerts, each statement, Variance % against Achievement % with the neutral total, the Accounts table, Activity History and Revisions; `tmp/bt-totals-ssr.mjs`, `tmp/bt-statement-ssr.mjs` and `tmp/bt-stmt-check.mjs` were rewritten for the new labels, the deleted mismatch row and the `check` row; `tmp/budget-create-ssr.mjs` was extended; `tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` reports 50/50.
- `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passes.
- Full sweep after the change: `FILES=72 green=58 red=10 tests=411 passed=400 failed=11` with the same ten pre-existing failures (`account-enterprise` 7/2, `account-master`, `create-page-navigation-ui`, `item-tax-ui`, `journal-business-view`, `journal-detail-ui`, `journal-heading-count-ui`, `operational-modules-ui`, `settings-ui`, `wayvida-date-picker-ui`) and `CANNOT-START(4)`. The pass count rises by two and no new failure appears.
- Not verified in this environment and not claimed: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is blocked here) and no production build (`npm` and `npx` are absent and esbuild cannot spawn, so `vite build` fails with `spawn EPERM`). ## Budget statement view checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user instruction: create a view page for a budget from the details the creation section collects, also carrying Profit and Loss, Balance Sheet and Cash Flow Statement, following the supplied Zoho Books budget-detail screenshots, and add `View budget` to the 3-dot menu of the Budgets list.
- New storage-free service `src/budget-statements.js` is the one derivation the allocation step and the view page share. It exports `BUDGET_STATEMENTS` (`Profit and Loss`, `Balance Sheet`, `Cash Flow Statement`), `budgetPeriodTotals(accounts,allocations,period)` (one pass per period returning the count, the labels, revenue, expense, profit, assets, liabilities, equity, `netWorth`, `mismatch`, margin and every total), `budgetTotalRows(...)` (the same nine statement lines the allocation step closes with, labels, classes, percent flags and note included) and `budgetStatementRows(statement,accounts,allocations,period)` (ordered `{kind,key,label,values,total,className,note,account}` rows whose `kind` is `section|type|group|account|groupTotal|typeTotal|grand`). It touches no storage and imports only `periodCount` and `periodLabels` from `src/budget-store.js`.
- Grouping follows the chart of accounts group the account was created in (`account.group||account.type`) under each accounting type, exactly as the reference sheet nests them, and every group, type and statement closes with its own total row. Profit and Loss reads Revenue (Income) then Expense (Expenses) and closes on `Total for Profit and Loss`; Balance Sheet reads Assets, Liabilities and Equities and closes on `Total for Liabilities & Equities` then `Budget Mismatch Adjustment`; Cash Flow reads Operating (income in, expenses out), Investing (assets out) and Financing (liabilities and equities in) and closes on `Net change in cash`. A planned amount stays positive where it was planned positive, and a figure that moves value out - an expense, or an asset purchase on the cash flow - is signed negative, so the statement reads on its own. The allocation-step sign convention is unchanged: type totals stay positive magnitudes, the derived lines are signed, and profit rolls into equity.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx`: the wizard-private `planned` / `periodSums` / `sumValues` / `marginOf` / `*ByPeriod` / `totalsRows` chain was deleted and replaced by the single call `const totalsRows=budgetTotalRows(form.accounts,form.allocations,form.period);`, so the allocation step and the statements can never disagree. The now-unused `AllocationTable` component was deleted with the flat `Budget Details` tab, because the statements render the same accounts, amounts and totals grouped the way the Chart of Accounts organises them.
- New exported `BudgetStatementView({statement,budget,business})` renders `budgetStatementRows(...)` into the existing `div.budgetAllocationGrid` table shell with the period columns plus one `Total` column: the `section`, `type` and `group` rows become full-width `th scope="rowgroup" colSpan={labels+2}` bands, the account rows name the account with its code beneath it in the accounting view, and the `groupTotal` / `typeTotal` / `grand` rows are `th scope="row"` label cells beside right-aligned figures. An unknown statement, or a budget with nothing planned, renders the existing `budgetEmpty` sentence rather than a broken table.
- The detail screen tab row now leads with the statements: `const BUDGET_DETAIL_TABS=[...BUDGET_STATEMENTS,'Budget vs Actual','Forecast','Accounts','Activity History','Revisions']` and `const BUDGET_DETAIL_DEFAULT=BUDGET_STATEMENTS[0]`, so the detail opens on Profit and Loss and every previous view stays reachable. The `detailTab` state and the detail `back()` reset both use `BUDGET_DETAIL_DEFAULT`; the register visible `View Budget vs Actual` button still opens `Budget vs Actual`.
- The Budgets register kebab gained `View budget` as its first entry, wired to a new `viewStatement` handler that opens the detail on the first statement, ahead of the existing `Edit`, `Duplicate` and the destructive `Delete`. The visible `View Budget vs Actual` button and the canonical kebab geometry are unchanged.
- `src/budget-workspace.css`: `.budgetStatement` was appended after the totals block - the band rows (`.budgetStatementSection` on `#f4f7fc`, `.budgetStatementType` on `#fbfcfe`, `.budgetStatementGroup` indented on `#536176`), the total rows on `#f8fafd` with `.budgetStatementDerived` on `#f1f5fb`, `.budgetStatementMismatch` on the same 5% amber tint `#fff6ec` / `#8a4b09` the totals block uses, `.budgetStatementNegative` on `#b42318`, right-aligned nowrap figures and a 12px floor. The detail tab row keeps its existing four-column boxed grid, which eight tabs fill as two even rows.
- Tests: new `tests/budget-statements.test.mjs` (9/9) covers the statement list, the per-period totals, the three statement row shapes, the cash-flow signs, the empty and unknown cases, the shared derivation and the tab-row wiring. `tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` (6/6) now expects four kebab entries and the `BUDGET_DETAIL_DEFAULT` wiring, and `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` (8/8) reads the totals labels from `src/budget-statements.js`. The new SSR probe `tmp/bt-statement-ssr.mjs` renders all three statements from a six-account plan and asserts the band order, the group, type and statement totals, the 14-column band span, the account code under the account name, the cash-flow negatives and the balance-sheet mismatch.
- Full sweep after the change: `FILES=72 green=58 red=10 tests=409 passed=398 failed=11` with the same ten pre-existing failures (`account-enterprise` 7/2 and one each in `account-master`, `create-page-navigation-ui`, `item-tax-ui`, `journal-business-view`, `journal-detail-ui`, `journal-heading-count-ui`, `operational-modules-ui`, `settings-ui`, `wayvida-date-picker-ui`) and `CANNOT-START(4)` (`account-ui`, `document-preview-ui`, `general-ledger-ui`, `receipt-ui`). The file and pass counts rise by exactly one file and nine tests, which is the new test file; nothing else moved and no unrelated fix was attempted.
- Not executed, not claimed: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is blocked in this environment) and no production build (`npm` and `npx` are absent and esbuild cannot spawn, so `vite build` fails with `spawn EPERM`). The live dev server was left serving `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` for a manual hard reload.

## Budget allocation totals checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user instruction: in the Budget Allocation step of Create Budget, add a last section for the total for expense, the total for revenue, the liability and equity total, mismatch adjustments and the total P & L, matching how standard budget sheets in other accounting software present those totals. Two Zoho Books budget screenshots were supplied as the reference.
- What was there before: the allocation step ended in `div.budgetReviewTotals`, four equal KPI chips (Total income budget, Total expense budget, Expected profit, Profit margin) that restated the same grand totals the grid already implied and carried no per-period detail.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx`: the `const summary=computeSummary(...)` line was replaced by the derived-total chain - `planned(type)`, `periodSums(accounts)` (one pass per period column), `sumValues(values)`, `marginOf(profit,revenue)`, then `revenueByPeriod`, `expenseByPeriod`, `profitByPeriod`, `assetsByPeriod`, `liabilitiesByPeriod`, `equityByPeriod`, `netWorthByPeriod` and `mismatchByPeriod`, and one `totalsRows` array of nine `{key,label,values,total,className,percent,note}` rows. The chips render was replaced by `section.budgetTotals` > `div.budgetTotalsGrid` > one table whose header repeats the allocation grid period columns plus a `Total` column, whose row label cell is a `th scope="row"` and whose value cells carry `budgetTotalsNegative` on a negative figure. `computeSummary` is still imported and still used by `BudgetDetail` and the reports.
- The sign convention, chosen deliberately and now documented: the type totals (revenue, expense, assets, liabilities, equities) stay positive planned magnitudes, while the derived statement lines are signed. `Total for Profit and Loss` is `revenue - expense`; `Total for Liabilities & Equities` is `liabilities + equity + profit`, because the planned result rolls into equity; and `Budget Mismatch Adjustment` is `assets - (liabilities + equity + profit)`. That reproduces the reference sheet exactly - on it the profit and loss total and the liabilities and equities total print the same figure (-50,000) and the mismatch line cancels it (+50,000) - and it means a plan with no balance-sheet account still balances to zero by construction.
- Nothing else changed: no storage key, no `src/budget-store.js` edit, no allocation, revision, variance, forecast, alert, permission or approval behaviour, and the allocation inputs, shortcuts and Create Budget footer are untouched. The mismatch figure is computed on the fly and is deliberately not stored.
- `src/budget-workspace.css`: the totals block was appended after the wizard styles - `.budgetTotals` (a light `#fbfcfe` panel with a `1px solid #e1e8f2` border), `.budgetTotalsGrid` as its own horizontal scroll port with a `min-width:680px` table, a `#f6f8fb` header row, `#f1f5fb` derived rows, the mismatch row on the 5% amber tint `#fff6ec` / `#8a4b09` with its `small` explanation, `#b42318` for a negative figure, and a sub-700px fold. Every figure stays at 12px or larger, which the existing wizard-style guard in `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` keeps enforcing because the block is inside the slice it measures.
- `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` (8/8): the totals assertion now checks that the step renders `className="budgetTotals"` and `Budget totals`, that the rows come from `totalsRows.map` in the period columns, that the nine statement labels exist with the mismatch adjustment derived as the balance between planned assets and planned liabilities and equities, and that every total derives from the allocation cells; four new CSS tokens were added to the style guard.
- `tmp/bt-totals-ssr.mjs` is the new SSR probe. It renders step three with income 5,000, expense 3,000 and an asset of 1,000 and asserts the nine row labels in order, 117 value cells, and the rendered currency cells (revenue 5,000 in its period and row total, expense 3,000, profit and loss 2,000 twice, assets 1,000 twice, mismatch -1,000 twice, margin 40% twice, no-revenue periods reporting 0% rather than dividing by zero). A second render of a quarterly income-and-expense-only plan asserts the four quarter columns, the profit and loss total repeated by the liabilities and equities total, and the mismatch total cancelling it - the reference-sheet behaviour.
- Full sweep after the change: `FILES=71 green=57 red=10 tests=400 passed=389 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)` (`account-ui`, `document-preview-ui`, `general-ledger-ui`, `receipt-ui` cannot spawn esbuild here) - identical to the recorded baseline, so no regression and no unrelated fix was attempted.
- Not executed, not claimed: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is blocked by this environment, which is an infrastructure limit rather than a decision about this change) and no production build (`npm` and `npx` are absent and esbuild cannot spawn). The live preview therefore needs a hard reload (Ctrl+Shift+R) before it is checked.



## Create Budget account selection checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user instruction: in the Account Selection step of Create Budget, show the list of all the accounts in the Chart of Accounts with their types, and under each account its code, in a simple and friendly UI.
- What was there before: `AccountSelectGrid` in `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` grouped rows by `account.group||account.type` and filtered the offered list through `TYPES[form.type]` (`BUDGET_TYPES`). Because the `Budget type` field was removed from step one in an earlier change, `form.type` kept its `'Profit & Loss Budget'` default, so the step silently offered Income and Expenses accounts only, a section heading could mix an account group (`Operating Income`) with a raw type, and a separate `Account Type` column repeated the heading.
- `src/terminology.jsx` now owns the one accounting-type vocabulary: `ACCOUNT_TYPE_ORDER` (`['Assets','Liabilities','Equity','Income','Expenses']`) and `accountTypeLabel(type,business)`, which returns the raw type in the accountant view and the shared business wording (`Assets` -> `What You Own`, `Expenses` -> `Money Spent`) in the business view. That wording is the same `TERMINOLOGY` map the Chart of Accounts page already reads, so the two screens cannot disagree.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx`: the wizard list is now `accounts.filter(account=>account.active&&!account.isGroup)` - every active posting account, with no budget-type filter - and the search matches the name, the code, the account group and the type together. `AccountSelectGrid` renders a two-column `.budgetAccountGrid`: one `budgetGroupRow` per type in `ACCOUNT_TYPE_ORDER` order (an unknown type falls into an `Other` bucket instead of disappearing), each carrying its count (`n of m selected`, or `m accounts`), then one row per account with a labelled checkbox and a `budgetAccountCell` printing the name over its code - and its account group when it has one and it differs from the type. Each type row is a `th scope="colgroup"` holding a `.budgetGroupCell` div, and each account cell is a `label` bound to its checkbox by `htmlFor` / `id`. The separate `Account Type` column is gone because the type is now the section, and the column header reads `Account` / `Money category`.
- `src/budget-workspace.css`: the type row is `tr.budgetGroupRow>*` (the spanning `th`) carrying its own `border-top` and the `#f1f5fb` background, with a `display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:space-between` `div.budgetGroupCell` inside it so the type sits at one end and its count at the other; `.budgetAccountCell` is `padding:0` and its `label` holds the flex column that stacks the name over a 12px muted `small`. The muted token is `#667085` (4.97:1 on white) rather than a lighter grey, the checkbox is 18px with `cursor:pointer`, `:focus-visible` rings it at 2px `#245fd9`, and `tbody tr:focus-within` tints the row so keyboard focus is visible. No new token is below 12px.
- Defect found in the running preview and fixed: the type rows rendered as a narrow column of one letter per line at the left edge of the grid. Cause: the flex rule was applied to the `td` itself (`.budgetGroupRow td{display:flex;...}`), and setting `display` to anything but `table-cell` on a table cell takes it out of the table layout algorithm - the browser wrapped it in an anonymous cell sized by the 56px choose column, so `Assets` wrapped one character per line. The fix keeps every cell a real table cell and moves the flex row into `.budgetGroupCell` inside the spanning `th`; the same mistake was present on `.budgetAccountCell`. `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` now asserts `!css.includes('td{display:flex')`, so a cell can never be taken out of the table layout again.
- Accessibility pass on that grid: the table carries an `aria-label`, both column headers are `th scope="col"`, each type row is a `th scope="colgroup" colSpan={2}` so a screen reader announces the group a checkbox belongs to, every checkbox keeps its `aria-label` of name plus code and gains a stable `id` (`budget-account-<code>`), and the account cell wraps its name and code in a `label htmlFor={boxId}`, which names the control and turns the whole text cell into a click target without nesting a label inside a label.
- Nothing else in the module changed: the store, the allocation grid, the summary totals, the register, the scope control and the approval lifecycle are untouched, and `BudgetList`'s and `BudgetReports`' `Budget type` filters still read `BUDGET_TYPES`.
- Regression caught by an SSR harness and fixed: removing the now-unused `TYPES` const also dropped `BUDGET_TYPES` from the `./budget-store.js` import list, which `BudgetList` and `BudgetReports` still use. `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs` failed with `ReferenceError: BUDGET_TYPES is not defined`; the import was restored and both report OK again. Only the unused `TYPES` lookup was removed, and the two suites that read the rendered register could never have caught this on their own.
- Validation actually performed: `node tmp/dump-budget-accounts.mjs` renders both views (accountant sections `Assets` / `Liabilities` / `Equity` / `Income` / `Expenses` with counts and codes; business sections `What You Own` / `What You Owe` / `Owner Investment` / `Money Earned` / `Money Spent`) and confirms the markup that fixes the vertical text - `<table aria-label=...>`, `<th scope="col">`, `<th scope="colgroup" colSpan="2">`, `<input id="budget-account-1000">` and `<label for="budget-account-1000">`; `node tmp/budget-create-ssr.mjs` -> `budget create flow SSR OK` (two type sections, one checkbox per account with its matching id, the accessible table name, the code and group under the name, the sections in chart order, and asset accounts now offered); `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/coa-detail-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/probe-scope-fix.mjs` all report OK; `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` -> `50/50 jsx files transformed` with the same five pre-existing `LEFTOVER JSX?` notices; the dev server serves `budgetAccountCell`, `accountTypeLabel` and `ACCOUNT_TYPE_ORDER` at `http://localhost:5173`.
- `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` (8/8): the grid test is now `account selection lists every chart of accounts entry under its accounting type` and asserts the type sections and their order, the counted section row, the labelled checkbox, the name-over-code cell, the two-column header, the absence of the `Account Type` column, the widened search placeholder and the two new CSS tokens.
- Full sweep after the change: `FILES=71 green=57 tests=400 passed=389 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)` (`account-ui`, `document-preview-ui`, `general-ledger-ui`, `receipt-ui` cannot spawn esbuild here) - identical to the recorded baseline, so no regression and no unrelated fix was attempted.
- Scratch-harness note: `node tmp/zscope2-ssr.mjs` now fails with `SyntaxError: tmp/pc2-probe.jsx: 'CurrentPeriodPanel' has already been exported`, because `tmp/pc2-probe.mjs` regenerates that probe file by appending an export list to `src/PeriodClosing.jsx`, which already declares `export function CurrentPeriodPanel`. That is a pre-existing defect in an unrelated scratch script, not a product failure; the shared scope picker is covered instead by `tmp/probe-scope-fix.mjs`, `tmp/zscope-ssr.mjs` and the organisation scope suites.
- Not executed, not claimed: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is blocked by this environment's auto-review, which is an infrastructure limit rather than a decision about this change) and no production build (`npm` is absent here and esbuild cannot spawn).


## Create Budget scope universe and details grid checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user decision: on the Create Budget page the `Budget scope` row printed `Planning for ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd · Kochi Branch.` with nothing selectable, and the `Budget name` input was too long. The scope must offer the organisations and branches to choose in the multi-select form, and the step-one fields must be standardised and arranged.
- Root cause, reproduced with a Node SSR probe before the change: `BudgetWizard` read its available list from `getAccessibleOrganizations()`, the working context the header is narrowed to. With the header on one organisation and one branch that list resolves to `[ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd [Kochi Branch]]`, so `getScopeVisibility()` reported one organisation with one branch, `showOrganisation` and `showBranch` both came back `false`, `scopePickerRows()` returned `{organisationRows:[],branchRows:[]}` and the control rendered an empty `div.pc-scope-picker` - no field to open and no way to widen the scope.
- `src/organisation-context.js` now owns both lists and the single `scopedOrganisations(companyKey,branchKey,companyFallback,branchFallback)` derivation behind them: `getAccessibleOrganizations()` (unchanged behaviour, `wayvida-context-companies` / `wayvida-context-branches`) and the new `getScopeOrganisations()` (`wayvida-accessible-organizations` / `wayvida-accessible-branches`, defaulting to the whole demo list, so it can never be empty). No third reader and no page-local copy was added.
- `src/HeaderOrgSelectors.jsx`'s Change / Customize drawer now reads its `accessibleOrganizations` from that shared reader instead of deriving the same list inline, so the header and the budget page cannot disagree about what may be offered.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx`; the wizard reads `getScopeOrganisations()` into state, seeds the opening scope from the working context through the one `wizardScopeSeed(initial)` helper, keeps `companyIds` / `branchIds` on the stored `scope` so editing reopens the same scope it was saved with, and re-reads the universe on `wayvida-working-context-change`, `wayvida-organization-change` and `storage`, pruning any selection the new list no longer contains through `normaliseScope()`.
- `src/budget-workspace.css`: the details grid is one four-column track (`repeat(4,minmax(0,1fr))`) whose `budgetFieldWide` name spans two columns while financial year and budget period take one each, and the scope row reuses that track at `grid-column:span 2` per field so Organisation sits under the budget name and Branch under financial year; it folds to two columns below 1100px and one below 700px. `.budgetWizard .budgetSetup{overflow:visible}` keeps the open checkbox list from being clipped by the card, the same way the journal lines card may not clip the account combobox.
- Validation actually performed: `node tmp/probe-scope-fix.mjs` (before: both selectors hidden and zero rows; after: `showOrganisation` and `showBranch` both true, `All organisations` plus all four organisations and `All branches` plus ABC's three branches, opened seeded on ABC + Kochi Branch, Northstar addable with its branches appearing filtered); `node tmp/budget-create-ssr.mjs` → `budget create flow SSR OK` (two triggers, the seeded values, and the shared visibility rule still hiding the organisation control when only one organisation is accessible); `node tmp/dump-step1.mjs` (markup of the new details and scope rows); `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` → `50/50 jsx files transformed` with the same five pre-existing `LEFTOVER JSX?` notices; `node tmp/ra2-profile-menu.mjs` → `PROFILE MENU + DRAWER SSR OK` (the app shell still boots with the header importing the shared context module); `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/coa-detail-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/zscope2-ssr.mjs` all report OK.
- `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` (8/8) now asserts the scope universe, the seed helper, the context listeners and the stored scope refs, and its wizard-style token list gained the four-column grid, the `span 2` scope fields, the shared field tracks and the 1100px fold.
- Suites re-run green after the change: `organisation-scope` 10/10, `organisation-branch-scope-ui` 10/10, `budget-create-flow-ui` 8/8, `budget-register-ui` 6/6, `budget-store` 7/7, `budget-navigation-ui` 3/3, `period-closing-ui` 27/27, `period-lock-settings-ui` 16/16, `period-scope-visibility` 16/16, `journal-line-columns` 6/6, `journal-create-ui` 22/22, `header-context` 3/3, `sidebar-shell` 2/2, `ui-quality` 5/5, `demo-data` 2/2, `navigation-structure` 2/2.
- Full sweep after the change: `FILES=71 green=57 tests=400 passed=389 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)` (`account-ui`, `document-preview-ui`, `general-ledger-ui`, `receipt-ui` cannot spawn esbuild in this environment) - identical to the recorded baseline, so no regression and no unrelated fix was attempted. The 11 known failures stay as recorded in the baseline list.
- Not executed and therefore not claimed: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is blocked by this environment's review policy, which is an infrastructure limit rather than an acceptance decision) and no production build (`npm` is absent and esbuild cannot spawn here). The dev server serves the edited sources at `http://localhost:5173/` - verified with `Invoke-WebRequest` against `/src/organisation-context.js`, `/src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` and `/src/budget-workspace.css`, all 200 and carrying the new tokens - and a hard reload (`Ctrl+Shift+R`) is required because the preview holds a cached module graph.
## Create Budget full-bleed page header checkpoint - 2026-09-15 (superseded in part by the budget scope universe and details grid checkpoint above)

- The user decision: on the Create Budget page the block holding the back arrow and the `Create Budget` title must align beneath the organisation and branch customisation bar in the header and match the header's width, and must reuse the style and UI of the Create Journal Entry page. This reverses the earlier same-day decision to pin that head to a 190px tile.
- `src/budget-workspace.css` now treats that head as the page header. Three shell rules carry it: `.app main:has(>.budgetV3.budgetWizard){max-width:none!important;padding:0!important}` drops `main`'s `22px clamp(16px,2.2vw,32px) 40px` padding and its 1680px cap for the wizard page only, `.app main>.budgetV3.budgetWizard{max-width:none!important;margin:0!important;padding:0 0 28px!important}` drops the page's own padding the same way, and `.app main>.budgetV3.budgetWizard>:not(.budgetV3Head){margin-left:clamp(16px,2.2vw,32px)!important;margin-right:clamp(16px,2.2vw,32px)!important}` hands that exact gutter back to the stepper, the active step card, the error line and the wizard footer.
- The head rule is now `.app main>.budgetV3.budgetWizard>.budgetV3Head.budgetWizardHead{display:grid!important;grid-template-columns:40px minmax(0,1fr)!important;align-items:center!important;gap:14px!important;width:100%!important;max-width:none!important;min-height:56px!important;margin:0 0 16px!important;padding:8px 24px!important;border:1px solid var(--budget-border)!important;border-left:0!important;border-right:0!important;border-radius:0!important;background:#fff!important;box-shadow:none!important}`, so the bar runs from the sidebar edge to the right edge of the viewport - exactly the span the fixed `.orgContextBar{left:270px;right:0}` working-context bar occupies, which is why it reads as aligned with the header rather than inset inside the page - and its 24px side padding puts the back button on the same left edge as the organisation name inside that bar.
- The user then asked for breathing room between that bar and the step card, so the head carries `margin:0 0 16px` - the same 16px the journal workspace grid and `.budgetSteps` use - and because `nav.budgetSteps` keeps its `margin-top:0`, the two margins collapse to one 16px gap between the bar's divider and the stepper.
- The back button took the Create Journal Entry geometry (`width:40px;height:40px;min-width:40px`, `border-radius:8px`, hover `#eef4ff` / `#bcd0f5` / `#2463d4`) and the title took `.je-compact-title`'s `font-size:16px;font-weight:650;line-height:1.25`; the `8px` vertical padding plus the 40px button reproduce the journal header's 56px height.
- The fixed 190px tile and its `.companySelect{width:190px}` rationale are gone: no rule in `src/budget-workspace.css` sets `max-width:190px` any more, and the only remaining `190px` in that file is the register's `select.budgetFilterYear` flex basis.
- The `@media(max-width:760px)` block shrinks the head to a 36px column with `8px 12px` padding, following the `.orgContextBar` mobile padding, and still stacks the stepper.
- No storage key, budget calculation, allocation, revision, variance, forecast, alert or settings behaviour changed. `src/budget-store.js` and `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` are untouched, so this is a stylesheet-only change and the rendered markup is unchanged from the previous checkpoint.
- Contracts: `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` 8/8 - its style-token list now asserts the three shell rules, the full-bleed head declaration, the 40px back button, the 16px title and the preserved gutter, and it gained assertions that `max-width:190px` is gone and that only the non-head children carry the gutter. Also green: `tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` 6/6, `tests/organisation-scope.test.mjs` 10/10, `tests/organisation-branch-scope-ui.test.mjs` 10/10, `tests/period-scope-visibility.test.mjs` 16/16, `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` 27/27, `tests/journal-line-columns.test.mjs` 6/6, `tests/budget-store.test.mjs` 7/7.
- Validation actually executed: `node tmp/budget-create-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs` all report success, and the rendered step-one DOM still opens `budgetV3Head budgetWizardHead` > `budgetHeadBack` > `budgetHeadTitle Create Budget`, confirming only the stylesheet moved. `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` reports `50/50 jsx files transformed` with the same five pre-existing `LEFTOVER JSX?` warnings, and the dev server serves the new rules at `http://localhost:5173/src/budget-workspace.css` (full-bleed head, shell padding reset, gutter rule, 40px button and 16px title all present; `max-width:190px` absent).
- Full sweep: `FILES=71 green=57 tests=400 passed=389 failed=11`, plus `CANNOT-START(4)`. The failing files are the same pre-existing, unrelated ones.
- Limitation for this environment: no painted browser pass (in-app browser automation is blocked by the environment auto-review, not by any decision about this change) and no production build (`npm` is unavailable here and esbuild cannot spawn). The full-bleed alignment is therefore reasoned from the cascade - every wizard rule outranks the shell `main` and `.budgetV3` rules on specificity and again on source order - rather than confirmed on screen.

## Create Budget head width, stepper alignment and multi-select scope checkpoint - 2026-09-15 (superseded in part by the full-bleed page header checkpoint above)

- The user decision: on the budget creation page the `Create Budget` head must match the width of the header's `Change / Customize` organisation control and sit below it, the step names must never be cut by a line in the progress stepper, and the `Budget type` select must be replaced by a multi-select organisation and branch dropdown.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` still renders `div.budgetV3Head.budgetWizardHead` first inside `section.budgetV3`, but the head is now a 190px-wide bordered tile instead of a full-width bar, because `190px` is the width of the header working-context control itself (`.companySelect{width:190px}` in `src/styles.css`); the wizard head therefore lines up with `Change / Customize` above it rather than stretching the content width. `src/budget-workspace.css` sizes it with `.app main>.budgetV3>.budgetV3Head.budgetWizardHead` (`190px` wide, `min-height:46px`, `7px 12px` padding, `1px solid var(--budget-border)`, 10px radius, `0 3px 14px rgba(16,24,40,.05)`), so keep that rule above the generic `.budgetV3Head` reset.
- The head keeps one square `button.budgetHeadBack` (now 30px with a 7px radius, `1px solid #d7e0eb`, hover `#eef4ff` / `#2463d4`, `IconArrowLeft` at 19px, `aria-label="Back to budgets"`) beside `div.budgetHeadText`, which stacks `span.budgetHeadTitle` (`Create Budget`, or `Edit Budget` with a `small.budgetHeadHint` naming the budget). The `Step <n> of 3` chip is no longer in the head.
- The step chip now lives in the stepper card, beside the steps rather than in the head: `nav.budgetSteps` is a `minmax(0,1fr) auto` grid whose second column is `span.budgetStepCount` (`justify-self:end`, a neutral `#eef2f7` pill at 12px/600). The ordered stepper is behaviourally unchanged - `nav.budgetSteps > ol` holds one `li` per step, each `button` taking the same computed state as its parent (`active` with `aria-current="step"`, `complete` drawing `IconCheck`, `upcoming` and `disabled`), and clicking an earlier step still walks back.
- The stepper no longer truncates a step name. `li` is `flex:1 1 170px` rather than a fixed `min-width:155px`, the 2px connector is painted behind every label (`li:not(:last-child)::after` at `z-index:0`, `top:12px`, `left:36px`, `right:12px`), each `button` sits above it at `z-index:1` with a `#fff` background and `padding:0 12px 0 0`, and `button span` is `13px/600` with `white-space:nowrap` and no `text-overflow:ellipsis` and no `overflow:hidden`. Because the connector can no longer overlap the text, no step name is cut by a line. Below 760px the stepper stacks: `nav.budgetSteps` drops to one column, `ol` becomes a 10px-gap column, the connectors are hidden and `button span` returns to `white-space:normal`.
- `Budget type` is removed from step one. `budgetSetupFields` now holds `Budget name` (given `className="budgetFieldWide"`, so `.budgetWizard .budgetSetupFields .budgetFieldWide{grid-column:1/-1}` spans it across the two-column grid), `Financial year` and `Budget period`, and `form.type` keeps its `'Profit & Loss Budget'` default so the step-two account filter still filters by type without asking for it again.
- The scope row renders the shared control in its new multi-select form: `<OrganisationBranchScope ... label="Budget scope" showLine={false} multiple/>`, so an organisation or branch set can be chosen rather than one value. Visibility and the option lists still come from the pure `scopePickerState()` in `src/organisation-scope.js` over the `getAccessibleOrganizations()` list. No `multi` flag was added to that helper - visibility stays derived from the available organisation list, so the journal-lines and Period Lock rules are untouched - and the one binding call site passes only the shared inputs.
- `src/organisation-scope.js` gained `ALL_ORGANISATIONS` and two more pure helpers, `scopePickerRows(state)` and `toggleScopePicker(state,row)`: `scopePickerRows` turns the picker state into `{organisationRows,branchRows}` of `{key,kind,ref,label,meta,checked}` rows, each list led by an `All organisations` / `All branches` row with ref `''` that is ticked when nothing is chosen, and `toggleScopePicker` returns the next `{companyIds,branchIds}` for a picked row. `scopePickerState` also now composes `getScopeVisibility()` from the available organisation list and reports `branchesAll`, `resolved`, `selectedBranchIds`, `organisationSummary`, `branchSummary` and `allOrganisations`. No page re-implements that filtering.
- `src/OrganisationBranchScope.jsx` renders both forms from the same decision: the original one-choice `<select>` pair (used by Create Journal Entry journal lines and the Period Lock lock drawer) is unchanged, and the opt-in `multiple` form renders `div.pc-scope-picker.pc-scope-multi` holding a `MultiScopeField` per visible column - `button.pc-multi-trigger` (`aria-label`, `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, `aria-expanded`, `IconChevronDown`) opening `div.pc-multi-panel` (`role="listbox"`, `aria-multiselectable="true"`) of `button.pc-multi-option` rows, closed by an outside `pointerdown` or `Escape`. The new `src/organisation-branch-scope.css` scopes every rule to `.pc-scope-picker` so it outranks the page button defaults, keeps all text at 12px or larger, and makes the panel static below 760px instead of absolutely positioned.
- No storage key, budget calculation, allocation, revision, variance, forecast, alert or settings behaviour changed; `src/budget-store.js` is untouched.
- Contracts: `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` 8/8 asserts step one renders three fields with no `Budget type` and a `budgetFieldWide` name, that the scope call passes `showLine={false} multiple/>`, that the head slice excludes the step chip, that the compact head and un-clipped stepper style tokens are present, and it gained a test proving the scope uses the shared multi-select control. `tests/organisation-scope.test.mjs` 10/10 covers the new `scopePickerRows` / `toggleScopePicker` exports, and `tests/organisation-branch-scope-ui.test.mjs` 10/10 covers the multi-select render. Also green: `tests/period-scope-visibility.test.mjs` 16/16, `tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` 6/6, `tests/journal-line-columns.test.mjs` 6/6, `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` 27/27, `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs` 16/16, `tests/budget-store.test.mjs` 7/7.

## Create Budget head and progress stepper checkpoint - 2026-09-15 (superseded in part by the head width, stepper alignment and multi-select scope checkpoint above)

- The user decision: the Create Budget page must use the same back navigation as the Create Journal Entry page, in the head, and the three create steps must read as progress rather than as tabs because they are `Budget Details`, `Account Selection` and `Budget Allocation` in order.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` replaced the wizard head with `div.budgetV3Head.budgetWizardHead`: one square `button.budgetHeadBack` (40px, `1px solid #d7e0eb`, 8px radius, `aria-label="Back to budgets"`, `IconArrowLeft` at 19px), `div.budgetHeadText` holding `span.budgetHeadTitle` (`Create Budget` or `Edit Budget`) and, when editing, a `span.budgetHeadHint` naming the budget, then a neutral `span.budgetStepCount` chip reading `Step <n> of 3`. The old text link `button.budgetBack` (which the budget detail screen still uses) and the subtitle `Set the budget scope, choose the accounts and plan the amounts.` are gone from the wizard.
- The step row became a real progress stepper: `nav.budgetSteps > ol` holds one `li` per step, each rendering one `button` whose class and that of its parent are the same computed state - `active` with `aria-current="step"` for the current step, `complete` for a finished one (it draws `IconCheck` instead of the number), `upcoming` and `disabled` for a later one so it cannot be jumped to. Clicking an earlier step still walks back, and the 2px `li:not(:last-child)::after` connector turns `var(--budget-blue)` behind a completed step.
- Step one gained the `div.budgetSelectHead` heading its two siblings already had (`Budget details`), so every step of the wizard is titled.
- `src/budget-workspace.css` retired the underline tab row (`.budgetSteps{display:flex;border-bottom:1px solid var(--budget-border)}`, `.budgetSteps button{flex:1;border-bottom:3px solid transparent}`, the 21px `font-size:11px` number circle, the active/finished fills) and the tab-era media rules `.budgetSteps{overflow:auto}` and `.budgetSteps button{min-width:155px}`. It keeps the detail screen `button.budgetBack` styling because that screen still renders the text link, and it gained the head and stepper rules at the end of the file, all at 12px or larger, plus a `@media(max-width:760px)` block that drops the connectors and stacks the steps.
- No storage key, calculation, allocation, revision, variance, forecast, alert or settings behaviour changed; `src/budget-store.js` is untouched.

## Budgets register toolbar and grid checkpoint - 2026-09-15

## Budget create flow checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user decision: Create Budget must ask for the budget name, financial year, budget period and budget scope, keep the template behind a toggle, show every income and expense account in one grouped checkbox grid with no account-type tabs, then allocate and save with one `Create Budget` action; the created budget joins the register and opens in the detail view.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` now renders `BudgetWizard` as three steps (`Budget Details`, `Account Selection`, `Budget Allocation`) instead of four, so the separate `Review Budget` step is gone and its income, expense, expected-profit and profit-margin totals moved onto the allocation step inside `.budgetReviewTotals`.
- Step one keeps one `budgetSetupFields` grid (Budget name, Financial year, Budget period, Budget type) and adds a `budgetScopeRow` that renders the shared `OrganisationBranchScope` control from `src/OrganisationBranchScope.jsx`, whose visibility, branch options and branch clearing come from `scopePickerState()` in `src/organisation-scope.js` over the `getAccessibleOrganizations()` list. The wizard keeps `companyIds` / `branchIds` on `form` and resolves the saved scope (`type`, `name`, `organisationId`, `organisationName`, `branchId`, `branchName`) from the picker state, so `BUDGET_SCOPES`, the hardcoded branch list and the manual scope-type select are gone from the create page while the register scope filter still reads `BUDGET_SCOPES`.
- The template moved behind `label.budgetTemplateSwitch` (a visually hidden `input[type=checkbox][role=switch]`), which reveals `budgetTemplateCards` only while it is on and resets the template to `Custom Budget` when switched off. The standalone budget type card grid (`budgetPurposeCards`) was removed; the four planning types are now one `Budget type` select, so the Balance Sheet, Cash Flow and Complete Business budgets stay creatable.
- Step two renders `export function AccountSelectGrid`, one `.budgetAccountGrid` table of every account the selected budget type allows, grouped by `account.group||account.type` through `budgetGroupRow` header rows, each account toggled by a labelled checkbox, with the Account Code column hidden in the business view. The `budgetAccountTabs` type tabs are gone from this step; the detail screen Accounts tab still uses `AccountTree`.
- The footer is `Cancel` + `Next` on step one, `Back` + `Continue` on step two and `Back` + `Create Budget` on step three (`Save Changes` when editing). `Save Draft` and `Submit for Approval` moved off the create page, so `BudgetDetail` gained a mode-aware status action that advances the lifecycle through the existing `setBudgetStatus` and `FLOW` engine (`Submit for approval`, `Approve budget`, `Activate budget`, `Mark complete`); an edit now keeps the stored status instead of resetting it to `Draft`.
- An imported CSV seeds `accounts` / `allocations` in the form initialiser as well as the import effect, so the allocation step never renders an empty first paint.
- `src/budget-workspace.css` gained a scoped block for the new pieces: the scope-row skin (the shared `.pc-*` control restated in the budget palette because the `.pc` page scope does not reach this page), the template switch, the account grid (sticky header, group rows, chosen-row tint, checkbox sizing), the four-column wizard totals, the footer layout and a 700px reflow. Every new token is 12px or larger.
- No storage key, budget calculation, allocation, revision, variance, forecast, alert or settings behaviour changed; `src/budget-store.js` is untouched.

## Budgets register table checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user decision: the Budgets tab must show a data grid rather than the earlier card-bearing table. The register carries exactly four columns - `Budget Name`, `Financial Year`, `Budget Period` and `Actions` - and every rows Actions cell holds one read-only `View Budget vs Actual` button beside one 3-dot menu with `Edit`, `Duplicate` and the destructive `Delete`.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` renders the register as `<table><thead><tr>{['Budget Name','Financial Year','Budget Period','Actions'].map(label=><th key={label}>{label}</th>)}</tr></thead>` with one `<tr>` per budget. The Budget Type, Scope, Total Amount, Status, Created By and Last Updated columns and the six icon-only row buttons (View, Edit, Duplicate, Export, Archive, Delete) were removed from the register; `exportBudget` and `archive` are still passed to `BudgetDetail`, which already exposes them in the detail header, so CSV export and archiving stay reachable and no handler was deleted.
- `View Budget vs Actual` (`button.budgetRowView`, `aria-label="View Budget vs Actual for <name>"`) reopens the existing detail screen with its `Budget vs Actual` tab already active: `BudgetWorkspace` keeps a new `detailTab` state, passes `initialTab={detailTab}` into `BudgetDetail`, and gives it `key={budget.id+'/'+detailTab}` so a row click always remounts on the requested tab. `BudgetDetail` now seeds its `useState` from `initialTab||'Budget Details'`, and `back()` resets the state so a later plain open still lands on Budget Details.
- The row menu is the canonical shared kebab: `details.budgetKebab` > `summary` holding a horizontal `IconDots` (17px, `aria-label="More actions for <name>"`) and `div.budgetKebabMenu`, whose three entries stay `type="button"` and close the menu through a new `menuRun(handler)` helper. A new page-level `useEffect` in `BudgetList` registers `pointerdown` and `Escape` dismissal over `document.querySelectorAll('.budgetKebab[open]')`, matching the Period Lock, Journal Entries and Chart of Accounts effects.
- `src/budget-workspace.css` gained `.budgetRowActions` (flex, 8px gap) and `.budgetRowView` (34px minimum height, 11px padding, 7px gap, 13px/650 `#245fd9` label) plus the canonical kebab geometry, written with element selectors because the manifest `.budgetV3 button` reset has higher specificity. `.budgetListCard` already set `overflow:hidden`, which would have clipped every dropdown, so it now sets `overflow:visible` and the first and last header cells carry the matching corner radii; the menu opens downward from `right:0`, so it is never clipped at the viewport edge. No font size below the 12px floor was introduced.
- No storage key, status workflow, allocation, revision, variance, forecast or settings behaviour changed: `src/budget-store.js` is untouched, so the persisted budget shape and every budget calculation keep their exact contract.
- Contracts: new `tests/budget-register-ui.test.mjs` 4/4 covers the four columns and one row per budget, the removal of the old columns and icon buttons, the row controls including `menuRun`, the `[open]` dismissal effect and `Escape`, the `initialTab` / remount wiring for View Budget vs Actual, and the kebab geometry with `overflow:visible` and the type floor.
- Validation actually executed: `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs` renders the real page against two seeded budgets and reports `budget register SSR OK - 4 columns, View Budget vs Actual plus a 3-dot Edit/Duplicate/Delete menu on 2 rows (8625 chars)`; `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` reports `50/50 jsx files transformed` with the same five pre-existing `LEFTOVER JSX?` warnings; the dev server serves `/src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx?raw` and `/src/budget-workspace.css?raw` at 200 with `budgetKebabMenu`, `budgetRowView` and `.budgetKebab>summary{` present.
- Full sweep: `FILES=70 green=56 tests=388 passed=377 failed=11`, plus `CANNOT-START(4)`. The ten failing files are the same pre-existing, unrelated ones (`account-enterprise` 7/2 and one each in `account-master`, `create-page-navigation-ui`, `item-tax-ui`, `journal-business-view`, `journal-detail-ui`, `journal-heading-count-ui`, `operational-modules-ui`, `settings-ui`, `wayvida-date-picker-ui`).

## Account details right-side popup checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user decision: on the Chart of Accounts page, clicking an account must open its details as a right-side popup instead of the inline detail column that sat beside the account grid.
- `src/AccountWorkspace.jsx` now renders the details as a drawer built on the geometry the module already ships for Create Account: `<div className="am-create-drawer-layer am-detail-layer">` holding the `am-create-drawer-backdrop` button and a full-height `<aside className="am-create-drawer am-detail-drawer" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-label="Account details">`. Its `am-create-drawer-header` carries `<h2>Account details</h2>` with the open account as `{account.name} · {account.code}` plus an explicit `type="button"` close, above the unchanged `am-detail-body` and `am-detail-footer`. Both the backdrop and the close button call the existing `setSelected(null)`, so the row click path (`open(a)`), the register 3-dot "View Transactions" entry and the `wayvida-open-account` sessionStorage handoff from Balance Sheet, Day Book and the ledger all keep opening the same detail with no route or state change.
- Because the details no longer occupy a grid column, `am-layout` lost the `has-detail` modifier and its two-column rules were removed from `src/account-workspace.css`: `.am-layout.has-detail{grid-template-columns:minmax(330px,1fr) minmax(380px,.9fr)}`, `.am-layout.has-detail .am-cards{...repeat(2)...}`, the `@media(max-width:1150px)` block that re-declared them plus `.am-detail{position:relative;top:auto;grid-row:1}`, the `repeat(3)` override in that same block, and the `.am-detail{...position:sticky;top:82px}` container rule, with the `700px` and `420px` card rules reduced to plain `.am-cards`. The account grid therefore keeps the full page width, and the cards view keeps its three-up and four-up breakpoints. No other selector in `src/` used `has-detail` or `.am-detail`, and `.am-detail-top` stays because the Day Book transaction detail header still uses it.
- Appended to `src/account-workspace.css`, in the same commented style as the sticky-grid block, is the detail-drawer block: `.am-detail-layer .am-detail-drawer{width:min(560px,100vw)}` (the Create Account width, raised above `.am-create-drawer`'s own width at equal-class specificity), an ellipsised header subtitle, `.am-detail-drawer .am-detail-body{flex:1;min-height:0;overflow-y:auto;padding:16px;background:#f6f8fb}` so the body is the only scroll container, and `.am-detail-drawer .am-detail-footer{flex:0 0 auto;justify-content:flex-start;padding:13px 16px;background:#fff;box-shadow:0 -8px 24px rgba(23,35,58,.05)}` so the actions stay pinned. The block declares no font size at all, so every visible string keeps the existing 12px-plus scale. Reusing `am-create-drawer-layer` also means the page's existing `.am-coa-page:has(.am-create-drawer-layer)` compaction applies while the popup is open, which is the same behaviour as Create Account.
- New behaviour added with the popup, mirroring `src/EnterpriseAccountForm.jsx`: one `useEffect` on `account` locks `document.body.style.overflow`, closes the popup on `Escape` and restores the previous overflow on close. `start(account)` already clears `selected` before opening the form, so the two drawers stay mutually exclusive and the scroll lock cannot leak.
- Every data point and action survives the move: the `AccountConfiguration` summary with its dated `Current Balance`, the `Transactions` / `Audit Trail` tabs and their `AccountLedger` and audit list renderers, and the `Edit Account`, `{t.viewLedger}` and `Mark Inactive` / `Mark Active` footer actions are all unchanged inside the new shell. Nothing was added to the account record, and no accounting, validation or storage rule changed.
