## Budget group navigation checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user decision: Budgets, Budget Reports and Budget Settings must be shown separately inside one Budget tab under Accounting instead of three flat Accounting entries.
- Navigation (`src/Navigation.jsx`) now nests them: `{label:'Accounting',icon:IconBook,children:[leaf('Chart of Accounts'),leaf('Journal Entries'),leaf('Period Lock'),{label:'Budget',children:[leaf('Budgets'),leaf('Budget Reports'),leaf('Budget Settings')]}]}`. The existing `render(node,parent,depth)`/`contains(node,page)` recursion already opens the containing groups while a budget page is active and derives the group id `Accounting/Budget` (and the `aria-controls` id `nav-Accounting-Budget`), so no renderer, CSS or page-name change was needed; the three page names stay `Budgets`, `Budget Reports` and `Budget Settings`, which keeps `App.jsx` routing (`['Budgets','Budget Reports','Budget Settings'].includes(a)`), the welcome-block exclusion and the `BudgetWorkspace` key unchanged.
- `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` gained one shared row, `const BUDGET_TABS=['Budgets','Budget Reports','Budget Settings']` plus `function BudgetTabs({page,onNavigate})` rendering `nav.budgetWorkspaceTabs[aria-label="Budget sections"]` with three buttons, `aria-current="page"` and `className="active"` on the current one, each calling the existing `onNavigate` prop. It is rendered once per budget screen, directly under that screen's `budgetV3Head` in `BudgetList`, `BudgetReports` and `BudgetSettings`.
- Prop threading that the SSR probe caught before the browser did: the budgets list is the nested `BudgetList` component, not `BudgetWorkspace`, so `page` and `onNavigate` are now declared on `BudgetList` and passed from `<BudgetList state={state} accounts={accounts} business={business} page={page} onNavigate={onNavigate} .../>`; without that, the new row threw `page is not defined` when the Budgets page rendered. `BudgetReports` and `BudgetSettings` were widened the same way (`{budgets,business,page,onNavigate}` / `{state,save,notify,page,onNavigate}`).
- Styling is appended to `src/budget-workspace.css` as `.budgetWorkspaceTabs` (a bordered `#f8fafc` strip whose buttons are `min-height:42px`, `font-size:13px`, separated by `1px #e1e8f2` rules, with the active tab marked by a 3px `--budget-blue` underline, `#f0f5ff` fill and `#2463d4` label, a `:focus-visible` ring and a 700px breakpoint that splits the three tabs evenly at 12px text). It deliberately reuses the existing `.budgetAccountTabs` visual language rather than inventing a new one, and it never sets text below 12px.
- Nothing else moved: budget lifecycle, allocations, revisions, variance, alerts, forecast, settings, permissions, CSV import/export, `src/budget-store.js` storage keys and every accounting rule are untouched, and no page was merged or renamed.
- Contracts: new `tests/budget-navigation-ui.test.mjs` (3 tests) asserts the nested Budget group, exactly one leaf per budget page, the single shared tab row rendered on all three screens, the threaded props (including the `BudgetList` regression) and the `.budgetWorkspaceTabs` design tokens; `tests/navigation-structure.test.mjs` was updated because its Accounting assertion named the three flat leaves, and it now also asserts the Budget group. `tests/operational-modules-ui.test.mjs` keeps its pre-existing failure: it asks for a `Budgeting` group in the navigation and for `Budget vs Actual` in `App.jsx`, so the new `Budget` group satisfies neither string.

## Chart of Accounts sticky grid head checkpoint - 2026-09-15

- The user decision: on the Chart of Accounts page, the filter row (Search, Type, Status, Filters), the column header row (Account Name, Account Code, Account Group, Status, Actions) and the account-group / accordion row (Assets, Liabilities, Equity, Income, Expenses) must stay visible while the account list scrolls.
- Where the scroll happens: the shell keeps `html,body,#root{height:100%;overflow:hidden}`, `.app{height:100dvh;overflow:hidden}` and `.app>main{height:calc(100dvh - 105px);overflow-x:clip;overflow-y:auto;overscroll-behavior:contain}` (all in `src/ui-quality-polish.css`, which `src/main.jsx` imports last), so `main` is the only scroll port in the product. `.am-coa-page>.am-filter-toolbar{position:sticky;top:0;z-index:9}` therefore pins the filter row to the top of that port and needs no application-header offset.
- Why the grid wrapper is bounded: `.am-account-grid{min-width:1120px}` with `.am-table-wrap:has(.am-account-grid){overflow:auto}` already made the table wrapper a scroll port for horizontal scrolling, and a scroll port with an unconstrained height sticks nothing vertically. The fix is CSS-only, appended to `src/account-workspace.css`: `.am-coa-page{--am-coa-head-h:40px}` (the measured header-cell height: 11px padding twice plus a 12px/1.5 label), `.am-coa-page .am-table-wrap:has(.am-account-grid){overflow:auto;max-height:max(360px,calc(100vh - 280px));max-height:max(360px,calc(100dvh - 280px));overscroll-behavior:contain}` (the `dvh` line wins where supported, the `vh` line is the fallback), `.am-coa-page .am-account-grid thead th{position:sticky;top:0;z-index:6;background:#f7f9fc;box-shadow:inset 0 -1px 0 #e6ecf6}` and `.am-coa-page .am-account-grid .am-group-row th{position:sticky;top:calc(var(--am-coa-head-h) - 1px);z-index:5;box-shadow:inset 0 -1px 0 #dde5f0,inset 0 1px 0 #dde5f0}`.
- Layering and legibility: the pinned filter row sits at 9, the column header row at 6 and the group row at 5, so a group row sticks under the header and all three stay below the existing `.am-more` row menu (`z-index:80`) and the heading `Templates` disclosure. The header row keeps `.am-table th`'s opaque `#f7f9fc` fill and the group row its `#edf2f9`, which sticky cells require, and the inset shadows replace the collapsed-table border browsers drop from sticky cells. The group row overlaps the header by one pixel (`calc(var(--am-coa-head-h) - 1px)`) so a measurement drift hides behind the header instead of opening a gap.
- Nothing else moved: no markup, filter, tree, `colSpan`, `scopeColumns`, drawer or empty-state change, and `.am-layout.has-detail`, `.am-detail{position:sticky;top:82px}` and the detail-pane ledger table are untouched. No test asserted CSS geometry before or after.
- Validation actually executed: `node tests/account-header.test.mjs` 5/5, `node tests/account-settings.test.mjs` 4/4, `node tests/account-group-search.test.mjs` 6/6, `node tests/account-templates.test.mjs` 2/2, `node tests/account-create-drawer-ui.test.mjs` 2/2, `node tests/account-group-picker-ui.test.mjs` 6/6, `node tests/ui-quality.test.mjs` 5/5, `node tests/organisation-scope.test.mjs` 9/9, `node tests/organisation-branch-scope-ui.test.mjs` 9/9 `node tests/journal-actions-ui.test.mjs` 5/5 and the new `node tests/account-sticky-grid-ui.test.mjs` 5/5, a CSS contract asserting that the filter row pins at the top of the shell scroll port, that the grid wrapper keeps `overflow:auto` with both `max-height` lines and `overscroll-behavior:contain` and never `overflow:visible`, that the header row and the account-group row are sticky at `top:0` and `calc(var(--am-coa-head-h) - 1px)` with z-indexes 6 and 5, and that the row menu still outranks them at `z-index:80!important` - with the pre-existing failures unchanged (`account-enterprise` 7/2, `account-master` 7/1, `create-page-navigation-ui` 0/1). `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` reports 50/50 jsx files transformed with the same five pre-existing `LEFTOVER JSX?` warnings, `node tmp/zscope-ssr.mjs` reports `scope SSR OK - grid 12141/12285 chars, drawer 3558/5183/5016/5504 chars`, `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passes, and the full sweep (`powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tmp/sweep.ps1`) reports `FILES=67 green=53 tests=376 passed=365 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4): account-ui.test.mjs, document-preview-ui.test.mjs, general-ledger-ui.test.mjs, receipt-ui.test.mjs` and the same ten failing files - that is the pre-change run (`FILES=66 green=52 tests=371 passed=360 failed=11`) plus this checkpoint's one new green suite, so the only delta is the suite added here.
- Served evidence: the dev server started with `node tmp/dev-live.mjs` (no npm in this environment, so it runs under the esbuild shim) returns HTTP 200 for `http://localhost:5173/src/account-workspace.css?raw`, and that response contains `.am-coa-page>.am-filter-toolbar{position:sticky;top:0;z-index:9}` and the `max-height:max(360px,calc(100dvh - 280px))` bound; `src/account-workspace.css` grew from 47307 to 48318 bytes.

## Organisation and branch picker checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The user decision: the organisation and branch selection dropdown must use the same logic as the Create Journal Entry -> Journal Lines section, and be offered in Period Lock and the Request Unlock form.
- `src/OrganisationBranchScope.jsx` is the one interactive control. It renders a pure `scopePickerState({organisations,companyIds,branchIds,label})` from `src/organisation-scope.js` and contains no scope logic of its own: it imports only `ALL_BRANCHES`, `normaliseScope` and `scopePickerState`, holds no `flatMap` branch filtering and never filters the organisation list itself. One organisation with one branch renders no `select` element at all and states `Locking for: One Company - Main Branch`; one organisation with several branches renders the Branch list alone; several organisations render Organisation and Branch with the branch list filtered to the owning organisation; a branchless organisation prints the em dash.
- `scopePickerState` resolves a one-option branch automatically (`branches.length === 1`), clears a branch that belongs to another organisation (the regression covers the wider context where a retained branch of the previous organisation yields an empty `branchId`), and returns `organisationOptions`, `branchOptions`, `branchId`, `branchLabel`, `line` and `allBranches`. An empty scope returns the `no organisation selected` line instead of throwing.
- The Create Lock / Close drawer in `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` now renders the picker over the available working context, passing `organisations={scopeOrganisations}`, `companyIds={form.companyIds}` and `branchIds={form.branchIds}` with a `showLine={false}` prop and an `onChange` that writes both arrays straight into the lock form. The header chip prints the strip from `getScopeVisibility({organisations:scopeOrganisations,companyIds:form.companyIds,branchIds:form.branchIds,label:Locking for})`, so the stated scope and the selectors can never disagree. The Branch list keeps All branches as its first option, so a whole-organisation lock is never silently narrowed; the picker emits only on a user change, so `branchIds` stays empty until a branch is chosen. `createLock` still validates through `validateScope` and still feeds `createLockWindow`.
- The Request Unlock form renders the same control with `showFields={false}` and an explicit `strip`, stating the unlocking scope from the locked period. It is presented, not selected: `requestPeriodUnlock` derives the organisation, `scopeType` and `branchIds` from the locked period, so a selectable branch there would print a scope the approval cannot honour (temporary unlocks are enforced on the period date window, not per branch). Recorded as a deliberate limitation rather than faked - a branch-narrowed unlock request needs a new enforcement rule.
- The available organisation list is now shared. New `src/organisation-context.js` exports `readOrgSetting`, `readOrgList`, `getAccessibleOrganizations` and `getCurrentOrganizationContext`; `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` imports them instead of keeping its own copy (its three duplicated const lines and the now-unused `demoOrganizations` import were deleted) and `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` resolves `const scopeOrganisations=useMemo(()=>getAccessibleOrganizations(),[])`. Behaviour is byte-identical: the module holds the same expressions, including the empty-context fallback to the full organisation list and the storage try/catch.
- `src/period-closing.css` gained three rules for the control (a `.pc-scope-picker` column with a 10px gap and a 16px bottom margin, plus margin resets for the reused `.pc-lock-scope` chip and the `.pc-fields` grid); the picker reuses the page `.pc-field` and `.pc-fields` styles, so no new design language was introduced.

- The user decision: `remove pagination from period closing - Request page table and show branch and organization in a single separate column and status (Requested, Approved, Cancelled/Rejected) in Request section`, plus `remove 4 periods and 8 requests texts showing the right of Filter Button`.
- Pagination is gone, not hidden: `REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE`, `const [page,setPage]=useState(1)`, the `pageCount/active/visible` slice and the `nav.pc-request-pager` block all left `RequestList`, which now renders `<RequestTable requests={requests} periods={periods} actions={requestActions}/>` for every filtered request. `IconChevronLeft` / `IconChevronRight` left the page import with it, and the dead `.pc-request-pager` / `.pc-request-pager button` / `.pc-pager-status` rules were removed from `src/period-closing.css` along with `.pc-filter-count`, `.pc .pc-filters>.pc-filter-count` and `.pc .pc-register-bar .pc-filters>.pc-filter-count`.

The toggle badges (`pc-view-toggle em`) still carry each half's count, so no number was lost.
- `RequestTable({requests,periods=[],actions})` now carries seven columns - Period, one combined `Organisation & Branch` cell, Reason, Requested, Requested By, Status, Actions - replacing the old Period cell with its organisation `<small>` line.
- Two small pure helpers were added above the table. `requestScopeOf(request,periods)` finds the period the request belongs to, resolves the organisation name from `period.organisationName` / `request.organisationName` / `demoOrganizations` by code (falling back to the code itself, so a stored `ABC01` is never the display value) and reuses `periodBranchLabel()` on a synthesised scope object so the branch line reads `All branches`, a real `Kochi Branch, Bengaluru Branch` list, or `All branches` again when no period matches - never raw branch ids. `requestStatusLabel(request)` maps the stored `Pending approval` to `Requested` and passes every other stored status through unchanged, so no engine vocabulary changed. `LockRegister` now passes `periods` into `RequestList`, which passes it to the table.
- The Status cell reuses the page's canonical `span.pc-badge` (`{'pc-badge '+status.toLowerCase()}`) so the register keeps one pill vocabulary, with the three tone rules added in `src/period-closing-focus.css`: `.requested` amber (`#fff4df` / `#8a5d08`), `.approved` green (`#e4f5ec` / `#19734c`), `.cancelled` and `.rejected` red (`#ffe9ec` / `#b7374b`). The same file re-specifies the seven column widths (12 / 19 / 21 / 11 / 11 / 12 / 14%) in place of the old five-column rhythm and drops the pager gutter rule; the 1100px block already reset every `nth-child` width, so narrow screens are unchanged.
- One adjacent defect was fixed while touching the same table: the Branch filter was rendered in the Requests half but `filterRequests()` ignored it. It now applies `branchMatch` exactly as `filterPeriods()` does.

It also asserts the combined header and that a row prints `ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd` with its branch line.

## Period Lock request row actions checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The user decision: `from period closing - Request page table show view button and more action (3dot - Approve, Cancel, View Details, Delete) instead of showing Approve Unlock, Cancel Request and 3 dot`. The Actions cell of the Requests table is now the same shape on every row - one read-only `View` button plus one 3-dot menu - instead of an emphasised inline `Approve Unlock` with a requester-only inline `Cancel request` beside it.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx`: `RequestActions` lost its three early returns and now has one `return` for every row: `<button type="button" className="pc-request-view" onClick={()=>onDetails(request)}>View</button>` followed by the existing `details.pc-kebab.pc-request-kebab` menu, which gained `<button type="button" className="pc-kebab-danger" onClick={pick(()=>onDeleteRequest(request))}><IconTrash size={15}/>Delete</button>` after `View Details`. `IconTrash` joined the `@tabler/icons-react` import. The gating collapsed into one line - `canDecide=canApprove&&decidable&&pending`, `canCancel=self&&pending` (unchanged), `canDelete=self||canApprove` - and the two explanatory notes became one `note` value rendered as `span.pc-action-note`: empty on a decided row, `Approval requires an approver role.` when the acting role cannot approve, and `Another approver must review your own request.` when there is no second approver. Both notes still explain a missing menu entry, so no validation copy was lost.
- The page now deletes through the engine: `const deleteRequest=request=>{try{save(deleteUnlockRequest(data,{requestId:request.id,user:activeRole}));setBanner('Unlock request for '+request.period+' deleted.')}catch(e){setBanner(e.message)}}`, and `onDeleteRequest` is threaded through `LockRegister` and `RequestsDrawer` into `RequestList` and then `RequestActions`, exactly like `onCancelRequest`.
- `src/period-locking.js` gained `export function deleteUnlockRequest(state,{requestId,user='Accountant',now})`. It refuses an unknown id (`Unlock request not found.`) and any role that is neither the requester nor an approver (`Only the requester or an approver can delete this unlock request.`), then - because deleting a live request must never strand the period - it runs a `Pending approval` request through `cancelUnlockRequest` first (restoring the previous Hard Locked / Soft Locked status and writing the `Unlock cancelled` entry), removes the record from `state.requests` and prepends an `Unlock request deleted` audit entry. A decided or expired request is removed without touching its outcome.
- `src/period-closing.css`: the dead `.pc .pc-request-actions button.pc-quiet` and `.pc .pc-request-actions button.primary` rules went with the buttons they styled (`pc-quiet` no longer appears anywhere in `src/`), replaced by `button.pc-request-view` (white, `#e1e6ed` border, `#475467` ink, `#b9c9e3` / `#f8faff` / `#245fd9` hover - neutral, never the primary blue) and `.pc .pc-request-kebab-menu button.pc-kebab-danger` (`#b42318` ink with a `#fff5f6` hover), the one destructive row in the menu. The group comment above `.pc .pc-request-actions` now describes the one-cell-shape rule.

## Period Lock attention pill and request row checkpoint - 2026-09-14 (superseded in part by the request row actions checkpoint above)

- The user decisions: `remove "Recorded as Business Owner, the next authorised approver." from period closing - Request page table`, and `Before you close · 3 checks need attention · 4 entries affected` must stop being a full-width row - it becomes a small attention button in the header action group, left of `More actions` with a slightly wider gap so the pill reads as status and the two controls beside it read as actions, amber for three or more checks (red when a check blocks the close) and neutral grey for one or two, never the primary blue, never dismissible, no trailing chevron, one computed value behind the pill, the popover and the count, `aria-live="polite"` on the count region, a pinned header row, and no reserved height once the row collapses.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx`: `function RequestActions({request,canApprove,approver='',role,onDecide,onCancelRequest,onDetails})` no longer renders the reviewer note - the `{self&&<span className="pc-action-note">{'Recorded as '+approver+', the next authorised approver.'}</span>}` span left the final `return` - so a pending self-raised request now shows the emphasised `Approve Unlock`, the requester-only quiet `Cancel request` and its 3-dot menu and nothing else. `approver` still feeds `decidable=!self||Boolean(approver)`, `Another approver must review your own request.` and `Approval requires an approver role.` are untouched, and the decision is still recorded under the resolved approver because the page still threads `unlockApprover` in.
- The old full-width `<button className={'pc-attention '+...}>` strip left the page body. In its place one memo is the whole feature: `const attention=useMemo(()=>{const failed=readiness.checks.filter(check=>check.state!=='Passed'),blocking=failed.filter(check=>check.state==='Failed').length;return {checks:failed,count:failed.length,tone:blocking?'danger':failed.length>=3?'warn':failed.length?'neutral':'ok',noun:'check'+(failed.length===1?'':'s'),verb:failed.length===1?'needs':'need',entries:impact.transactionImpactCount||0}},[readiness,impact])`, so the pill, its tone, the popover summary, the noun/verb pairing and the count can never disagree. `attentionChecks` and the `pc-more-badge` count are deleted.
- The pill is `details.pc-attention-pop > summary.pc-attention-pill`, rendered only while `attention.count>0` and as the first child of `div.pc-heading-actions`: `className={'pc-attention-pill '+attention.tone}`, `IconAlertTriangle size={15}`, `span.pc-attention-count[aria-live="polite"]` printing `N checks` as text (never a badge, never a bare number), `aria-label={'Review '+count+' '+noun+' that '+verb+' attention'}`, `title="Before you close"` and no chevron, because it opens a popover rather than navigating.
- Its `div.pc-attention-pop-menu[role=region][aria-label="Before you close"]` holds `div.pc-attention-pop-head` (`<b>Before you close</b>` plus `N checks need attention · M entries affected`, or `All checks passed` at zero), then `<Checklist readiness onNavigate only/>` - `function Checklist({readiness,onNavigate,describe=true,only=false})` gained `only`, which filters `Passed` out so one renderer serves the popover and the pre-close drawer - then `button.pc-attention-pop-all` (`Review all` + `IconArrowRight`, wired to `menuRun(openBeforeClose)`). No close action lives in the popover, because `Create Lock / Close` is the page's only close entry point. `Checklist`'s small line now reads `${count} item${s} ${count===1?'needs':'need'} attention. `, and `openMenus()` queries `.pc-more[open],.pc-kebab[open],.pc-filters-more[open],.pc-attention-pop[open]`, so the pill closes on an outside pointerdown or Escape like every other disclosure.
- CSS: `src/period-closing.css` replaced the five `.pc .pc-attention{...}` rules with the pill block - `.pc .pc-attention-pop{position:relative;display:flex;flex:none;margin-right:7px}` (the wider status gap), `.pc-attention-pill` (999px radius, 40px minimum height, `padding:0 13px`, 13px/650 nowrap label, `#f4f6f9` / `#e2e6ed` / `#4a5468` neutral, hover, focus-visible ring, hidden `::-webkit-details-marker`), `.pc-attention-pill.warn` (`rgba(234,88,12,.07)` / `#f0d3b8` / `#9a3412`), `.pc-attention-pill.danger` (`rgba(220,38,38,.06)` / `#f3c9cd` / `#b42318`), `.pc-attention-count{font-variant-numeric:tabular-nums}` and the popover shell (`min(400px,92vw)`, `top:calc(100% + 8px)`, `right:0`, `z-index:30`) with `.pc-checklist` flattened to borderless rows and its `small` kept at the 12px floor. Primary blue is deliberately absent: it stays on `Create Lock / Close`.
- `src/period-closing-focus.css` appended - after the `.pc-focused>.pc-heading` reset, which it must stay after in source order - `.pc-focused>.pc-heading{position:sticky!important;top:0!important;z-index:26!important;background:#f7f9fc!important;padding:10px 0 12px!important;box-shadow:0 1px 0 #e6ebf3}`, pinning the heading row inside the page's own `.pc` scroller so the count stays visible while the register scrolls. Because the pill is a `details`, collapsing it returns the row height and the register moves back up.
- Contract tests updated: `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` **27 passed, 0 failed** - the register contract renamed to `...compact pre-close attention pill`, its two `pc-more-badge` assertions replaced, `opens from the attention pill` for the new `openMenus()` selector, and a new 27th contract `the attention pill states the count once and opens a read-only review popover` - and `tests/journal-actions-ui.test.mjs` **5 passed, 0 failed**, because its dismissal contract now expects the four-selector `document.querySelectorAll('.pc-more[open],.pc-kebab[open],.pc-filters-more[open],.pc-attention-pop[open]')` string. That second suite is the one real defect this change caused: it was failing on the old three-selector string until the expectation was widened.
- Harnesses kept honest: `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` (already failing on a now-singular `1 entry affected`) asserts the pill is hidden and that `Before you close` / `All checks passed` never reach the page while every check passes, then `pc-attention-pill neutral` + `aria-live="polite"` + `>1 check<` + `Review 1 check that needs attention` + `<b>Before you close</b>` + `1 check needs attention` + `/\d+ entr(?:y|ies) affected/` + `Review all`, then `pc-attention-pill danger` + `No draft journal entries` for a draft journal, and finally that `className="pc-attention ` never comes back; `tmp/pc15-requests.mjs` now asserts the row renders neither `Recorded as` nor any `pc-action-note` and logs the action cell instead of the note; `tmp/pc15-probe.jsx` was re-synced to the live source.

- `function RequestTable({requests,actions})` is now `div.pc-table-fit pc-request-table > table` - the same fit shell the locked-periods table above it uses, so the register never scrolls sideways and the row menu is never clipped - with exactly five columns: Period (the period name plus the organisation as its small line), Reason, Requested, Requested By, Actions. - `function RequestActions({request,canApprove,approver='',role,onDecide,onCancelRequest,onDetails})` renders that whole Actions cell and keeps `decidable=!self||Boolean(approver)`, both explanatory notes and, at that checkpoint, the `Recorded as <approver>` line - that line was removed in the attention pill checkpoint above - adding `pending=request.status==='Pending approval'` so a pending decidable row shows the emphasised `Approve Unlock` then the requester-only quiet `Cancel request`, while a decided row shows the menu alone. `Reject` and the status chip remain absent.
- The row menu is `details.pc-kebab.pc-request-kebab > summary` with `IconDots size={17}` and `div.pc-kebab-menu.pc-request-kebab-menu` holding `Approve`, `Cancel` and `View Details` (`IconEye`, added to the `@tabler/icons-react` import), so it reuses the canonical 3-dot geometry, the local `pick` close-on-select helper and the page's existing outside-pointerdown / Escape dismissal, because `openMenus()` already queries `.pc-kebab[open]`.
- `function requestScopeLabel(request)` resolves `Selected date range` once, and the new `function RequestDetailsDrawer({request,onClose})` renders one unlock request as `button.pc-history-scrim` + `aside.pc-history-drawer.pc-request-drawer` (`aria-label="Unlock request details"`) whose `pc-drawer-body` holds `dl.pc-request-facts` with Requested By, Requested, Reason, Unlock scope, Approver and then Unlock duration, Access until and Approver remarks only when present. `RequestList` owns `const [detail,setDetail]=useState(null)` and portals it through `div.pc pc-portal-scope`.

## Requests register cards-or-list view checkpoint - 2026-09-14 (superseded in full by the request table checkpoint above)

- The user decision: `in period closing page, Request section Shows in list view and Card vieew`. The `Requests` half of the locked-periods register now renders the same unlock requests as cards or as one compact list, chosen from a new right-aligned `div.pc-request-view-bar` that reuses the existing `.pc-view-toggle` control (`aria-label="Show requests as cards or as a list"`, one `type="button"` per view carrying `aria-pressed` and an `.active` class). The bar renders only while the register actually holds requests (`{requests.length>0&&...}`) and the choice lives in one `const [requestLayout,setRequestLayout]=useState('cards')` inside `LockRegister`, so it is presentation state only: no new storage key, service change or engine contract.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` kept the card markup exactly as it was and added `layout` to `RequestList`. The return is now `<div className={'pc-request-list'+(layout==='list'?' pc-request-list-table':'')}>{layout==='list'?<RequestTable requests={visible} actions={requestActions}/>:visible.map(...cards...)}{pageCount>1&&<nav className="pc-request-pager" ...>}`. The six-per-page pager, the `REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE=6` constant, the empty state and the two label variants stay shared, so paging behaves identically in both views and the pager still appears only past one page. The register's `RequestList` now receives `layout={requestLayout}`; the approvals drawer (`RequestsDrawer`) still calls `RequestList` with no layout and therefore keeps its cards.
- The per-request decision markup moved into one shared `function RequestActions({request,canApprove,approver='',role,onDecide,onCancelRequest})` declared above `RequestList`, so the card footer and the list row can never drift apart: it keeps the exact `decidable=!self||Boolean(approver)` rule, the `Another approver must review your own request.` note, the `Approval requires an approver role.` note, the `Recorded as <approver>, the next authorised approver.` reviewer line, the quiet `Cancel request` and the single emphasised `Approve Unlock`. The card footer is now `{isPending&&<footer>{requestActions(request)}</footer>}`; the decided row of the list shows a muted `pc-request-none` dash instead of an empty cell. No `Reject` action and no status chip exist in either view.
- The list view is `function RequestTable({requests,actions})`, a `<div className="pc-table-scroll pc-request-table"><table>` so a narrow viewport scrolls instead of squeezing the columns. Its seven columns are Period, Requested By, Reason, Unlock Scope, Requested, Approver and Actions, and it keeps every data point the card showed: the organisation as the Period cell's secondary line, `Duration <value>` and `Access until <date>` under the unlock scope, and the approver remarks under the approver, with the scope resolved by the same `Selected date range` check as the card. It deliberately does not carry the `pc-table-fit` class, so the locked-period register's `th:nth-child(n)` width rules and the `pc-locks-panel .pc-table-fit` overrides cannot reach it.
- `src/period-closing-focus.css` owns the new geometry: `.pc .pc-request-view-bar{display:flex;justify-content:flex-end;padding:14px 18px 12px}`, `.pc .pc-request-list-table{padding:0}` with `.pc .pc-request-list-table .pc-request-pager{padding:12px 18px 14px}`, `.pc .pc-request-list-table .pc-table-scroll{overflow-x:auto}`, `.pc .pc-request-table table{min-width:1040px}`, `th` kept `nowrap` while `td` becomes `white-space:normal;vertical-align:top` (overriding the `.pc th,.pc td{white-space:nowrap}` base), a 170px first column, a right-aligned last column, the seven proportional widths (17/12/21/16/11/12%), `overflow-wrap:anywhere` on the reason cell, right-aligned actions inside the cell and `.pc .pc-request-none{color:#98a2b3}`. All text stays at or above the 12px floor.

- Note for the next agent: `tmp/pc15-probe.jsx` is a snapshot of `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` with `../src/...` specifiers and an added export line, so it must be regenerated whenever that page changes (`RequestTable` was added to its export list). `tmp/pc16-requests-html.mjs` prints the card markup from the same probe.

## Period Lock request decisions, pagination, lock type and More actions checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The `Requests` half of the register no longer prints a per-request status. `RequestList` in `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` stopped rendering the `pc-badge` span that echoed `request.status` in the card header, so a card header now carries only the period and the organisation. A pending request is identified by its decision footer, which was already the only place it could be actioned; the card still keeps its `pending` / `decided` class.
- The reject option is gone. The footer used to offer `Reject` beside `Approve Unlock` plus a second, separate `Cancel request` row for the requester. `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` now renders one `.pc-request-actions` group holding `Cancel request` (class `pc-quiet`, requester only) and `Approve Unlock` (class `primary`, carrying `IconCheck`) as the last item, so approving is the single emphasised action and withdrawing is the quiet secondary one. `onDecide` is only ever called with `'Approved'` from the list. `decideUnlockRequest(state, {requestId, decision, user, reason})` in `src/period-locking.js` still accepts `'Rejected'` and no engine code, storage key or audit entry changed; `REQUEST_STATUS_FILTERS` still lists `Rejected` so the register filter keeps matching any request already stored with that status - that constant is the one remaining place the removed action is named.
- The request list pages six at a time. `REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE = 6` and a `useState(1)` page number live inside `RequestList`; `pageCount` / `active` / `visible` derive from them, a list that fits one page renders whole, and `pageCount > 1` renders `<nav className="pc-request-pager" aria-label="Unlock request pages">` with `Previous`, `Page <n> of <n>` and `Next` (the step buttons disable at each end). `src/period-closing.css` gained `.pc .pc-request-actions`, `.pc .pc-request-actions button.pc-quiet` (+ its hover), `.pc .pc-request-actions button.primary`, `.pc-request-pager`, `.pc-request-pager button` and `.pc-pager-status`; every new label is 12px, so the AGENTS.md minimum text size holds.
- Hard Lock / Soft Lock no longer reaches the screen. The `Lock type` row (`<dt>Lock type</dt>` plus the raw `period.lockType`) left the `pc-detail-summary` list in `DetailsDrawer`. The engine still validates `['Soft Lock','Hard Lock']` in `lockPeriod`, `newForm` / `newLockForm` still default `lockType:'Hard Lock'`, and the `Closed Type` filter (`CLOSED_TYPES = ['All','Manual','Automatic']`) stays, because Auto/Manual is the product's own lock vocabulary; no lock, journal, audit or storage behaviour changed.
- The `More actions` button is restyled. `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` renders an `IconChevronDown` with class `pc-more-caret` after the label, and `src/period-closing.css` gained a transition on the base `summary` rule plus `.pc-more>summary:hover`, `.pc-more>summary:focus-visible`, `.pc-more-caret`, `.pc-more>summary:hover .pc-more-caret`, `.pc-more[open]>summary` (now also `background:#f2f7ff` and `box-shadow:0 0 0 3px #eaf1ff`) and `.pc-more[open]>summary .pc-more-caret{transform:rotate(180deg)}`, so the disclosure hovers and focuses like every other button and the open state reads as selected. The box parity asserted by `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` is untouched - the base rule still carries `flex:1 1 auto`, `min-height:40px`, `border-radius:7px`, `padding:0 15px`, `gap:7px`, `font-size:14px`, `font-weight:600` and `white-space:nowrap`, matching `.pc-focused>.pc-heading-actions button`. The menu now anchors with `top:calc(100% + 6px)`, caps at `max-height:min(70vh,420px)` with `overflow-y:auto` instead of growing past the viewport, and its rows gained `.pc-more-menu button:focus-visible` and an icon colour transition.
- Disclosure menus now dismiss themselves. A native details element only closes when its own `summary` is clicked, so `PeriodClosing` gained one `useEffect` that listens for `pointerdown` (closing whichever of `.pc-more`, `.pc-kebab` and `.pc-filters-more` is open and does not contain the event target) and for `keydown` `Escape` (closing all three), and removes both listeners on unmount. `useEffect` was added to the existing `react` import. This is a behaviour change on three existing menus, not only on `More actions`.

## Period Lock header chip removal, request decisions and lock history removal checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The inline read-only locking policy chip left the Period Lock heading. `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` no longer renders the `pc-policy-chip` button - the `chip=policyChip(settings)` binding and the `policyChip` import went with it - and the single-child `<div className="pc-title-row">` wrapper was deleted, so `pc-title-block` now holds only `<h1>{t.periodClosing}</h1>` above the journal-style `pc-heading-note` subheading. The `Period closing settings` entry in `More actions` carries the static sub-label `Locking schedule and unlock approval` instead of the old chip text, so no `Auto-lock: ...` / `Manual locking` / `Locking off` string renders anywhere on the page. Dead rules were removed from `src/period-closing.css` (`.pc-policy-chip`, `.pc-policy-chip.off`, `.pc-policy-chip svg`, the 650px chip media rule, `.pc-title-row`) and from `src/period-closing-focus.css` (`.pc-focused>.pc-heading .pc-policy-chip` and the 650px title-row/chip block). `policyChip()` still exists in the engine and is still covered by `tests/period-scope-visibility.test.mjs`; only the page stopped rendering it.
- The lock history surface is gone, so the filtered register is the only lock list. Removed: the `Audit log / History` entry in the `More actions` menu, the `panel==='history'` portal with its `pc-history-wide` drawer, `openHistory`, the `historyFilters` state, the `HISTORY_FILTERS` constant, the `allLockRows` memo, `lockMeta`, and the then-unused `closedCount`, `pendingRequests` and `lastClosed` memos with the `lastClosedPeriod` import. `src/period-closing.css` lost `.pc-history-wide`, its drawer-body rules and the two responsive overrides. The `title` / `note` props on `LockRegister` remain supported, but no caller passes them, so the register bar renders the `Locked Periods` / `Requests` toggle plus its filters only. A period's audit trail is still reachable per row through `View Audit Log` and the details drawer, so no audit access was lost.
- Unlock requests are now decidable from the `Requests` half of the register. `RequestList` already rendered `Reject` / `Approve Unlock`, but only when the acting role was not the requester, and the acting role is always `allowedRoles[0]` (`Admin` by default) - the same role that raises requests - so the card dead-ended on `Another approver must review your own request.` and offered only `Cancel request`. The engine gained `UNLOCK_APPROVER_ORDER` and `unlockApproverFor(requestedBy, settings)`, which returns the first of `['Business Owner','Admin']` that is neither the requester nor excluded by `allowedRoles` and that can `approve-unlock`; `submitUnlockRequest` now reuses that helper for its daily auto-approval instead of its own inline lookup, so the rule exists once. `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` resolves `unlockApprover = unlockApproverFor(activeRole, settings)`, threads it through `LockRegister` and `RequestsDrawer` into `RequestList` as `approver`, renders the decision buttons when `canApprove && (!self || approver)`, states `Recorded as <approver>, the next authorised approver.` when the acting role raised the request itself, and `decideRequest` records the decision under that alternate approver. With no other allowed approver the explanatory note and the `Cancel request` action are unchanged, and a role without `approve-unlock` still reads `Approval requires an approver role.` The reviewer note renders before the buttons and `.pc-request-card>footer` now wraps (`flex-wrap:wrap` with `.pc-request-card>footer .pc-action-note{flex:1 1 100%;white-space:normal;text-align:right}`), so a long `Recorded as ...` line takes its own row above the decision and cancel buttons instead of overflowing the 520px drawer.
- The pre-close review drawer lost its duplicated text block: the inner `<header>` holding the second `<h2>Before you close</h2>`, the sentence `The system verifies the following before <period> can be closed.` and the `pc-ready-pill` readiness badge (`Cannot close yet`, `Warnings to review`, `All checks passed`) was deleted, so the drawer body runs straight from the entries-affected count and the wrapped category chips into the checklist. The drawer's own header (`Pre-close review`, `<h2>Before you close</h2>`, the period and scope line) is untouched, and `.pc-ready-pill` plus its three state rules left `src/period-closing.css`. The separate Close Period modal subtitle (`Before closing, the system verifies the following.`) was not in scope and is unchanged.
- Validation actually executed: `node tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` **23 passed, 0 failed** (rewritten contracts: the heading carries no policy chip, the lock history surface is deleted, the register reports its request decisions, and the pre-close drawer keeps only the chips plus the checklist); `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs` **16 passed, 0 failed** (the page-keeps-the-chip assertion is now inverted); `tests/period-locking.test.mjs` **27 passed, 0 failed**, including one new engine test that asserts a request raised by `Admin` is refused for `Admin`, allowed for `Business Owner`, that `unlockApproverFor('Admin', {allowedRoles:['Admin']})` returns nobody, and that the approved decision records `Business Owner`; `tests/period-scope-visibility.test.mjs` and `tests/period-unlock-scope.test.mjs` pass unchanged.
- Runtime evidence: server-rendered the live page (`node tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs`, 6803 chars) and every internal surface from a probe generated from the current source (`node tmp/pc15-requests.mjs`). ## Account Group search picker and Period Lock settings heading trim checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- Create Account / Edit Account (`src/EnterpriseAccountForm.jsx`) now picks the Account Group through a searchable combobox instead of a native `<select>` with `<optgroup>`s - the same control the Journal lines account cell already uses. The picker is `src/AccountGroupPicker.jsx` and its pure matching logic is `src/account-group-search.js`, holding `accountGroupKey`, `matchesAccountGroup`, `flattenAccountGroups`, `accountGroupRows` and `selectedAccountGroup` outside the component the way `src/journal-templates.js` holds `matchesAccountQuery` for the journal picker, so the rules are testable without a DOM.
- The control keeps the existing contract: `optionId` derives `agp-option-<type>-<purpose>` from the same `type::purpose` key the drawer already used (`${type}::${purpose}`), and `onChange` splits that key back into `applyGroup(nextType,nextPurpose)`. `groupOptions`, `applyGroup`, the fallback group for an unknown type/purpose and every downstream `form.type` / `form.purpose` write are unchanged, so account creation, editing, validation and posting are untouched.
- The list is grouped by account type, each option showing its label with its purpose as the meta line. A query matches label, account type, purpose and group name case-insensitively, and a section that loses every option is dropped, leaving the two empty states `No account group matches “<query>”.` and `No account group is available.` The search box is `aria-label="Search account group"`; the trigger and the search box are both `role="combobox"` pointing at `#agp-listbox`; options are `role="option"` with `aria-selected`; and Arrow/Enter/Escape, hover, outside-`pointerdown` close and `scrollIntoView({block:'nearest'})` behave exactly as in the journal picker.
- Two deliberate departures from the journal picker exist because the drawer renders this field inside `label.am-field`, and both are pinned by `tests/account-group-picker-ui.test.mjs`: the search wrapper is a `div` rather than a `label` (a label may not contain another label, and the input is already named by its `aria-label`), and the picker root carries `onClick={event=>event.preventDefault()}`. A label's activation behaviour fires a click on its labelled control - here the trigger button - for a click that lands on a non-interactive descendant such as an option row, which would have reopened the list immediately after a selection. The visible `Account Group *` text sits outside that root, so clicking it still opens the picker through the same label association. No DOM library is installed in this environment, so this was reasoned from the label activation rules and pinned in the suite rather than reproduced in a browser.
- `src/account-group-picker.css` mirrors the `.je-account-*` rules in `src/journal-form.css` under `.agp*` class names, `!important` flags included: `.am-create-drawer .am-field input{width:100%;min-height:40px;padding:9px 11px;border:1px solid #d5deea}` in `src/account-create-drawer.css` would otherwise restyle the search box and its focus ring - the same reason the journal picker overrides its host rules. Every text size is 12px or 13px, so the AGENTS.md minimum is met without the journal picker's legacy 10.5px / 12.5px values.
- The Period Lock Settings popup (`src/PeriodLockSettings.jsx`) lost the read-only text block the brief asked to remove: the `Automatic locking` card heading (`<h2>`), its `Periods lock on this schedule, and a locked period refuses new or modified postings. Switch it off to create every lock by hand.` description, and the `policyChip()` label (`Auto-lock: ...` / `Manual locking` / `Locking off`) that sat on the card header. The panel now runs header (`<h1>Period Lock Settings</h1>` plus the one-sentence lead) -> warnings -> one `section.pls-card.wide` that opens directly with the `Enable automatic locking` switch -> sticky footer. `policyChip` is no longer imported or called here; the engine export stays because the Period Lock page heading still renders the chip through it, and `src/period-lock-settings.css` lost its now-dead `.pls-popup .pls-card>header h2{font-size:15px}` rule. Removing the heading changed no setting, no stored value and no lock.
- Investigated and dismissed this pass: `src/period-lock-settings.css` resets `.pls header{position:static;inset:auto;...}` to neutralise the legacy app-bar rule, and since `.pls-popup` is portalled inside `aside.pc-history-drawer.pc-settings-drawer`, itself inside the page's `.pls` element, that reset also matches the drawer's own `<header>`. It changes nothing: `src/period-closing.css` already declares `.pc-history-drawer>header{position:static!important;inset:auto!important;z-index:auto!important;width:auto!important;height:auto!important;min-height:0!important;max-width:none!important;margin:0!important;...}`, because the drawer header is intentionally a static flex row with `.pc-drawer-body` as the only scroll container. No CSS was changed.

## Period Lock settings popup consolidation checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- `src/PeriodLockSettings.jsx` was restructured from three cards into one. The separate `Period locking` card and the separate `Unlock approval` card are gone; the surviving `Automatic locking` card (`<h2>Automatic locking</h2>`) is now the master control and carries the read-only policy chip, the two switches and the schedule. The card description states the outcome (`Periods lock on this schedule, and a locked period refuses new or modified postings. Switch it off to create every lock by hand.`) so the removed card's meaning is not lost.
- The two switches are `label.pls-switch` rows built by one local `Switch` helper: text span, visually hidden `input[type=checkbox][role=switch]`, decorative `<i>` track. `Enable automatic locking` writes only `patch({lockingMode:next?'Automatic':'Manual'})`, and `Require approval to unlock` writes `patch({approval:next})`. The schedule fields keep `disabled={scheduleField}` and `pls-fields-off`.
- `lockingMode:'Off'` is no longer reachable from the popup, so the master switch renders `disabled={!lockingOn}` with `pls-switch-off` (55% opacity) and the note `Period locking is switched off in Accounting settings, so no lock runs anywhere and every lock control stays hidden. Turn period locking on there to configure this schedule.` When the mode is `Manual` with locking on, the note is the original `Manual locking is active, so these schedule fields stay disabled until automatic locking is switched on.` The switch sub-label is computed once as `autoNote`.
- The collapsed `More policy settings` `<details>` was deleted whole, not re-homed. Frequency boundaries, organisation timezone, default scope, allowed roles, module exemptions and the read-only enforcement coverage list are no longer rendered; the popup is now the header, the warnings block (which still carries the locked-forever confirmation checkbox and the daily auto-approval offer), one card, the sticky footer and the discard guard. `normalisePeriodSettings` still supplies `defaultScope`, `allowedRoles` and `exemptModules`, so no stored value, no lock and no enforcement path changed.
- One structural defect was caught and fixed during verification: the first cut left the closing `</section>` before the `pls-rule` divider, so the approval switch and its note rendered as siblings of the card instead of rows inside it. `tmp/pc13-card-check.mjs` now renders the popup in Automatic, Manual and Off and asserts that there is exactly one `<section>`, that it contains both switch rows and the divider, and that nothing renders between the card and the footer; the source suite pins the same contract by slicing the card markup. Result: `CARD NESTING OK` in all three modes.
- Code removed with it: the `MODE_COPY` constant, the unused `scope`/`setScope` locals, and the `IconArrowLeft`, `IconShieldCheck`, `LOCKING_MODES`, `PERIOD_MODULES`, `PERIOD_ROLE_OPTIONS` and `ENFORCED_OPERATIONS` imports. `export function ScopeSelector` stays (it is the single consumer of `scopeVisibility`), but the popup no longer renders `<ScopeSelector`, and `PERIOD_MODULES` is still exported and used by the engine and by `tests/period-locking.test.mjs`.
- `src/period-lock-settings.css` gained the toggle idiom (`.pls-switch` as a 58px row with `justify-content:space-between`, `.pls-switch>i` 38x22 track, `input:checked+i`, a `focus-visible` ring on the track, `.pls-switch-off`) plus `.pls-rule` for the divider between the schedule group and the approval switch, and lost every `.pls-advanced` rule (7 declarations) that belonged to the removed disclosure.
- Validation actually executed: `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs` **16 passed, 0 failed** (rewritten to the new contract: one card, two switches, the five schedule fields with no boundary fields, the chip on the card header, `role="switch"` and the track styling, and the removal of `pls-advanced`); `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` **21 passed, 0 failed**; `period-locking` **26/26**; `period-scope-visibility` **16/16**; `period-unlock-scope` **9/9**; `ui-quality` **5/5**. Harnesses: `tmp/pc12-pls-dump.mjs` renders the popup in Automatic (6184 chars, schedule and both switches), Manual (3171 chars, inert fields plus the manual note, no schedule) and Off (3297 chars, `pls-switch-off` and the Accounting settings note); `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` `SSR OK - 8293 chars`; `tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` `DRAWER SSR OK - 12 surfaces`; `tmp/pc5-validate.mjs` `STRUCTURE OK - 17 surfaces`; `tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` `48/48 jsx files transformed` with the 4 pre-existing `LEFTOVER JSX?` warnings; `tmp/pc13-card-check.mjs` `CARD NESTING OK` for all three modes.

## Period Lock register toolbar, current period popup and settings polish checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The page subheading now reads `Lock accounting periods and control when postings stop.`; the trailing sentence `Every close, reopen and override is written to the audit trail.` was removed from `pc-heading-note`.
- `LockRegister` now renders one compact toolbar row inside `<div className="pc-register-bar">`: the `Locked Periods` / `Requests` toggle (and, in the history drawer, the `pc-register-heading` title block) sit on the left, and the right side holds the reused `.pc-filters` row with the search box, the `Filters` disclosure and the row count. `Period` and `Status` moved inside the `.pc-filters-panel`, ahead of `Financial Year`, `Organisation`, `Branch`, `Closed By` and `Closed Type`, so the visible row is only the toggle, the search box and `Filters`. The `advanced` badge now counts `Status` and `Period` as well, so the badge matches the filters that are actually set.
- The toolbar is a `<div>` on purpose. The legacy global app-bar rules in `src/styles.css` are element selectors - `header label{width:min(320px,27vw);height:38px;display:flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;background:#f6f8fb;padding:0 12px}`, `header input{border:0;outline:0;background:none;width:100%}` and `header>button{height:38px;border:1px solid #e5eaf1;background:white;border-radius:6px}` - and they apply to every `header` in the app, so moving the filter labels into a header would have forced them to 38px with a grey background and horizontal padding.
- The same stylesheet hides close buttons: `@media(max-width:1050px){header>button:not(.primary):not(.hamb){display:none}}`. Every Period Lock drawer header and every `.pc-modal` header holds a direct child close button, so below 1050px those buttons silently disappeared. Restored explicitly with `.pc-history-drawer>header>button,.pc-modal>section>header>button{display:inline-flex!important}` in `src/period-closing.css` and `.pls-popup .pls-close{display:inline-flex!important}` in `src/period-lock-settings.css` - the same `!important` display restoration the organisation switcher already used.
- `CurrentPeriodPanel` left the page body. It is now the `panel==='current'` right-side drawer (`pc-current-drawer`, 520px), opened from the first `More actions` entry `Current accounting period`, whose sub-label is `currentMeta` (`<period> · <status>`). The drawer renders the real card as its body - the `Current accounting period` title and status eyebrow, the period name, the `01 Sept 2026 – 30 Sept 2026 · ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd · All branches` line, the status badge and the one status-dependent action (`Lock this period`, `Request Unlock`, `Admin override`, or the reopened/unlocked note) plus `View details`. Inside the drawer the card is flattened: `display:flex!important` beats the 900px grid reflow, the duplicate eyebrow is hidden under the drawer title and the period line wraps instead of truncating. `CurrentPeriodPanel` is now a named export so the render harnesses exercise the real component instead of a stale copy.
- Period Lock Settings popup polish: the close button is restored, cards use `padding:16px 18px` with a 12px internal gap, switches get a hover state and tighter padding, schedule rows, the `More policy settings` summary and the footer were tightened, the sticky heading and footer gained a separation shadow, every surface shares the 22px gutter (16px below 760px), and the `@media(max-width:760px)` block reflows the grid, the warnings, the heading and the footer together.

## Period Lock register views and single locking switch checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The locked-periods register header now holds nothing but the `Locked Periods` / `Requests` toggle (`pc-view-toggle`) as its first, left-hand child. The `Locked periods` / `Unlock requests` heading and the `Every lock for <organisation> in the selected financial year.` / `Every reopen request for <organisation>, newest first.` notes were removed from the main register; `LockRegister` still renders that heading block for the lock history drawer, which passes `title` and `note` and no `onView`, and the header itself only renders when it has something to show (`.pc-register-heading` is the new class). `title` and `note` now default to `''`.
- Row visibility moved into the engine as `lockRegisterRows(periods,{mode='Manual',today='',createdIds=[]}={})` and is consumed once by the `historyRows` memo: `Automatic` keeps only locks whose period has already ended (`period.end<today` and a `Closed` display status, so future locks never appear), `Manual` keeps only periods present in `createdIds`, and `Off` hides nothing. `createdLockIds` is derived from the creation audit entries (`entry.periodId` where `entry.action` is `Lock period created` or `Period closed`), so the seeded demonstration locks stay out of the manual register and a lock appears as soon as the user creates or closes one - and survives a reload, because the audit trail is persisted with the period state. The lock history drawer (`allLockRows`, filtered from `data.periods`) is deliberately unaffected.
- `manual` and `lockMode` moved above the register memos so the `historyRows` memo can read them, `mode={lockMode}` is passed into `LockRegister` from the page, and the manual empty state is `No locks created yet. Use Create Lock / Close to lock a period for every module.` (the generic `No periods match these filters.` remains the default for the history drawer and for filtered-out rows). Both the table empty row and the mobile card empty paragraph use the same `emptyText` value.
- The register `Period` select now lists the period names the register can actually show (`rows`, or `requests` in the requests half) plus the current `filters.period`, so a hidden future lock can no longer be offered as a filter that can never match. The now-unused `source` local was removed with it.
- Period Lock Settings leads with one sentence - `Locking applies to every module in Wayvida Books, and changes apply from the next unclosed period.` - replacing the two-sentence `Locking schedule and unlock approval...` paragraph. The `Unlock approval` card is now the `Require approval to unlock` switch alone: the `Unlock approval role` select was deleted, and the `Unlock defaults` card (default unlock duration, maximum unlock duration, unlock scope, re-lock automatically on expiry, auto-approve daily lock unlocks) was deleted from `More policy settings`. The engine keeps `normalisePeriodSettings` defaults for those values, `settingsWarnings()` still offers `Enable auto-approval for daily locks`, and the approval-off note is now the only extra line in that card. (Superseded 2026-09-14: the separate `Unlock approval` card later merged into the single `Automatic locking` card, the `Period locking` card was deleted and the `More policy settings` block was removed - see the settings popup consolidation checkpoint above.) The locking-off note was corrected to say that existing locks keep protecting their period until an administrator unlocks them.
- The product-wide locking switch is now genuinely shared: `src/AccountingSettingsPanel.jsx` (`Period control`) gained a `Period locking` switch bound to `lockingMode` (`Off` <-> `Manual`) and its `Automatic period closing` switch was rebound from the legacy `autoLockPreviousMonth` flag to `lockingMode` (`Automatic` <-> `Manual`), with the automatic switch disabled while locking is off. Its `Closing frequency` and `Close after days` fields, which previously edited the unused `defaultFrequency` / `autoLockAfterDays`, now edit `schedule.frequency` / `schedule.lockAfterDays` (with `LOCK_FREQUENCIES` as the option list), the values the scheduler and the closing popup read. Both paths write through `normalisePeriodSettings` and `writePeriodSettings`. This fixes a real defect: the old toggle wrote `autoLockPreviousMonth`, which `normalisePeriodSettings` derives from `lockingMode` and therefore overwrote on save, so switching automatic locking on from Settings did nothing.
- Tests: `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` **17 passed, 0 failed** (the register contract now asserts the toggle leads the panel, the prose heading is gone, `mode={lockMode}` is passed, `lockRegisterRows` drives the rows, the manual empty state exists and the Settings-module switches write `lockingMode`), `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs` **14 passed, 0 failed** (new `the popup lead is a single line...` contract and the rewritten `unlock approval is one switch and drops every other unlock control`), and `tests/period-locking.test.mjs` **26 passed, 0 failed** (new `lockRegisterRows` mode contract and the stored-`lockingMode` round trip shared by Settings and Period Lock). Regression suites re-run green in the same session: `period-scope-visibility` 16/16, `period-unlock-scope` 9/9, `ui-quality` 5/5.
- Harnesses: `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` renders the page (`SSR OK - 9184 chars`, down from 9764 because the manual register starts empty and the `Period` select no longer lists hidden periods), `tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` reports `DRAWER SSR OK - 12 surfaces rendered`, `tmp/pc5-validate.mjs` reports `STRUCTURE OK - 15 surfaces well formed`, all 48 `src/*.jsx` files transform, and a purpose-built probe renders 2 rows (`May 2026`, `April 2026`) in automatic mode with a seeded future lock while `December 2026` appears nowhere in the page.
- Live preview: the running dev server at `http://localhost:5173/` serves `/src/PeriodClosing.jsx` with `lockRegisterRows` and `pc-register-heading` and without `Every lock for `, and `/src/period-closing.css` with `.pc-view-toggle`, all HTTP 200. The rendered register header is `<header><div class="pc-view-toggle" ...>Locked Periods<em>0</em>...Requests<em>0</em></div></header>` on a fresh store.
- Full-suite sweep in the same session: 61 test files, 57 runnable, 321 tests, 310 passed, 11 failed, 4 files blocked because esbuild cannot spawn a service process. The 11 failures are exactly the previously documented unrelated ones and were left untouched.

- The Period Lock heading now mirrors Journal Entries: `pc-title-block` is a column holding `pc-title-row` (the `Period Lock` title with the read-only `pc-policy-chip`, adjacency unchanged) and a new `pc-heading-note` subheading, "Lock accounting periods and control when postings stop. It is tinted 5% orange (`rgba(234,88,12,.05)` background, `#f0d3b8` border, `#9a3412` ink) and `.pc-attention.ok` switches to a 5% green tint when nothing needs attention. Clicking it still opens the `panel==='before'` `pc-before-drawer`.

The `All / Open / Closed` button group (`pc-status-toggle`) was removed with it.
- The toolbar (`pc-filters`) became a wrapping flex row holding a search box, a `Period` select (every period name in the current view), a `Status` select (lock states in the locks view, request states in the requests view, via `REQUEST_STATUS_FILTERS`) and one `Filters` disclosure (`pc-filters-more` > `pc-filters-panel`, badged with the active advanced-filter count) holding Financial Year, Organisation, Branch, Closed By and Closed Type (`CLOSED_TYPES`), plus `Clear filters`. `CLOSED_TYPES` derives Manual/Automatic from the stored lock reason prefix (`^Automatic schedule:`), so no engine or storage change was needed; `Closed By` and `Closed Type` are hidden in the requests half, where they do not apply. `LOCK_FILTERS` replaces the inline default filter object and `HISTORY_FILTERS` is now `{...LOCK_FILTERS,fy:'all'}`; `filterPeriods()` applies the new `period`, `closedBy` and `closedType` predicates and a new `filterRequests()` serves the requests half.

## Period locking enforcement checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The unlock lifecycle is now complete in `src/period-locking.js`. `unlockWindowFor()` resolves a requested scope against the lock: `Whole period` records the full range, and `Selected date range` requires both dates, requires start on or before end, and throws when the range is not completely inside the locked period. `requestPeriodUnlock()` stores `unlockScope` / `unlockStartDate` / `unlockEndDate` on the request and its `Unlock requested` audit entry, and `decideUnlockRequest()` copies the window onto the period.
- `validatePeriodAction()` allows only dates inside an approved window, so a date in the same period but outside the window stays blocked with `PERIOD_HARD_LOCKED` and a message naming the window; `blockingLocksFor()` applies the same window so overlapping locks stay independently enforceable. `LOCK_ACTIVE_STATUSES` now includes `Temporary Unlock`.
- New `cancelUnlockRequest(state, {requestId, user, reason, now})` is limited to the requester or an approver while the request is `Pending approval`; it restores the previous lock status, marks the request `Cancelled` with `cancelledBy`/`cancelledAt`/`decisionReason` and writes an `Unlock cancelled` audit entry. `relockExpiredPeriods()` now stamps the request `Expired` with `expiredAt` while keeping its history, clears the window fields, and writes two `System`-attributed audit entries, `Unlock expired` then `Period reclosed`, both carrying the request id. New `recordPolicyChange()` writes nothing when the policy is unchanged and otherwise audits a `Policy changed` event with `beforeValue`, `afterValue` and the `policyChangeGuard()` message.
- Enforcement now fails closed when the resolved lock state cannot be read: `assertOperationAllowed()` returns `allowed:false` with code `PERIOD_NOT_CONFIGURED` and an explanatory remedy instead of throwing inside a posting path.
- Every locking audit entry is written through one shared record builder, so each event carries `user`, `date`, `action`, `organisation`, `branch`, `beforeValue`, `afterValue`, `reason`, `periodId` and `requestId`. Organisation names resolve from the period, the request or the shared code map; a global policy change is attributed to `All organisations`.
- Period Lock exposes the lifecycle: the unlock drawer collects the requested scope with bounded start and end dates, the requests panel shows each request's scope, and a pending request raised by the current simulated role shows a `Cancel request` action.
- Focused tests passed: **9 passed, 0 failed** (`node tests/period-unlock-scope.test.mjs`, new) covering window containment, escaping ranges, whole-period and date-range requests, an approved window releasing only its own dates, the overlap rule, cancellation, expiry with both audit events, the audited policy change, the fail-closed `ENFORCED_OPERATIONS` sweep and the required audit record fields.
- Regression suites re-run in the same session: `period-locking` 24/24, `period-scope-visibility` 16/16 (one stale expiry assertion was updated to assert both audit events, matching its own test name and the specification), `period-lock-settings-ui` 13/13, `period-closing-ui` 11/11 and `journal-create-ui` 22/22. The sandbox SSR harnesses `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` (7382 chars) and `tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` (12 surfaces) pass, and the extended harness also asserts the new unlock scope control, the hidden date fields for a whole-period request and the bounded range inputs. All 48 `src/*.jsx` files still transform through the sandbox JSX pipeline.
- Full-suite sweep in the same session: 61 test files, 51 green. The 10 failing files are exactly the previously documented unrelated failures (`account-enterprise`, `account-master`, `create-page-navigation-ui`, `item-tax-ui`, `journal-business-view`, `journal-detail-ui`, `journal-heading-count-ui`, `operational-modules-ui`, `settings-ui`, `wayvida-date-picker-ui`) and were left untouched.
- Not verified: no rendered browser or human visual pass (in-app browser and localhost tooling stay blocked by the environment auto-review) and no production build, because `vite build` needs the esbuild binary this sandbox cannot spawn (`spawn EPERM`). The unlock scope inputs are the first fields in this module that need a real visual check.

## Journal lines organisation and branch column checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The Journal lines grid now renders the Organisation and Branch columns only while the working context needs them, using the same `getAccessibleOrganizations()` data the header working-context selector writes (`wayvida-context-companies` / `wayvida-context-branches`). One accessible organisation hides the Organisation column; one organisation with a single branch hides Branch as well, so that grid renders five columns; one organisation with two or more branches keeps only the Branch column (six columns); two or more organisations keep both (seven columns).
- The rules are `showOrganizationColumn = contextOrganizations.length > 1` and `showBranchColumn = showOrganizationColumn || contextOrganizations[0]?.branches?.length > 1`. Empty, null or storage-blocked contexts keep the previous `getAccessibleOrganizations()` fallback (the currently selected company and branch), which now means the single-organisation case rather than a seven-column grid.
- A new `alignLine()` helper plus a guarded `useEffect` keep every line consistent with the context: the organisation falls back to the first accessible organisation when it is no longer in scope, the branch is kept when it still belongs to that organisation and otherwise resets to that organisation's first branch. `lineBranchOptions(line)` now returns only `branchOptionsFor(line.organization)`, so the previous behaviour of prepending the line's current branch is gone and a branch from another organisation can no longer appear in the dropdown. `updateLineOrganization()` already reset the branch when the organisation changes and was left unchanged.
- When the Organisation column is hidden the Journal lines subtitle states the implicit choice in the existing sentence (`Posting to <organisation>`, plus ` · <branch>` when both columns are hidden). No second selector, no posting-context card and no new storage key was added.
- Alignment that depended on column position was replaced: `.je-create .je-line.head > .je-head-amount` and `.je-create .je-line > .je-cell-amount input` now carry the head and amount alignment, so hiding a column cannot shift the Debit and Credit alignment. The three grid variants are `no-organization`, `no-branch` and `no-organization.no-branch`, restricted to `min-width: 761px` so the existing single-column mobile card layout is untouched.
- No journal calculation, validation rule, posting rule, storage key or stored record field changed: `validate()`, `commit()`, `action()`, `postToLedger()`, `emptyLine()` and the `wayvida-manual-journals-v2` shape were not modified.
- Focused tests passed: **22 passed, 0 failed** (`node tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs`, two new contracts) and **6 passed, 0 failed** (`node tests/journal-line-columns.test.mjs`, a new file that extracts the real `readOrgList`, `getAccessibleOrganizations`, `showOrganizationColumn`, `showBranchColumn`, `branchNamesFor` and `alignLine` expressions from the page and runs the three configuration scenarios plus empty, unavailable and full-list fallbacks).
- Related journal suites re-run in the same session: `journal-templates` 8/8, `journal-detail-actions` 2/2, `journal-actions-ui` 2/2, `journal-entries-ui` 1/1. The same three known-stale suites still fail on their pre-existing assertions and were not touched: `journal-business-view`, `journal-detail-ui` and `journal-heading-count-ui`.
- Live preview verification: the running dev server at `http://localhost:5173/` serves `/src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` containing `showOrganizationColumn`, `showBranchColumn`, the `no-organization` / `no-branch` row classes, `je-cell-amount`, `je-head-amount` and `branchOptionsFor(line.organization)`, and `/src/journal-form.css` containing the five-, six- and seven-column variants with the class-based amount alignment; `GET /` returned 200. All 47 `src/*.jsx` files still parse.

## Journal posting gate and footer cleanup checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- Post journal on the Create Journal Entry page now depends only on the posted values: `canPost` is `difference === 0 && totals.debit > 0 && periodCheck.allowed`. The previous extra requirement that the stored journal already carry an approved status was removed; that requirement, not the amounts, is what kept the button disabled on a correctly balanced draft. `validate()` no longer rejects an unapproved record either.
- Posting is still refused for a locked or unconfigured accounting period through the existing `assertOpenPeriod()` call inside `commit()`, and the four `postingChecks()` conditions remain the tooltip detail behind the balance status. No calculation, validation amount check, storage key or record field changed, and posting still writes the same `wayvida-manual-journals-v2` record plus its ledger entry through `postToLedger()`.
- The balance status (`Debit equals credit`, or `Difference: <amount>`) now renders on its own line directly beneath the `Total Debit` and `Total Credit` amounts inside the same right-aligned block, so the posting condition reads as a caption to the figures above it instead of competing with them on one line. The block is a `flex-direction: column` container with a 2px gap, left-aligning with the rest of the footer on narrow viewports.
- The create-page footer now holds only the save action (`Save draft`, or `Save changes` while the record is pending or approved) and the primary `Post journal` button. The `Cancel` button, the `Approve this journal before posting.` hint paragraph and the three-dot More actions disclosure (Submit for approval, Duplicate Journal, Reverse Journal) were removed from this page, together with the now-unused `.je-post-hint` and `.je-more-actions` style rules. Duplicate and Reverse entry remain available on the journal register rows and on the journal detail screen.
- **Workflow change to flag:** because the create page no longer offers Submit for approval, the approval step moved to the journal detail screen, where a Draft, non-automatic journal now shows a `Submit for approval` secondary action that sets the record to Pending Approval and stamps `submittedBy`/`submittedAt`. The register row menu and the detail screen keep Approve, Reject and Post. A balanced journal can therefore now be posted straight from the create page without an intervening approval, which is the behaviour the user asked for on this screen.
- Focused tests passed: **20 passed, 0 failed** (`node tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs`) and **8 passed, 0 failed** (`node tests/journal-templates.test.mjs`). The updated contracts assert that the status follows the totals, that `canPost` carries no approval clause, that `Cancel`, the post hint and the More actions disclosure are absent from the create page, and that the sticky footer survives.

## Period Lock workflow redesign checkpoint — 2026-09-12

- Rebuilt `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` as a task-oriented workspace: four summary cards (Current Period, Closed Periods, Pending Unlock Requests, Last Closed Period) above Current Period, Period History, Unlock Requests and Settings tabs, with close, unlock, period-detail and audit drawers.

## Required accounting scenarios

- Balanced taxable invoice with intra-state CGST/SGST.
- Balanced taxable invoice with inter-state IGST and optional Cess.
- Round-off debit and credit cases.
- Missing/inactive/wrong-type account mapping blocks posting.
- Duplicate/idempotent post returns the existing result.
- Partial receipt, full receipt, customer advance, allocation and over-allocation rejection.
- Credit note within invoice/item/tax limits; excessive credit rejected.
- Cancellation/reversal produces equal opposite journal lines and preserves history.
- Branch/cost-centre-required account rejects missing dimensions.
- Closed-period commands block or require authorized exception according to lock type.
- Trial Balance debit equals credit after every successful posting/reversal sequence.

## UI acceptance checklist

- No clipped or overlapping text at supported widths.
- Minimum 12px text and Instrument Sans applied.
- Search, filters, menus, drawers, tabs, forms and primary actions work.

## Validation protocol

1. Run all Node tests.
2. Run the production build and Sites worker test.
3. Exercise the main flow in the in-app browser.
4. Inspect console errors and accessibility structure.
5. Capture desktop and narrow responsive screenshots for changed screens.
6. Compare against the accepted visual target and record unresolved issues.

## Evidence limits

Passing source/unit tests does not establish database atomicity, multi-user consistency, authentication, authorization, statutory compliance, cross-browser accessibility, performance, disaster recovery, or security. Those require a production backend and dedicated verification environments.

## Central audit checkpoint — 2026-09-07

`tests/audit-log.test.mjs` verifies record creation, ordinary updates, status transitions, removals, and configuration changes across the persistence-boundary classifier. Manual browser QA covers Reports navigation, search/filter controls, responsive table rendering, and CSV export. Historical events before installation are not backfilled.

Focused UI and audit contracts passed 8/8. Production build and representative live-screen inspection are required alongside these source contracts; screenshot review alone does not establish WCAG conformance or complete cross-browser coverage.

### Period Lock locked-periods register checkpoint — 2026-09-14

- Period Lock became a locked-periods register page. The tab row was deleted, so the register (`LockRegister`, `pc-locks-panel`) is the page body: a three-state `All / Open / Closed` toggle (`pc-status-toggle`), Financial Year defaulting to the current year through `CURRENT_FY` with `All financial years` still offered, Organisation, Branch and search filters plus a result count, `pc-history-table` on desktop and `pc-history-cards` on small screens, and columns Period, Organisation, Branch, Status, Closed Date, Closed By and Actions.

One lock applies to every module and a blank reason falls back to `Manual lock created for all modules`, so no scope selector, lock name or lock strength is rendered and the reason is no longer mandatory.

The engine keeps its three-state `lockingMode` and the two switches only map onto it.
